(v) A third non-get_option store closes the set: HARD_SETTINGS (config.rs), read by is_incoming_only/is_outgoing_only (the conn-type key, gating real behavior at client.rs/rendezvous_mediator.rs) and get_hard_option (exposed to Dart via main_get_hard_option) — and, more pointedly, a second at-rest credential store: get_preset_password_storage_and_salt reads HARD_SETTINGS's password/salt, and validate_password (connection.rs)/has_permanent_password (config.rs) fall through to that preset when the local store is empty. Like BUILTIN_SETTINGS, it is written only by the RustDesk-signed read_custom_client (common.rs, gated on sign::verify with the embedded key), so a fork build leaves it empty — but the fork MUST assert it empty at startup (R-A4) for the same reason as BUILTIN_SETTINGS, which removes the conn-type funnel and the preset-password credential vector by construction (and reconciles R-S9's single-h1 at-rest model, which this preset branch otherwise contradicts). (This is also why reusing RustDesk's existing OVERWRITE_SETTINGS precedence would be insufficient: it too sits only on the get_option path — the decisive requirement is intercepting every read funnel — Config::get_option, get_builtin_option, get_socks, permission()'s control_permissions, the LocalConfig namespace (R-SV10), and HARD_SETTINGS ((v)) — not just the get_option path.)
The credential storage format (the password-equivalent PRS the controlled side holds) is R-S9/R-P1's concern; (a)'s verification-method pin fixes only the policy that selects it. The single self.authorized = true edge (connection.rs) collapses onto the PAKE KEYED success (R-S2). R-A6 proves the whole mechanism complete — that the keys are pinned, that the funnels consult the pin, and that the resolvers reach config only through Config::get_option (so none is rewritten to a divergent constant).
R-S17RETIRED Host-key pin / known_hosts — retired; the fork is pure password-PAKE, with no per-box identity
Withdrawn. Earlier drafts added a mandatory viewer-side host-key pin (an SSH-style known_hosts over the box's self-generated Ed25519 key, delivered as a channel-bound HostIdentity possession-proof and compared on every reconnect). It is removed entirely — the ID is retired, not reassigned, and R-S18/R-S19 keep their numbers. Why it added nothing in scope: the threat model (§2) is unlimited active MITM, and a balanced PAKE over a secret password already defeats that by construction — an attacker who does not know the password cannot key, impersonate, relay-inject, or MITM, and a non-password-knowing substitute fails the PAKE closed (§2/§4.4). The pin did not add to MITM defense (CPace already provides it) and did not stop online guessing (the salt is public and the viewer reveals its CPace confirmation tag before any host-proof); its only job was catching a substitute box that already knows the password (password reuse across boxes, a leak, or prior compromise) — and password secrecy is assumed, so that threat is out of scope (§2). Defending only an out-of-scope threat, at the cost of a cross-platform GUI pin store, a first-contact/mismatch dialog ceremony, a headless --get-fingerprint bootstrap, and an extra signed frame in the handshake, was not worth its complexity. Consequences, folded into the live spec: the PRS salt is now a fixed domain-separation constant, no longer the host-key fingerprint (R-P1); "no long-term identities / no PKI" holds unconditionally, with no local-pin carve-out (R-P5); the password is the sole authenticator and sole substitution defense (R-S9/R-S18); and the host-proof KAT, the pin dialogs (ex-R-G5), the --get-fingerprint CLI, and the known_hosts store are gone (R-A6/R-A10/§19). No live requirement in this document mandates a host key, a fingerprint pin, or a host-proof.
R-S18MUST One authentication, zero second OS-credential paths — the same class on every platform, removed symmetrically from client and server (primarily server)
The audit that proves the thesis. All five session types funnel to the lone self.authorized = true (verified: a single assignment site, connection.rs), and the type — Remote/FileTransfer/ViewCamera/PortForward/Terminal — is resolved after it as a capability tag. So remote control, file transfer, clipboard/CLIPRDR, audio/voice, camera and port-forward are free: pure post-auth capabilities gated only by their enable-*/Permission::* flag, the same shared fs.rs/codec on every platform, carrying no credential of their own (verified — fs.rs has zero pam/logon/elevate). They are kept, and there is no security argument to touch them. Exactly one class violates "one authentication": a second OS credential — a peer-supplied OS username/password driving a real OS authentication distinct from the PAKE — appearing as the same finding in three platform skins, which the fork MUST remove from the tree (every build, structurally — not merely disabled by config): (i) the terminal OS-login (Windows real / Linux stub→root PTY) — prepare_terminal_login_for_authorization, should_use_terminal_os_login_scope, the peer-credential branch of terminal session-user selection, the FailureScope::TerminalOsLogin limiter scope, the LogonUserW/CreateProcessWithLogonW token path (R-X8); (ii) the os_login→PAM desktop-start (Linux) — linux_desktop_manager::try_start_x_session → pam::Client…authenticate() (R-X14); (iii) the peer-triggered elevation-with-logon (Windows) — ElevationRequest::Logon → StartPara::Logon(username,password) → CreateProcessWithLogonW (R-X9). R-X8/R-X9/R-X14 are the platform excisions of this one requirement, unified here. Symmetric removal — server primary, client too. The exposure is the server (controlled) side, which receives these from a network peer, so its receive/process code is the primary cut. But each is protobuf-mediated and the viewer sends them, and that sender is a real hazard, not dead weight: the viewer persists OS credentials per peer (os-username/os-password options) and auto-sends them on every connect as LoginRequest.os_login (client.rs — os_username/os_password = get_option("os-username"/"os-password")), plus a stable hardware fingerprint hwid when trust-this-device — so a malicious or substituted peer (a different box answering at the same address) harvests the operator's stored OS credentials automatically, a viewer-side credential leak the server-only audit cannot see (Appendix C #22). The fork MUST therefore delete the client senders too: the os_login: Some(OSLogin…) build (client.rs), the Data::ElevateDirect/Data::ElevateWithLogon → set_logon(ElevationRequestWithLogon) path (io_loop.rs), the set_switch_sides_response send (client.rs), the persisted os-username/os-password options and the hwid emission, and the viewer UI that drives them (the "Request Elevation" toolbar menu + showRequestElevationDialog + elevationModel, and the OS-account login dialog — §19/R-G4). Delete the protocol fields so neither side can speak a second credential (message.proto, hbb_common): LoginRequest.password (field 2 — the PAKE replaces the challenge-response, R-S2/R-S6), LoginRequest.os_login (field 12) + the OSLogin message, LoginRequest.hwid (field 14), ElevationRequest/ElevationRequestWithLogon, SwitchSidesRequest/SwitchSidesResponse, Auth2fa, and the Hash oracle — R-A6 greps each absent (one build). The bypasses fold into the one edge: the alternate paths that reach authorized without a credential — SwitchSidesResponse's 10-s UUID (which also sets from_switch to skip 2FA), the 30-s is_recent_session re-admit, and the "2FA bypassed by trusted devices" hwid path (connection.rs) — are deleted with the class (R-S2); the CM ipc::Data::Authorize operator-accept survives only as the attended GUI accept, downstream of the PAKE (the headless --service shows no CM window). No verification beyond the password: with the host-key pin retired (ex-R-S17), the mutual PAKE on the shared password is the only authentication and the sole gate — there is no second check, credentialed or otherwise, to bind into the channel or to relay around, so the compound-/tunneled-authentication class cannot arise. The standing invariant: access is the password alone, and nothing beyond the one PAKE-keyed channel confers or gates it (never a second credential, never a separate channel). After this, authorized == KEYED is literally true — one edge, no second credential anywhere in the tree or on the wire, on any platform.
R-S19MUST Capability confinement by AuthConnType — every peer-triggerable capability keys on the session type, never on a decoupled per-capability flag
The class R-S2 leaves open inside an authorized session. R-S2 collapses whether a peer is authorized onto the one CPace KEYED edge; this requirement pins what an authorized session may do. RustDesk seeds a set of per-capability booleans — keyboard, clipboard, audio, file, restart, recording, block_input, privacy_mode — from the local permission policy independent of the session's authorized connection type (AuthConnType::{Remote,FileTransfer,ViewCamera,Terminal,PortForward}, resolved from the LoginRequest oneof), and the post-auth handlers gate on those booleans (or the broad self.authorized state) rather than on the AuthConnType. Only keyboard is ever force-cleared, and only for terminal/view-camera — so a narrow session type reaches capabilities outside its granted scope. This is CWE-863 (incorrect authorization), the shape of CVE-2026-58056 (a FileTransfer authorization injecting input and capturing the screen — Appendix C #24) — and the same shape recurs at every flag-gated sink the message allowlist does not cover: host clipboard-text write, peer→host audio playback, block_input, privacy toggle, and remote-restart. Under the fork's pinned access-mode=full (R-S16) every permission resolves true, so the per-capability booleans provide ZERO session-type confinement — the AuthConnType is the only real boundary. The fork therefore MUST make every peer-triggerable capability/action a function of the authorized AuthConnType, not a decoupled boolean: (a) the post-auth dispatcher gates INPUT (mouse/key/pointer) to Remote only and desktop DISPLAY capture/control (screenshot, capture/switch-display, resolution, refresh, virtual-display, privacy, restart) to Remote-or-ViewCamera — an AuthConnType allowlist, load-bearing because screen capture has no capability flag to clear; (b) the capability booleans MUST be derived from the AuthConnType at authorization time — computed before any peer-supplied LoginRequest option is applied — so no handler can contradict the session type and no login-time option can transiently re-grant a cleared capability (an ordering the ad-hoc per-branch clears did not guarantee); and (c) the flag-gated sinks the message allowlist does not cover — host clipboard-text write (kept distinct from the FileTransfer file-clipboard, which legitimately needs clipboard), peer→host audio playback (gated Remote-or-active-voice-call), block_input, privacy toggle, and remote-restart — MUST additionally key on the AuthConnType. This holds on EVERY platform: the sinks and their cfg-gating differ per target (block_input is a live local-input DoS on Windows and a no-op stub on Linux; virtual-display/privacy are Windows-cfg; the input backends differ) — the confinement is correct only if it binds the capability to the AuthConnType on Windows, macOS, Android, iOS and Linux alike, not merely where a given sink happens to have effect. R-A6 MUST gate the derivation and the sink confinement so the class cannot regress. Principle: once the booleans are a pure function of AuthConnType, a handler physically cannot grant a capability the session type was not authorized for — the CWE-863 class is closed by construction, not patched sink-by-sink. (All instances are moot under §2 — the CPace password-holder is the trusted owner — so this is defense-in-depth / least-privilege coherence, not a §2-blocking exposure; it is nonetheless normative, because an "actually-secure" fork must not carry the upstream scattered-flag authorization model.)

8. Paths to EXCISE (block / remove)

The audit found several inherited paths that cannot be made safe on an internet-exposed host and add nothing to a serverless fork. These MUST be compiled out / removed so they cannot be re-enabled.

R-X1MUST Excise the auto-updater (fetch-and-run RCE)
updater.rs's check_update downloads a binary from a server-supplied URL (the version-check JSON's url field) with no signature or hash check and executes it privileged (--update / MSI). A second identical path exists in the Flutter UI (the flutter_ffi update-me/download-new-version keys). Whoever controls that response — or DNS/TLS for the version host, or a re-pointed fork — gets privileged code execution. MUST remove src/updater.rs (its mod in lib.rs, desktop-cfg-gated), the --update handler in core_main, and the Flutter update keys. The fork ships its own releases (§12); if update-checking is ever re-added it MUST verify a pinned SHA-256 of the artifact before execution (R-B2) — never fetch-and-run. macOS source twin: the sharpest form is the root DMG path — macos.rs's update_me/update_from_dmg run an osascript … with administrator privileges shell that strips com.apple.quarantine and re-installs as root, and mod updater compiles on macOS — so it MUST be excised on the Apple source too. The version-check egress and the universal cross-platform form are §18 (R-SV2/R-SV3).
R-X2MUST Excise the native-plugin loader
plugin/plugins.rs dlopens any plugin_*.{so,dll,dylib} from a system path and calls its C entrypoints — with the validation left as a literal // to-do; install_plugin_with_url unzips a download with no verification. That is arbitrary native code in-process. It is already double-feature-gated and not in the default build, and is not remotely installable (the PluginRequest message is post-auth and only invokes already-loaded plugins) — but it is dead weight on a hardened fork. MUST never compile plugin_framework, and MUST drop the src/plugin/ tree and the --plugin-install/--plugin-uninstall args so it cannot be re-enabled by accident.
R-X3MUST Excise the remote ConfigureUpdate server-list rewrite
rendezvous_mediator.rs lets the rendezvous server rewrite the client's rendezvous-servers list at runtime and restart — re-homing the client to arbitrary infrastructure. A serverless fork's server list MUST be fixed by local config; make the ConfigureUpdate arm a no-op. Note the twin — and that it is not the mediator's: a malicious rendezvous server can also rewrite rendezvous-servers from inside an unauthenticated TestNatResponse, but the cu field → set_option("rendezvous-servers") + set_serial handler lives inside test_nat_type_() (common.rs), reached by the startup test_nat_type() probe — not by RendezvousMediator. So excising the mediator (R-D4) does not remove it: on the viewer/Android/iOS builds the running app's startup probe still TCP-calls Config::get_rendezvous_server() (default rs-ny.rustdesk.com) and would adopt an attacker-supplied rewrite from the reply. It is removed only when the test_nat_type subtree is made cfg-absent and its startup callers deleted (R-SV4(d)). And a third twin: hbbs_http::sync::handle_config_options (sync.rs) writes arbitrary config keys — including the trust-anchor key, custom-rendezvous-server, api-server, even direct-access-port — from a /api/heartbeat strategy.config_options response, with no key allowlist (inert once mod sync is gated out per R-D4 and api-server is pinned empty per R-D6, but uncaught otherwise). R-A6 MUST grep all three re-home paths (ConfigureUpdate · TestNatResponse/common.rs · handle_config_options/sync.rs) so a partial fix — or a server-assisted rebuild that keeps hbbs_http::sync — cannot leave a twin live.
R-X4MUST Pin the trust anchor; remove the key/server overrides
The public key (trust anchor of the rendezvous-secured channel) and the server set are overridable via the "key" config option, a license/"config string" (which even accepts unsigned JSON — custom_server.rs), a renamed executable (or the RUSTDESK_APPNAME env var that spoofs the exe-name license without renaming, windows.rs), and the registry (common.rs, custom_server.rs, core_main.rs; on Windows the HKLM license read at windows.rs). A malicious installer name or shared config string silently points the client at attacker infrastructure with the attacker's key. The fork MUST bake its own values, make get_key() ignore the runtime override, and remove custom_server.rs + the --config path; it MUST NOT accept the unsigned-JSON branch. The same class includes three ungated CLI paths beyond --config: --import-config <path> overwrites the entire config (incl. trust anchor + servers) from an attacker-suppliable file with no is_root gate (core_main.rs); --remove <path> is an ungated file-delete gadget; and --assign/--deploy/--set-id presuppose a server account this fork removes and leak tokens/passwords on argv. All MUST be excised and CI-grepped (R-A6). macOS · iOS source: the trust-anchor override — get_key() reading the "key" option (the rendezvous anchor, distinct from the per-device get_key_pair()) and overriding RS_PUB_KEY — is live on both Apple platforms (the deep-link ?key= reaches it via mod client); custom_server, --config and --import-config exist on macOS but are absent on iOS (no core_main); the RUSTDESK_APPNAME/renamed-exe spoof is Windows-only, hence absent on Apple. So get_key() MUST ignore the override on macOS and iOS, and the macOS config-import paths MUST be excised on the Apple source.
R-X5MUST Excise LAN discovery; the recent-session re-admit is deleted with the FSM collapse
LAN discovery (lan.rs, UDP 21119) discloses MAC, RustDesk ID, hostname, active username, and platform to any LAN pinger. Per §8's "compiled out / removed" bar (not merely "disabled"), the fork MUST excise the lan.rs UDP listener — absent, not firewalled (cf. R-D4). On Android this is acute: start_lan_listening is hard-forced true (rendezvous_mediator.rs), so the excision MUST extend to the Android build (R-D7a), not Linux alone. The is_recent_session 30-s auto-re-admit (connection.rs) is not a separate weaker path: R-S2's collapse deletes it outright — authorization is the CPace KEYED edge, established fresh per connection, so there is no re-admit path to gate and the SESSION_TIMEOUT window (connection.rs) goes with it. Reconnect simply re-runs the sub-second handshake; there is never an unauthenticated re-admit.
R-X6MUST Excise the trust-anchor/password deep-link writes; confirm the connect deep-link
A completeness sweep of the platform/UI layer found the one cross-trust-boundary surface the Rust-core audits could not see: the rustdesk:// URL-scheme handler — a malicious web page, QR code, or co-installed app can fire it, pre-authentication. rustdesk://config/<b64> writes the rendezvous/relay/api server and the public key (trust anchor); rustdesk://password/<pw> sets the permanent password — both default-OFF behind compile-time flags but with no install+root gate (unlike the CLI), and both are the same trust-anchor / credential-injection class as R-X4. The fork MUST remove the config/server/key/password-writing deep-link authorities entirely. The third path, rustdesk://<id>?password=…&/r@<server>, silently auto-initiates an outbound session to an attacker-chosen peer/relay on all platforms; the fork MUST require explicit user confirmation for any deep-link-initiated connection and MUST NOT honor an embedded password/relay/key (incl. the ?key= trust-anchor injected into the outbound session) without it. The strip MUST be enforced in both layers: the Dart parser (flutter/lib/common.dart handleUriLink) and the Rust core, where the embedded ?key= is adopted into other_server (client.rs) and where the raw URI also reaches the core via bind.sendUrlScheme (the desktop_multi_window child-window path) — a Dart-only fix is bypassed, since the trust-anchor adoption lives in the Rust initialize (client.rs). (flutter/lib/common.dart; client.rs.) The Linux delivery transport is a D-Bus session-bus method (src/server/dbus.rs: bus org.rustdesk.rustdesk, method NewConnection(id)) that ignores the caller (no peer check) and forwards the URI verbatim into the same handler — any co-installed same-session app can invoke it, and it claims the bus name with replace_existing=true (a name-hijack that lets a malicious process intercept legitimate links). The fork MUST remove or caller-restrict NewConnection and drop replace_existing (CI-grep org.rustdesk.rustdesk · NewConnection · start_dbus_server). The Windows and macOS existing-instance delivery transports are a local-IPC boundary — desktop URL handoff MUST use authenticated _url IPC, not public window messages or caller-blind sockets: the listener authenticates the sender before delivering Data::UrlLink (same user/session plus current executable, with a restricted Windows named-pipe DACL). All transports still route through the explicit-confirmation gate above. On Android the deep-link is the manifest's rustdesk:// VIEW/BROWSABLE intent-filter on an exported MainActivity, and the manifest also ships an exported BootReceiver with a DEBUG_BOOT_COMPLETED broadcast any installed app can fire (AndroidManifest.xml: MainActivity, BootReceiver); the fork MUST remove the debug broadcast, set those components exported=false where possible, scope the intent-filter, set android:allowBackup="false" (else the UUID-obfuscated config holding the password-equivalent PRS is exfiltratable via adb backup) + a cleartext-deny network-security-config, and minimize the permission set to the dual viewer+controlled minimum (the app is one binary in both roles, R-R2, so it cannot be pared to "viewer-only"). Drop the legacy READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE/WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE (superseded by MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE on the pinned targetSdk 33) and requestLegacyExternalStorage. Keep (each maps to a preserved feature) INTERNET, ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE, FOREGROUND_SERVICE (+ the typed FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MEDIA_PROJECTION if targetSdk is bumped to ≥ 34), BIND_ACCESSIBILITY_SERVICE (controlled-side input injection), RECORD_AUDIO, MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, POST_NOTIFICATIONS, WAKE_LOCK, REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS and RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED (controlled auto-start). SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW is the one high-risk permission to drop, and this profile decides it: cut the FloatingWindowService and drop SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW — the controlled-status surface is already carried by the mandatory foreground-service notification (MainService.kt), and a TYPE_APPLICATION_OVERLAY window is an overlay/tapjacking primitive a single-purpose box does not need. The inherited floating window is moreover default-on (disable-floating-window is opt-out, == "Y", MainActivity.kt) with cosmetic size/transparency/untouchable/SVG sub-options — all removed with it. Cutting the permission MUST sever, not silently disable, the boot-auto-start dependency that currently requires it (BootReceiver.kt; canStartOnBoot). (If a status affordance is ever retained, it MUST be a single non-configurable minimal form — no runtime variability.) iOS source (the transport this list omits): iOS has no Rust IPC — the natively-registered rustdesk:// CFBundleURLSchemes (ios/Runner/Info.plist) flow OS → FlutterAppDelegate → the uni_links plugin (uriLinkStream/getInitialLink) — which natively supports iOS (its iOS impl is UniLinksPlugin.m; the Dart API carries no platform guard), with rustdesk's own initUniLinks/listenUniLinks (common.dart) routing only isLinux to D-Bus instead, so the iOS path is live — into the Dart handleUriLink, and the Rust ?key=→other_server adoption (in mod client's LoginConfigHandler::initialize) is reachable there too — so the strip MUST cover the iOS transport in both layers, and the iOS-only config/password deep-link write authorities MUST be removed, not left flag-gated. The iOS plugin has a second ingress this openURL framing misses: UniLinksPlugin.m also implements application:continueUserActivity: (NSUserActivityTypeBrowsingWeb — the Universal-Links/Handoff path), forwarding a matching https:// webpageURL into the same handleUriLink sink; the strip MUST remove that handler too, and the build MUST keep the associated-domains entitlement / NSUserActivityTypes absent (today's safety rests on that absence — R-A6's per-key entitlement sweep flags any future associated-domains addition). Desktop URL IPC detail: start_ipc_url_server is a separate listener that bypasses the main IPC handler; it MUST authenticate its sender before emitting on_url_scheme_received (peer uid + executable on macOS; named-pipe session/access checks + executable on Windows).
R-X7MUST Excise the one-time-password mechanism and the TOTP second factor; pin to the permanent password
RustDesk carries two credentials: the permanent password and an in-memory, rotating one-time password (TEMPORARY_PASSWORD, hbb_common/src/password_security.rs), and the default verification-method accepts either (UseBothPasswords; validate_password tries temporary first, then permanent, connection.rs). A balanced PAKE authenticates from one shared secret, so a second live credential is dead weight at best and a non-PAKE bypass at worst; the one-time password is also attended-only (screen-shown, rotating) and useless for the unattended access this fork targets. The fork MUST excise the temporary-password code (TEMPORARY_PASSWORD, get_auto_password, update_temporary_password, check_update_temporary_password) and hard-pin verification-method = use-permanent-password, making the permanent password the sole credential and sole PAKE PRS (R-P1, R-S9). (With R-S2's collapse, validate_password is itself deleted, so verification-method no longer feeds any auth decision — the hard-pin and its CI-grep persist purely to keep the OTP/temporary-password code absent, not because the resolver is still consulted.) The TOTP second factor is excised on the same logic (R-S2): Auth2fa (connection.rs), the require_2fa state, generate2fa/verify2fa/get_2fa (auth_2fa.rs), the trusted-device store (add_trusted_device, the RemoveTrustedDevices/ClearTrustedDevices IPC arms), and the totp-rs dependency (Cargo.toml) MUST be removed: TOTP is enrolled only through the 2FA UI ceremony this excises, and R-D6 pins the 2fa option empty, so it is un-instantiable dead surface, and a post-keying second factor would re-admit the multi-step pre-authorized state the R-S2 collapse eliminates. Dropping totp-rs also retires a puller of the idna 0.5 RUSTSEC-2024-0421 vuln (Appendix D, R-R3). This also deletes the consecutive-failure temporary-password rotation (connection.rs) — a remotely-triggerable lockout where 10 failed attempts rotate the password out from under a legitimate user, already gated on temporary_enabled(), so it vanishes with the excision. Enforcement MUST be fail-closed and immutable: verification_method()/approve_mode() today read a runtime-writable option and fail OPEN — an unset/unknown value yields UseBothPasswords/Both, re-enabling OTP and click-to-accept (password_security.rs) — and a DEFAULT pin can be shadowed by a written option (the get_or read-precedence at config.rs returns a set value over the DEFAULT; set_option writes) — which is precisely why the policy is pinned at the funnel, not in a DEFAULT tier. The fork pins verification-method and approve-mode in the controlled-side policy table (R-S16): the resolvers stay unmodified, and the Config::get_option funnel returns the policy while is_option_can_save rejects every runtime write — so the funnel pin wins over any written value, the failure these resolvers' DEFAULT-only pin could not prevent. R-S16/R-A6 own the enforcement and its CI proof.
R-X7aMUST Excise the Android approve-mode / verification-method selector UI — not merely fix it off
R-X7/R-S9 pin verification-method = use-permanent-password and approve-mode = password in the controlled-side policy table (R-S16), so the Config::get_option funnel returns the policy and is_option_can_save rejects any write to those keys (R-A6 AST-checks the resolvers reach config only through that funnel). But the Android server-settings UI still presents and writes the multiplicity — selectors for password / click / both and one-time / permanent / both, plus the one-time-password length and the numeric-OTP toggle, via bind.mainSetOption (flutter/lib/mobile/pages/server_page.dart; writers) — gated only by the soft isOptionFixed managed-config lock (config.rs), the exact "defaulted off, not removed" pattern R-S12 forbids. Once the R-S16 pin lands, the funnel overrides any written value and the write guard rejects it, so the UI is a second, divergent presentation of a setting it can no longer change. The fork MUST remove these selectors (leaving only Set permanent password), not disable them — the same toggle-and-surface removal R-X8 applies to the terminal. The same selectors ship on the desktop front-end — the Safety tab of desktop_setting_page.dart presents the identical verification-method (incl. the kUseBothPasswords case) and approve-mode choices through the same mainSetOption FFI — so this removal MUST generalize from Android to every front-end the fork ships (Flutter desktop + mobile); it is the first-named instance of the broader UI-conformance sweep §19 (R-G1/R-G4) carries.
R-X8MUST Pin the terminal ON — full control is the single mode (locking it off would be theater on the sovereign shared-password box)
Post-PAKE the Terminal session type spawns a shell as the service user — root on the exposed box (R-D3) — with no second credential on Linux: the OS-login/PAM re-auth (should_use_terminal_os_login_scope, connection.rs) is Windows-only by construction (cfg!(target_os = "windows")), and Linux select_terminal_launch_authority selects the process owner and yields a root PTY via spawn_command (terminal_service.rs). The reflex reading is that one PAKE password buys a direct root shell — more permissive than the bar. On this box that reading is void. §17 is a single-user sovereign machine and the CPace-authenticated peer is its owner, who holds the box's shared password — which here is also its OS/sudo/login password. §2 is explicit that the fork does not confine a password-knower ("confining it would mean defending against a password-knower, which we do not"), so denying that owner a terminal is security theater: with the keyboard on the desktop and the shared sudo password they already reach root in one step (xterm → sudo), exactly as they reach a tunnel (socat/ssh -R, R-D6). And the fork's own yardstick is SSH — a full interactive shell after a single authentication — so withholding the terminal would make this box strictly weaker than the bar it measures itself against. Full control is therefore the honest, correct, single mode. The fork MUST pin enable-terminal = Y in the controlled-side policy (R-S16) so the Self::permission(OPTION_ENABLE_TERMINAL, …) gate (connection.rs) resolves true through the Config::get_option funnel and the LoginRequest.Terminal arm is admitted. It stays defensive precisely because the value is a pin, not a flippable default: the funnel returns it and is_option_can_save rejects every runtime write (a password-knower cannot mutate policy mid-session in either direction), and the runtime SwitchPermission widener is removed (R-S16(d)(ii)) so the single mode is un-flippable — CI-proven per R-A6. What is not reversed — the second-credential path stays excised (R-X14/R-S18): the terminal's OS-login credential (os_login→token, the Windows LogonUserW terminal token, and the elevation-with-logon path) is deleted from the tree on every build — that removal is not theater but a real second-authentication surface — so an attended terminal runs as the service user (root on the exposed box), never via a peer-supplied OS credential, and granting the terminal re-introduces no PAM/LogonUserW step. The session-type code stays in the one binary (§14); an operator who wanted a different policy edits the pin and rebuilds (the R-F4 build-time-choice discipline), not a runtime knob.
R-X9MUST Excise the Windows run-mode, elevation, and service-disable plurality — one mode, one privilege transition
The Windows binary is a multi-tool selected by argv, executable name, install state, elevation state, and runtime config — the opposite of "one mode, the correct one." The controlled host needs exactly one: an installed, auto-start LocalSystem service (--service → start_os_service, windows.rs) that launches the session --server via the winlogon-token CreateProcessAsUserW — the single privilege transition — which launches the same-user --tray. Everything else MUST be compiled out, not gated:
  • Non-service run modes. The portable / un-installed in-process service (start_portable_service, StartPara, portable_service.rs; core_main.rs) and quick-support (set_quick_support, is_quick_support_exe, --quick_support, core_main.rs) are removed — they run the controlled side without the installed-service privilege model.
  • The stop-service runtime toggle. get_create_service honors a runtime-writable Config::get_option("stop-service") (windows.rs) that suppresses the service entirely — a local process (via --option/IPC) can disable the controlled host's service. This is the textbook R-S12 "security-relevant toggle, default-off-but-reachable": the branch MUST be excised and the key removed from the IPC-writable set (R-S11). The installed service is always present and always auto-start — the desktop daemon model (this Windows service; the Linux systemd --service, R-D1), whose direct listener is unconditional for the life of the unit; this is distinct from the Android controlled side, where the foreground service owns the listener and releases it on a graceful stop or process death (R-D7a). The excised stop-service key is dead on every target (the listener reads no such option), so removing it and its IPC-writable entry takes nothing from Android's foreground-service stop, which is an OS-lifecycle transition, not a config write.
  • Redundant & peer-reachable elevation. Three of the four SYSTEM routes are removed: the interactive UAC re-launch (run_uac/elevate, windows.rs); the SYSTEM token-theft git dependency impersonate_system (Cargo.toml) and its run_as_system wrapper (windows.rs); and — the sharp one — the post-PAKE peer-triggered elevation: Misc::ElevationRequest → handle_elevation_request → start_portable_service (connection.rs) lets a connected peer spawn a SYSTEM portable service. On the installed-service deployment it is dead-by-state (is_installed() true skips it), but the code path and the token-theft primitive remain compiled in — they MUST be structurally absent, not merely unreachable. The winlogon-token launch above is the sole sanctioned transition.
CI-greps these absent (R-A6). Persistence & installers (SHOULD): keep the auto-start service as the only persistence owner (it already re-spawns the tray, core_main.rs) and drop the independent all-users Startup-folder --tray shortcut (windows.rs, MSI RustDesk.wxs); converge the MSI (CreateServiceW) and EXE (sc create) on one service-creation path so they cannot drift. (The Sciter UI is dropped by R-B6; the world-creatable main pipe is tightened by R-S11; the trust-anchor / config sources are pinned by R-X4.) macOS source twin (the X9/X11-class obligation): the analog is a two-tier launchd model — a root LaunchDaemon running the service binary plus a per-user LaunchAgent running --server, the cross-uid step being launchctl asuser (the macOS equivalent of the winlogon-token launch) — installed by an osascript … with administrator privileges script. macOS additionally carries two in-process root-exec primitives: the osascript-admin elevate (re-launches the binary with arbitrary argv as root) and the AuthorizationExecuteWithPrivileges-based Elevate in macos.mm (linked even where dead-by-state). Both MUST be excised so launchctl asuser is the sole privilege transition — the source twin of R-X11's "carry no in-process sudo-driving primitive."
R-X10MUST Excise the Linux run-mode plurality — rustdesk --server (via the service or a supervisor) is the sole controlled entry
The Linux twin of R-X9. The controlled side runs as rustdesk --server — the capture/listener process — launched either by the installed auto-start systemd service (--service → start_os_service, core_main.rs, platform/linux.rs, which drops root→user via the single sanctioned sudo -u transition, run_as_user, linux.rs; R-D3a) or directly by a per-user process supervisor as the session user, capturing the supervisor-provided $DISPLAY/$XAUTHORITY with no logind seat required (the supervisord+Xvfb variant, R-D8). The --server entry is therefore the one sanctioned controlled entry both paths use — not compiled out. What MUST be compiled out, not gated, is the run-mode plurality around it: the --no-server flag (core_main.rs) — the Linux portable/quick-support twins — and the run-from-terminal "no-args starts the server" fallback (core_main.rs), so the only way to reach the controlled side is a deliberate --server (under a supervisor) or the --service that spawns it. The --cm/--cm-no-ui connection-manager UIs (core_main.rs) are cosmetic under approve-mode=password (R-S9) and MUST reduce to at most one non-configurable form. The canonical controlled config tree is whichever home the persistent --server reads (config.rs): under the root --service model it is the root service account's /root/.config/RustDesk — not the transient, seat-following user --server's ~/.config — whereas under a supervisor the --server is the persistent session user, so its own ~/.config/RustDesk is the tree. The operator configures it via --option (R-D2). (Sciter's second controlled UI is dropped by R-B6.) CI-greps the excised entries absent (R-A6).
R-X11MUST Excise the gtk_sudo interactive-elevation cluster — sudo -u is the sole privilege transition
Beside the sanctioned root→user drop, the Linux binary carries a second, interactive privilege route: gtk_sudo (src/platform/gtk_sudo.rs), a GTK/PTY front-end that drives sudo -S/su - <user> with injected-password elevation, entered via -gtk-sudo (core_main.rs) and reached from run_cmds_privileged (linux.rs), check_super_user_permission (linux.rs), and the UI/tray service install/uninstall that shells systemctl enable/start through it (linux.rs; ui_interface.rs, tray.rs). On the .deb-installed, always-auto-start deployment (R-D1) this is dead by state — systemd owns the unit and service lifecycle goes through Debian's package helper layer — but, exactly as R-X9 requires of the Windows token-theft primitive, the path MUST be structurally absent, not merely unreachable: a root-exposed service must carry no in-process sudo/su password-driving primitive. Drop gtk_sudo.rs, the -gtk-sudo arm, run_cmds_privileged, and the UI-driven install/uninstall callers; package lifecycle is the .deb's postinst/prerm through deb-systemd-helper/deb-systemd-invoke. CI-greps gtk_sudo · -gtk-sudo · run_cmds_privileged absent (R-A6).
R-X12MUST Pin the capture backend to X11; compile the Wayland/pipewire path and its selector out
The binary contains both capture backends, runtime-selected by session type — Display::primary() branches on is_x11() into Display::X11 vs Display::WAYLAND (libs/scrap/src/common/linux.rs) — and the root Cargo.toml hard-enables Wayland (scrap = { path = "libs/scrap", features = ["wayland"] }). §17 is Xorg; Wayland is N-A. To make "one capture mode" structural, the fork MUST drop features=["wayland"] so #[cfg(all(x11, feature="wayland"))] pub mod wayland (scrap/src/lib.rs) and the xdg-portal ScreenCast / restore-token persistence (wayland/pipewire.rs) are absent — which resolves R-S14's restore-token at-rest exposure by construction rather than mitigating it. The fork MUST also remove the RUSTDESK_FORCED_DISPLAY_SERVER env override (hbb_common/src/platform/linux.rs) — a runtime knob that re-selects the security-relevant capture/input path, the precise R-S12 "control removed, not defaulted off" — and make is_x11() a pinned constant true (asserted at startup, R-A4) — which also subsumes the one legitimate reason real deployments set this knob today: in a container or minimal session loginctl may exist with no seat, so the inherited get_display_server() falls back to a stray XDG_SESSION_TYPE and --server can mis-select the capture/input backend or refuse incoming sessions outright ("Unsupported display server type"); the compile-time x11 pin makes that determinism a property of the binary, so no operator ever needs the env override the fork removes. CI-greps the wayland feature, mod wayland (in scrap), and RUSTDESK_FORCED_DISPLAY_SERVER absent (R-A6). macOS source (assert, not excise): macOS already has one compile-time capture backend — Quartz/CGDisplayStream — with no runtime selector and no RUSTDESK_FORCED_DISPLAY_SERVER analog, so the obligation is to assert that sole backend, not excise an alternate. iOS: no capture backend exists (viewer-only). One macOS nuance: ScreenCaptureKit audio is a second consumer of the Screen-Recording TCC grant (R-S14).
R-X13MUST Pin input injection to XTEST; excise the dormant uinput service and the rdp_input path
Correcting a common misreading: on the Xorg box the controlled-side input backend is XTEST/xdo (the enigo default on X11, libs/enigo/src/linux/), not uinput — uinput is the Wayland path (wayland_use_uinput() = !is_x11() && is_server(), input_service.rs; gated !is_x11()), and rdp_input the Wayland-portal/flatpak path. Yet the root --service unconditionally stands up the uinput IPC listener (start_uinput_service(), linux.rs) with its world-mode 0o0666 _uinput_* sockets (Appendix B / R-S11a) that the X11 --server never connects to — dormant cross-uid surface. Once X11 is pinned (R-X12), wayland_use_uinput/wayland_use_rdp_input are constant-false; the fork MUST compile out src/server/uinput.rs, the start_uinput_service() call, the setup_uinput client path, and src/server/rdp_input.rs, so XTEST is the sole injection backend and the unused _uinput_* sockets are absent — shrinking the R-S11a cross-uid socket surface to _service alone on this build. CI proves them unreachable from the service entry (call-graph, not symbol-grep — the sanctioned XTEST/enigo injector legitimately stays; R-A6). macOS source (assert): the sole macOS injector is CGEvent (HID tap) — there is no uinput/rdp_input twin to excise, so this reduces to CI-proving CGEvent is the only injector reachable. iOS: enigo is an empty stub — no input backend (viewer-only).
R-X14MUST Excise the os_login → PAM desktop-session-start — a second, root-context credential path
The Linux controlled side carries an entire second authentication subsystem the PAKE does not subsume: a peer's LoginRequest.os_login{username,password} drives handle_login_request_without_validation (connection.rs) → try_start_desktop → linux_desktop_manager.rs's try_start_x_session, which performs real PAM authenticate()/open_session() on the attacker-supplied OS credentials to start an X session as an arbitrary OS account — and runs /etc/<app>/startwm.sh, which sources that user's ~/.bash_profile as root, with a predictable /tmp/.Xauthority (a sticky-dir cookie race). The module compiles unconditionally on Linux (platform/mod.rs) — it is not behind the terminal and is not the Windows-only/stub OS-login-scope path R-X8 disposes of (select_terminal_launch_authority is the terminal process-owner selector; linux_desktop_manager is the live desktop one). This is failure-mode-C, full stop — a root-context credential + exec path on an internet-exposed box, the same class §8 excises (updater R-X1, plugin R-X2, the terminal's peer-supplied OS-credential path R-X8/R-S18). It is gated on the allow-linux-headless option (default-OFF) — a documented headless-access toggle, not a path that fires by default (verified: LinuxHeadlessHandle::try_start_desktop returns early unless is_headless_allowed, connection.rs). Excision is correct anyway on two grounds: (i) the §17 box does not need it — greeter/seat0 capture (R-S14) draws its DISPLAY/XAUTHORITY from an independent discovery path (platform/linux.rs mod desktop: get_display_x11/get_xauth_x11), not from this PAM/session-start path (verified clean split — different file and module: mod desktop in platform/linux.rs vs the PAM path in linux_desktop_manager.rs, no shared mutable state — the only overlap is the read-only seat0/loginctl helpers (get_values_of_seat0/get_username/is_headless) that R-X14 keeps anyway); and (ii) when an operator does enable headless access, on upstream's plaintext direct path the os_login/desktop-start runs in the without_validation function ahead of validate_password (direct listener passes secure=false), so an unauthenticated network peer drives PAM authenticate() + a root startwm.sh: a remote, pre-auth root-context PAM oracle (rate-limited by the per-IP failure counter at connection.rs, so not an unthrottled brute-force, but still network-reachable and root-exec-capable). A runtime toggle that opens a root-context PAM+exec subsystem the box has no use for is exactly the R-S12 "no security-relevant toggle reachable" / one-mode case — and, as R-X8/R-S18 treat the terminal's peer-supplied OS credential, default-off is not sufficient. The shipped direct build MUST compile out only the session-spawn + PAM-auth in linux_desktop_manager.rs — try_start_x_session/start_x_session/start_x_session_thread/start_x11, the pam::Client authenticate()/open_session() calls, add_xauth_cookie, the /tmp/.Xauthority default and the startwm.sh exec — collapse try_start_desktop to existing-session-discovery-only and make LinuxHeadlessHandle::try_start_desktop return "" on Linux; MUST keep the capture-side discovery untouched (mod desktop in platform/linux.rs, and the loginctl helpers get_username/is_headless) so seat0/greeter capture (R-S14) still works; MUST pin allow-linux-headless off (a security-relevant toggle, R-S12); and MUST treat /etc/<app>/{startwm.sh,xorg.conf} + /etc/pam.d/<app> as root-trust files (reconciling the .deb-vs-Flutter xorg.conf install-path inconsistency). Scope: peer-supplied os_login is absent from the terminal authority path too: select_terminal_launch_authority derives only the process owner or the service-owned server process's Windows session user (R-X8/R-S18). The R-A6 desktop check is therefore pam::Client/authenticate/try_start_x_session/the os_login-desktop path unreachable from the connection accept path (call-graph, not symbol-absent). The session-discovery part is kept (collapsed to existing-session-only); R-S18 governs the credential itself: pam::Client authentication on peer-supplied OS creds is a second OS credential, deleted from the tree (an attended headless box pre-starts its own X server — the Xvfb model — rather than PAM-creating a session from a peer's credentials), so the PAM/session-spawn path is absent (cf. R-X8/R-D4). macOS source (negative finding — document, do not excise): macOS has no twin — linux_desktop_manager is Linux-only, the macOS select_terminal_launch_authority accepts no peer credential and selects the current process owner, and ElevationRequest::Logon is Windows-only — so the remote-pre-auth-root oracle is absent on macOS by construction; the R-A6 Apple-cfg check asserts pam::Client/try_start_x_session = zero there. (iOS has no controlled side at all.)

9. Secure-by-assertion requirements

Every property in §7–§8 MUST be guarded by a check the program, the build, or the test suite refuses to pass when the property is false.

9.1 Runtime (the program aborts rather than run insecure)

R-A1MUST No message is processed on an unkeyed stream
Before any protobuf message is dispatched — on the responder's accept path and, when the box views, the initiator's connect path (R-S13) — assert the secretbox is engaged and a confirmed PAKE produced its key; otherwise terminate (and panic in debug). Because authorization is the CPace KEYED edge (R-S2), this assert and authorized coincide: no message, and no authorized, before keying. Makes the unkeyed-direct-path bug unreachable in either direction (the inherited viewer is_secured() check at io_loop.rs is informational only — it MUST become this fail-closed assert on the initiator path).
R-A2MUST No AuthConnType resolves and authorized cannot set without a confirmed PAKE
authorized is set only on the CPace KEYED success edge (R-S2/R-P14a), and the AuthConnType is resolved from LoginRequest's session-type oneof after that edge. The inherited resume paths that set authorized without a fresh handshake — the 30-s is_recent_session reconnect and SwitchSidesResponse — are deleted (R-S2), not merely asserted: the CPace KEYED edge is upstream of every path that reaches the lone authorized=true assignment — its several callers of send_logon_response_and_keep_alive (the password path and the CM Data::Authorize arm) all run on an already-keyed stream, so no path sets authorized without a confirmed mutual key on this exact stream.
R-A3MUST set_raw() cannot strip a keyed session stream
The key-strip is removed; any residual must assert it operates on a stream carrying no session secrets, else abort.
R-A4MUST Startup self-check — including the live port surface
On listen, assert the genuinely-runtime invariants and refuse to listen otherwise: the only reachable stream constructor is the PAKE-secured one; the trust anchor equals the pinned value; verification-method == use-permanent-password / approve-mode == password read back (through Config::get_option) as the pinned policy values, and BUILTIN_SETTINGS and HARD_SETTINGS are empty so the get_builtin_option funnel and the HARD_SETTINGS/preset-password funnel carry no managed override or preset credential (R-S16(d)(iv)/(v)); the empty-permanent-password case is enforced at runtime rather than by this startup gate — while no permanent password is set the direct listener binds nothing (the R-S9 park) and every connection is refused per-connection (R-S9), so an unprovisioned box fails closed by presenting no listener; and the process's bound sockets match the audited surface — exactly one TCP listener on the pinned v4 port (21118, R-F4) once bound (and legitimately none while that park holds), and zero UDP listeners (enumerate /proc/self/net/{tcp,udp,udp6} after startup; a stray UDP listener means the LAN module or a probe leaked back in, R-D4/R-X5). Assert zero UDP sockets of any kind on the running --server process — not merely "no UDP listener." There is no carve-out for an ephemeral NAT-punch UDP socket, because the deletions make both roles TCP-only — the mediator (R-D4), KCP and udp_nat_connect (R-S13(d)), and LAN discovery (R-X5) are gone from the tree — so the --server process legitimately binds no UDP. (A box that is also viewing connects out over TCP; an outbound connect binds no UDP and is not a listener.) Enumerate /proc/self/net/{udp,udp6} and refuse to listen if any UDP socket exists — closing the blind spot whereby an ephemeral (OS-assigned-port, non-listening) UDP egress socket — a STUN probe (R-S11) or a dependency phoning home — would slip past a listener-only check (§4.1). (That the updater/plugin/mediator modules are absent (deleted), that the terminal is policy-pinned ON (full control — R-X8/R-S16) and mod client is unreachable from the --server entry (R-A6 call-graph) — and that egress is silent (R-D6) and the IPC write-allowlist and action-arm handling (R-S11) hold — are compile-time/CI facts proven in R-A6 plus, for egress, the operator firewall; they are not re-checkable runtime assertions. R-A4 is a bind/listener-surface check: an outbound TCP connect has no listener row, so TCP-egress silence rests on R-D6's compile-time removal plus the firewall, not on this assertion — acceptance criterion 8 MUST NOT over-credit R-A4 with catching egress. The two tiers are not interchangeable, and §9's "guarded by a check" is met at whichever tier — runtime, build, or test — each property names.) macOS · iOS source: this socket self-check reads /proc/self/net/*, which does not exist on Apple — so a macOS build MUST use a Darwin equivalent (proc_pidfdinfo / sysctl net.inet.*.pcblist) or record that the runtime check is unavailable and the surface rests on the §18 compile-out plus the build smoke-test (R-B4); iOS binds no inbound socket, so the check is moot there.
R-A5MUST Frame-length cap + no-follow writes asserted
Assert max_packet_length is set (not usize::MAX) on every connection and that writes use no-follow opens (R-S8) — all before any byte is accepted/written. Also assert the secretbox layer has two distinct per-direction keys engaged (R-P2/R-P10) — never one shared key both ways — and that a counter approaching wraparound forces a fresh-key rekey, not a counter reset. This is the one inherited catastrophic bug (same (key, nonce) both directions); the wire-capture test (R-A9) proves ciphertext but would not catch a regression to a single shared key, so key-distinctness MUST be asserted directly — and on the engaged cipher state, not the derived values: the assertion MUST read back the send-key and recv-key actually installed in the running Encrypt and confirm they differ. Comparing the two derived keys (k_c2s ≠ k_s2c, R-A10) is insufficient — they differ by HKDF construction even if a regression re-engages a single key for both directions (the precise way the catastrophic bug returns, and one R-A9 cannot see). The assertion MUST further confirm the role→direction binding (R-P2): the responder seals with k_s2c / opens with k_c2s, the initiator the mirror.

9.2 Build-time (insecure code cannot compile/link)

R-A6MUST CI proves the excised & deleted paths are absent
CI fails if the plaintext constructor, the hardcoded-false secure flag, a b"direct-ok"-style constant ack, the updater/plugin modules, custom_server, the ConfigureUpdate body, the temporary-password mechanism (TEMPORARY_PASSWORD/update_temporary_password), or any "trust unverified"/key-override config reappear (grep/AST guards). Deletion is verified, not assumed. The exact tokens CI greps (each must return zero hits in the shipped build): mod updater · "--update" · "download-new-version"/"update-me" (R-X1); plugin_framework · mod plugin · install_plugin_with_url · --plugin-install (R-X2); the ConfigureUpdate arm (R-X3); mod custom_server · "--config" · "--import-config" · get_custom_server_from_string (R-X4); TEMPORARY_PASSWORD · update_temporary_password · check_update_temporary_password · get_auto_password · use-temporary-password · add_trusted_device · Auth2fa · require_2fa · generate2fa/verify2fa · the totp-rs crate (R-X7); SignedId · set_signed_id · Union::PublicKey · box_:: in the connection handshake · the secure parameter (R-P5/R-P14); set_raw (R-S5 — absent, or assert-only); direct-ok and any constant byte-string ack (R-P3 — must stay absent); the lan.rs broadcast listener / RENDEZVOUS_PORT + 3 bind (R-X5); uri.authority == "config"/"password" and the DEBUG_BOOT_COMPLETED manifest action (R-X6). Plus the controlled-policy AST checks (R-S16): every key R-S16 names is present in PINNED_SETTINGS; Config::get_option (config.rs) consults PINNED_SETTINGS before get_or and is_option_can_save rejects every pinned key; the security resolvers (verification_method/approve_mode/is_permission_enabled_locally) reach config only through Config::get_option (never a direct CONFIG2.options/OVERWRITE_SETTINGS read that bypasses the pin, and — left unmodified — not rewritten to a hand-rolled constant either); no KEYS_BUILDIN_SETTINGS security key (one-way-file-transfer, allow-logon-screen-password, one-way-clipboard-redirection, enable-perm-change-in-accept-window) is read via get_builtin_option without a matching pin, and BUILTIN_SETTINGS is asserted empty (R-S16(d)(iv)); permission() takes its early is_permission_enabled_locally return and never consults control_permissions; and the SwitchPermission widener is absent. Plus a check that get_direct_port() returns the pinned 21118 literal (not the inherited RENDEZVOUS_PORT + 2 derivation) and never reads direct-access-port from config (R-F4); and that the direct listener in start_direct_only() starts unconditionally, not gated on a direct-server option (R-D2/R-D4). Add the terminal grant check — enable-terminal is pinned Y in PINNED_SETTINGS so the Self::permission(OPTION_ENABLE_TERMINAL, …) gate (connection.rs) resolves true through the funnel and admits the LoginRequest.Terminal arm, while is_option_can_save still rejects every runtime write and the SwitchPermission widener is absent so the single mode cannot be flipped at runtime (R-X8/R-S16); and the os_login/PAM tokens — pam::Client · authenticate · try_start_x_session · LoginRequest.os_login must be unreachable from the connection accept path (call-graph, R-X14); and a positive AST check that no Config::set*/save_config is reachable from a peer-message arm in io_loop.rs except through the viewer config-write allowlist (R-S15). Host-identity removal tokens (ex-R-S17, now R-P5): the retired host-key pin leaves zero hits for the HostIdentity frame/message, --get-fingerprint, build_host_identity/verify_host_identity, the rustdesk-fork/host-proof label, and any viewer-side known_hosts/pin store — the box presents no host-proof and the viewer pins and compares nothing (R-P5). Address-normalization KAT (R-SV5): the peer-address normalization (lowercase, IDNA to_ascii, default-port fill, IPv4 canonicalize) keeps its one frozen KAT so the re-keyed peer list feeding Client::_start (R-SV5) is deterministic. GUI-conformance tokens (§19, extending this grep into the Dart/asset/lang/ layers): the dead lang/ keys (connecting_status · not_ready_status · relay_hint_tip · the /r/@public clauses of id_input_tip · web_id_input_tip · websocket_tip · enable-2fa-title/enable-bot-tip · powered_by_me), the deleted assets (insecure.svg · secure_relay.svg · insecure_relay.svg · the unused auth-*.svg OIDC icons), the removed widgets/symbols (the numeric IDTextInputFormatter · buildPasswordBoard2's OTP path · the "ID/Relay Server" + changeSocks5Proxy editors · the verification-method/approve-mode selectors on every front-end · _forceAlwaysRelayAction/_wolAction · the update banner) and the rustdesk.com/github.com/rustdesk link literals MUST return zero — exactly as this gate already greps download-new-version/enable-2fa on the Rust side — plus the positive check that the connect box rejects a bare ID (R-SV10). One-authentication tokens (R-S18): zero hits for the OSLogin message · LoginRequest.os_login/.password/.hwid fields · ElevationRequest/ElevationRequestWithLogon · SwitchSidesRequest/SwitchSidesResponse · Hash (message.proto); the server paths pam::Client/authenticate · try_start_x_session · LogonUserW/CreateProcessWithLogonW · prepare_terminal_login_for_authorization · handle_elevation_request; and the client senders set_logon/Data::ElevateWithLogon (io_loop.rs), os_login: Some(OSLogin (client.rs), set_switch_sides_response, and the persisted os-username/os-password options — so no second OS credential survives on either side or on the wire. FSM-collapse tokens (R-S2): because authorization collapses onto the CPace KEYED edge and the legacy second authentication is deleted from the tree, these must return zero hits — validate_password · verify_h1 · is_recent_session · SwitchSidesResponse · the Hash{salt,challenge} emission (set_hash / the on_open hash send). The one surviving accept edge — the CM ipc::Data::Authorize arm (attended GUI accept) — is retained only downstream of the PAKE, so the positive AST check also holds: self.authorized=true has exactly one assignment site (connection.rs, inside send_logon_response_and_keep_alive) and every caller of it — the password path and the CM arm — is reachable only downstream of the CPace KEYED edge (none runs on an unkeyed stream). Two-key cipher tokens (R-P2/R-P10/R-A5): the symmetric single-key keying MUST NOT survive — grep that no single-key Encrypt::new(key) / single-arg symmetric set_key remains (the rewritten set_key carries a role/direction), plus the R-A5 check that the engaged send-key and recv-key differ (not merely the derived k_c2s/k_s2c values). Role-reachability checks (R-R2b — one binary): mod client, the VP8/VP9 video decode path (scrap's VpxDecoder; AV1/libaom is runtime-quarantined by Appendix C #87), the flutter UI, and the rustdesk:// deep-link receiver are present — the one binary is a full viewer — so absence is not a symbol grep here; CI MUST instead assert by call-graph/AST that they are unreachable from the --server/service entry (start_os_service/start_server), the build-time twin of R-A4's live socket-surface assertion. The hardware codecs (hwcodec/vram/ffmpeg HW) are compiled out on every build (CPU-only), so their absence is a sound symbol-grep; the software decoder stays linked (a viewer needs it) but the headless --server instantiates none, which R-A4 asserts on the live process. Where a mod client grep is used it MUST target crate::client::, not a bare client::, since four unrelated inner modules (keyboard::client · portable_service::client · uinput::client · rdp_input::client) would false-positive. (The genuinely deleted surface — mod kcp_stream, the mediator, the updater/plugin — is symbol-grep-absent, above.) And the egress tokens — post_conn_audit · post_alarm_audit · post_file_audit · record_upload · send_2fa_code_to_telegram/api.telegram.org must be unreachable from the accept path, or the api-server/bot/2fa options pinned empty in PINNED_SETTINGS (R-D6/R-S16). Critical caveat on the UDP/probe guards: the rendezvous probes (test_nat_type, test_rendezvous_server, stun_ipv4_test/stun_ipv6_test/test_bind_ipv6) and the UDP constructors (new_udp_for, FramedSocket::new, UdpSocket::bind) live in common.rs/socket_client.rs and are shared library code linked into the one build — so a tree-wide zero-hit grep on those symbols is wrong (they stay compiled for legitimate uses and a grep would pressure deleting needed code). The guard MUST instead be a call-graph / AST check that those symbols are unreachable from the controlled-service entry (start_os_service/start_server), not a symbol-absence grep. (Excising the mediator, R-D4, removes the start_all caller — but it is not the only service-path reach: the IPC handler also reaches test_rendezvous_server via Data::TestRendezvousServer (ipc.rs) and test_nat_type via the CheckTestNatType Drop guard (ipc.rs), independent of start_all — so the call-graph check MUST traverse Drop impls and IPC Data arms, and those reaches MUST be severed (R-S11/R-D4) or the check fails. The viewer-side callers — flutter_ffi, the cli feature, mod client — are present but unreachable from the --server entry (covered by the same call-graph check), so the residual the service entry can reach is exactly this IPC reach.) The check MUST run per target — including from the Android JNI startServer entry (R-D7), a third service-path root alongside start_os_service/start_server. Add the completeness tokens from the pre-auth sweep: the Linux deep-link D-Bus transport (org.rustdesk.rustdesk · NewConnection · start_dbus_server, R-X6); the TestNatResponse and hbbs_http::sync::handle_config_options (sync.rs) server-list-rewrite twins (common.rs, R-X3); and the IPC write-arms beyond the trio — with the legacy proxy/config write shapes asserted absent and any future handler write pinned by reachability/count gates — which R-A6 MUST enforce as the completeness check: a positive assertion that no Config::set*/set_socks/set_permanent_password/set_id/set_salt/set_unlock_pin is reachable from the IPC handle() dispatch (including via Drop impls) except through named receiver-authorized operations — so completeness across every write-arm is machine-checked, not left to per-arm review. Plus a positive AST check that the unkeyed-stream handshake decodes only the dedicated Cpace message (parse_from_bytes::<Cpace>) and that Message::parse_from_bytes is unreachable before set_key (R-P14/R-S7). Per-target Apple pass (R-R2 source-conformance): because iOS/macOS are not built but the source MUST conform, CI MUST also run these greps under cargo check --target {x86_64,aarch64}-apple-darwin and aarch64-apple-ios, adding the Apple-cfg tokens: the macOS DMG updater (update_me/update_from_dmg/extract_update_dmg, R-X1), the two root-exec primitives (elevate · AuthorizationExecuteWithPrivileges, R-X9/R-X11), the _url sender-auth (start_ipc_url_server, R-X6), the iOS deep-link write authorities and the ?key= adoption (R-X6), get_key() ignoring the "key" override on Apple (R-X4), and the §18 egress tokens (R-SV10); plus the negative assertion that pam::Client/try_start_x_session are absent on macOS (R-X14). And — because entitlements and Info.plist keys live only in the Xcode layer, invisible to cargo check/cfg — a full per-key allow-list across all four entitlements files: macos/Runner/Release.entitlements, macos/Runner/Profile.entitlements, macos/Runner/Debug.entitlements, and ios/Runner/Runner.entitlements. The Apple pass asserts every key is allow-listed or justified, and that com.apple.security.cs.allow-jit appears only in Debug because Flutter Profile/Release are AOT; plus — extending the same per-key allow-list to both Info.plist files (the sweep otherwise stops at the entitlements): the iOS UIFileSharingEnabled/UISupportsDocumentBrowser (both true upstream, exposing the Documents container), the privacy usage strings (NSCameraUsageDescription/NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription on iOS, NSMicrophoneUsageDescription on macOS — the capability-prompt twins of the swept device.audio-input entitlement), the CFBundleURLSchemes deep-link registration on both plists (R-X6, not iOS-only), and ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption (=false upstream — stale once the PAKE lands since the build is now cryptographic; remove or set correctly) — each off or justified. And two further Xcode-layer surfaces cargo cannot see: a per-pod allow-list over ios/Podfile.lock and macos/Podfile.lock — every pod justified against a preserved Flutter-plugin feature, CI failing on any addition (the present SDWebImage fetches remote images and MUST be justified or dropped; assert no Firebase/FCM/Crashlytics/Sparkle/analytics pod appears, R-SV8); and a grep over both Runner.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj that every PBXShellScriptBuildPhase is one of the four stock Flutter/CocoaPods phases, so a future fetch-and-phone-home run-script (the classic Crashlytics upload-symbols vector, invisible to cargo/plists/Podfile) fails the gate.

R-A6 helper-provenance companion: privileged or privilege-adjacent Rust cleanup paths MUST NOT invoke PATH-selected helpers when a native syscall/API exists. The Linux clipboard FUSE stale-unmount path is pinned to direct no-follow umount2, with CI rejecting Command::new("umount") and helper fallbacks.

R-A7MUST Pinned crypto; clean dependency audit; --locked
curve25519-dalek pinned ≥ 4.1.3 (RUSTSEC-2024-0344); the in-tree CPace implementation gated on the draft's published test vectors (plus a self-consistency KAT for the confirmation MAC, which has none); scripts/audit.sh runs cargo-audit and cargo-deny check advisories against the pinned RustSec snapshot with only documented deny.toml accepts; scripts/dart-audit.sh runs the Dart/Pub OSV gate against the pinned OSV snapshot; release builds use --locked; no alternate cipher suite or compatibility mode compiled in.

9.3 Test-time (adversarial proof)

R-A8MUST Injection is dead across all five types (all five reachable — the terminal is pinned ON by the policy, R-X8)
Per type, an active-attacker test proves post-auth injected frames are rejected (tag/sequence failure) and a first-contact MITM cannot complete key confirmation. Any landed injection fails the build. A separate test MUST prove the per-IP limiter (R-S10) is owner-safe: a flood from one source never blocks a different source, a tripped block decays (no permanent lockout), and a key-confirmation failure emits the structured record — the cardinal "a limiter must never lock the owner out of their own machine" property, otherwise untested.
R-A9MUST Wire-capture proves no plaintext — including the RDP tunnel
Capture a port-forwarded/RDP session and assert the bytes are indistinguishable from random; assert the login transcript yields nothing offline-crackable.
R-A10MUST PAKE known-answer & transcript-binding tests
The draft's published test vectors (which cover the protocol through ISK), plus a self-consistency KAT for the R-P3 confirmation MAC (both sides compute equal tags; any mutated input — including CI's role/port (§10.4) — flips the tag; no published vector exists for this step), plus negative tests that mutate each transcript element (ephemerals, sid, the version label / CI, AD) and assert the handshake aborts. Add a KAT that the two derived directional keys satisfy k_c2s ≠ k_s2c (R-P2/R-P10) — but this is necessary, not sufficient: add a two-peer end-to-end test driving an actual controlled↔viewer handshake and asserting each direction decrypts only under the mirrored key (responder seals k_s2c/opens k_c2s; initiator the mirror), which catches a role-binding inversion or a single-key regression that the derived-value KAT and the wire-capture test (R-A9) cannot (R-A5). Add a project KAT over the fork-specific CI byte-encoding (no draft vector covers it, R-P1). Add a second project KAT over a full 32-byte production sid (sid_a‖sid_b, R-P1/R-P14): the §10.4 anchor reuses the draft vector's 16-byte sid for reproducibility, but a live session always carries 32 bytes, so the 16-byte KAT never exercises the production sid length. The construction is parametric in sid length (length-prefixed in the generator and ISK; bare-concatenated under the fixed-width 64-byte ISK trailer in mac_key, which stays unambiguous), so a conformant implementation passes both — but the 32-byte KAT is what stops an implementer hard-coding a 16-byte sid buffer (§10.4 pins both anchors). Add a non-ASCII PRS KAT exercising the password normalization (R-P1): a combining-character password (e.g. "Café" with a decomposed e+U+0301) MUST normalize to its NFC form and yield the expected g/ISK, and an empty-after-normalization input MUST be rejected (R-S9) — the ASCII "Password" anchors never exercise normalization. (The fork's NFC-only normalization deliberately does not perform the OpaqueString non-ASCII-space map or FreeformClass rejection, R-P1; a non-ASCII-space password is therefore expected to differ from a full-OpaqueString peer — acceptable because both ends run the same build and any mismatch fails closed.) The negative AD test MUST confirm the abort is taken by the explicit ADa/ADb equality check (R-P5), not only by incidental transcript divergence. The suite MUST further include wire-choreography negative tests against the R-P14a state machine: a step delivered out of order (③ before ①), a duplicated step (two ① frames), a partial/dribbled frame that never completes (asserting the R-P14b timeout fires and the connection drops), a frame exceeding the ≤ 4 KiB pre-PAKE cap, and a replayed prior-connection transcript (asserting it aborts at the R-P3 tag, not via any sid cache). Each MUST assert fail-closed abort with no set_key, and that only the tag-mismatch case increments the R-S10 limiter — extending R-A8's owner-safe property to the pre-key path.

10. The PAKE specification

The handshake is small but unforgiving. Every step below is normative to the byte — each is a place real PAKE deployments have failed — and the construction follows the CPace draft and its published security proof, not intuition.

10.1 Primitive, suite & implementation

Primitive — balanced CPace

CPace, a balanced composable PAKE that authenticates from the shared password alone, with a published formal proof (Abdalla–Haase–Hesse 2021, adaptive UC). Suite: ristretto255 + SHA-512. This is a deliberate, documented deviation: the CFRG draft's recommended suites are CPACE-X25519-SHA512 (first) and the NIST-P curves; ristretto255 is a permitted "can also be implemented" option, chosen here because it is a prime-order group — eliminating by construction the cofactor / small-subgroup / twist pitfalls the draft enumerates for X25519.

Implementation reality — implement the draft; the crate is non-conformant

Settled empirically (and independently re-verified). A from-scratch draft-21 CPACE-RISTR255-SHA512 reference passes all of the CFRG draft's published ristretto255 test vectors (from github.com/cfrg/draft-irtf-cfrg-cpace): 18/18 protocol checks + 13/13 encoding checks, both abort cases included. Those published values are the derived CPace generator g = 222b6b19… (not the ristretto255 base point) and the symmetric-mode ISK ISK_SY = 544199d7… — the canonical ristretto255 vector, carried in testvectors.json under the group codename G_Coffee25519 and matching the draft body's worked example (the order-independent o_cat transcript reproduces it, R-P6). (testvectors.md lists the same vector — identical PRS/sid/scalars/AD — under a different example CI, giving a6fc82c3…/980dcc5a…; that is a channel-identifier difference, not a second vector, so do not conflate the two.) Computing jedisct1's pake-cpace on the same inputs diverges: the crate is on-the-wire non-conformant — it implements the 2019 draft-haase-cpace-01, unaudited, last released 2023. Its security-relevant deltas (no sid in the ISK, initiator-chosen sid, no key confirmation) live inside the hashed transcript and cannot be patched at the application layer — fixing them means forking lib.rs, i.e. rewriting draft-21 minus the vectors. There is no viable "use the crate" path.

Therefore the fork MUST implement draft-21 CPACE-RISTR255-SHA512 in-tree over curve25519-dalek — zero novel curve math (dalek supplies the audited, constant-time from_uniform_bytes map, scalar×point, decompress, is_identity; you write lv_cat/o_cat, the 64-byte generator string, the ISK transcript, and the R-P3 MACs). The construction core (encoding + generator/DH + ISK/MAC) is ~150–270 lines; counting everything §10 makes mandatory and security-relevant — the R-P14a handshake state machine, the two-key secretbox rewrite (R-P2/R-P10, which ripples through tcp.rs/websocket.rs/stream.rs), bounded reads (R-P14b), the dedicated Cpace protobuf, and the dual-role integration — the security-relevant diff is ~450–850 lines, with a comparably-sized KAT/negative-test harness on top. CI MUST gate on the draft's published test vectors — they catch the real failure modes (encoding off-by-ones, the zero-pad length prefix, 32-vs-64 hash length, transcript ordering, the aborts). Pin curve25519-dalek ≥ 4.1.3 (RUSTSEC-2024-0344). This adds no new external crate: every primitive the handshake needs is already in the 1.4.7 lock — curve25519-dalek 4.1.3, sha2 0.10.8, hkdf 0.12.4, hmac 0.12.1, subtle 2.6.1, zeroize 1.8.1, rand 0.8.5 (CSPRNG; getrandom 0.2.15 also present), and unicode-normalization (NFC, present via idna) for the PRS — which holds only because the PRS normalization is NFC-only, a deliberate subset of RFC 8265 OpaqueString (R-P1); a fully-conformant OpaqueString would need a new precis-profiles crate, which this fork does not add, and the inherited session layer is sodiumoxide 0.2.7 (libsodium: secretbox + the Ed25519 device key to be removed per R-P5 — there is no ed25519-dalek) — so the PAKE adds no new dependency, over crates already compiled in (exact lock versions in §3.2). The §11 review is vector-checkable for the construction core (encoding, generator derivation, ISK), but its scope is not bounded by that small line count: the R-P14a state machine, the two-key secretbox rewrite, the constant-time/identity guarantees, and the R-P3 confirmation-MAC composition (which has no published vector — its self-consistency KAT cannot catch a self-consistently-wrong composition) require review by someone fluent in protocol/nonce-misuse analysis. The independent expert audit R-V3 mandates is therefore necessary, not merely tractable-because-small.

No registry, no stored keys — the most sovereign anchor there is

Because a balanced PAKE authenticates from the shared password, this fork uses no long-term public keys, no signatures, no certificates, and no key registry of any kind. TLS anchors trust in CA registries; SSH anchors it in known_hosts (trust-on-first-use — a local registry). The PAKE anchors it in the password you already share — nothing to store, distribute, pin, or look up. One credential, one primitive, one path; no second PAKE to select, negotiate, or fall back to. (The per-connection ephemeral group elements on the wire are key-exchange values, not identities, and are kept nowhere.)

10.2 The handshake

R-P1MUST Inputs & channel binding
PRS = base64(Argon2id(pwd, salt)) — a memory-hard salted hash, not the plaintext. pwd = the NFC-normalized, no-case-fold UTF-8 of the permanent password (the sole credential, since the one-time-password path is excised — R-X7); salt = a fixed domain-separation constant, SHA256("rustdesk-cpace-prs-salt-v1")[..16] — the same 16 bytes compiled into every build, bound to no per-box value. It is computed with libsodium crypto_pwhash (Argon2id) at the INTERACTIVE ops/mem limits — phone-safe, since Android/iOS viewers derive it too — with a 32-byte output, base64-encoded to a fixed-length ASCII string; that string is the PRS the §10.4 construction consumes as its input (the §10.4 published-vector anchors pin that construction over the draft's fixed literal PRS for reproducibility, R-A10, while production feeds this Argon2id value). Both ends derive the same PRS with no salt exchange — the fixed constant salt is compiled into both roles, so the box (at --password time) and the viewer (at connect time, from the typed password) reach an identical PRS from the password alone, with nothing per-box to distribute, pin, or agree; an empty PRS still yields no usable credential (fail-closed, R-S9). At-rest honesty (fixed salt): the salt is not a secret and never was — the old host-key-derived salt was equally public (the box hands its Ed25519 key to every viewer that connects) and deterministic — so at-rest confidentiality never rested on it and is unchanged: the stored PRS is still the memory-hard Argon2id hash, never the plaintext. The one property a fixed global salt drops relative to a per-box one is cross-box precomputation-separation (a single Argon2id dictionary now applies to every fork box under a given password), but that bears only on offline guessing across many endpoints, which §2 places out of scope (endpoint at-rest read and password secrecy are assumed); the in-scope posture (no plaintext, memory-hard) does not change, and this is not a strengthening. The NFC normalization (now the Argon2id input) is a deliberate NFC-only subset of the RFC 8265 OpaqueString profile: it applies NFC (the only normalizer in the lock — unicode-normalization via idna) but not OpaqueString's non-ASCII-space→U+0020 map or FreeformClass disallowed-codepoint rejection — chosen so the PAKE adds no new crate (§10.1), and safe because both peers run the same fork build (R-R2b), so any normalization divergence fails closed at the R-P3 tag (wrong PRS → divergent generator → divergent ISK → abort) rather than weakening; a balanced PAKE compares by exact PRS equality, so there is no collision surface. The password MUST be non-empty after normalization (R-S9) — CPace has no empty-PRS guard — and a non-ASCII KAT exercises this (R-A10). Because CPace is balanced, the controlled side must hold this PRS — a connect-equivalent value, but now the Argon2id salted hash rather than the plaintext password — to run the handshake; protect it as R-S9 requires.
