The channel identifier CI MUST carry the protocol/version label and any per-connection channel binding, and MUST be identical and pre-agreed on both sides (CI is never sent on the wire). The session id sid MUST be a unique per-connection value agreed by both parties — e.g. a fresh 16-byte random nonce contributed by each side, concatenated initiator-then-responder (a 32-byte sid). That contribution exchange rides the still-unkeyed wire pre-PAKE, so its integrity rests solely on the R-P3 confirmation abort: sid MUST enter the ISK transcript (draft-21 symmetric — the ISK_SY vector), not merely the generator, so a tampered nonce yields a divergent ISK/mac_key and aborts. CI MUST be a fixed both-sides-identical string (fork tag · suite · version · port binding) — the per-side role instead lives in AD (ADa=b"viewer" / ADb=b"server"), since the draft requires both sides to hold the same CI; no per-connection value that would need a tamperable exchange. The port folded into CI MUST be the pinned compile-time direct-access port (21118, R-F4) — the same constant compiled into both roles, never a runtime option and never a value read from the live socket (NAT/port-forwarding would otherwise desync CI between peers and silently abort the handshake) — and its byte-encoding MUST be pinned (§10.4); since no published draft vector exercises a fork-specific CI, the build MUST add a project KAT over CI and the derived generator (R-A10). No identity keys (R-P5).
R-P2MUST Exchange & key derivation
Each side sends its CPace message (ephemeral group element); both derive the Intermediate Session Key ISK. The raw DH output K MUST NOT be exposed, logged, or used directly (a leaked K re-enables offline attack). From ISK, derive two directional secretbox keys (controlled→viewer and viewer→controlled) by HKDF-SHA-512 with distinct info labels; derive the confirmation key separately by the draft's own construction (mac_key = H("CPaceMac" ‖ sid ‖ ISK), R-P3). All three are domain-separated by construction — MUST NOT slice one hash for several keys, nor reuse one key across both directions (R-P10). Role→direction binding (load-bearing): both peers derive the same k_c2s and k_s2c, so correctness depends on each installing them in mirrored slots — the controlled side (responder) seals with k_s2c and opens with k_c2s; the viewer (initiator) the mirror. The inherited symmetric set_key(key) (one key both directions) MUST be replaced by a keying call that carries the role/direction, and the symmetric form MUST NOT survive (R-A6); a mis-binding that engages one key both ways re-creates the catastrophic reuse and is not caught by the derived-value k_c2s ≠ k_s2c KAT — only by R-A5's engaged-key assertion and R-A10's two-peer test.
R-P3MUST Explicit mutual key confirmation (the proven construction, not a transcript MAC)
After ISK: mac_key = H("CPaceMac" ‖ sid ‖ ISK); each party sends MAC(mac_key, lv_cat(its-own element, its-AD)); each MUST verify the peer's tag in constant time and abort before authorizing. The MAC MUST be HMAC-SHA-512 (the draft offers HMAC or CMAC; HMAC-SHA-512 matches the suite hash and carries its own RFC 4231 test vectors). This is the CPace draft's own Key confirmation construction, verbatim — the draft defines a single key-confirmation MAC — the parallel/no-message-ordering property of our symmetric mode (R-P6) lives in the order-independent o_cat ISK transcript that feeds mac_key, not in a distinct MAC variant — so this is the draft's construction, not an ad-hoc transcript MAC. The reply MUST NOT be a constant — it is a function of ISK by construction (the fix for the prior fork's b"direct-ok"). Do not MAC "the whole transcript and both identities" — there are no identities, and that diverges from the construction the proof covers. Explicit confirmation also upgrades CPace's forward secrecy from weak to perfect. In the symmetric mode these tags are sender-bound only once R-P5's role/AD discipline is applied (otherwise a tag is reflectable in a loopback) — R-P3 MUST NOT be implemented without R-P5.
R-P4MUST One handshake at the choke point, before the type is parsed
Run once where the single stream is created (server.rs), keying all six transports and all five types before any application message.
R-P5MUST No PKI/registry; defend the loopback
No trust anchor beyond the shared password — no CA, certificate, registry, rendezvous-issued identity signature, and (with the host-key pin retired, ex-R-S17) no long-term per-box identity key and no local pin of any kind: the fork holds no cryptographic identity, authentication is the password via CPace, and nothing about a per-box key is persisted, pinned, or proven (R-P1/§10). The existing SignedId/PublicKey/Ed25519 session-key bootstrap in the connection handshake is removed (§8) — but that is the device-identity keypair only: the rendezvous trust anchor (RS_PUB_KEY, R-X4) stays only as a baked constant. A server-assisted build would have needed the register_pk/key_confirmed registration-liveness gate (rendezvous_mediator.rs) preserved or the box goes unreachable — but §18 (R-SV4) compiles the mediator out of every shipped artifact, so there is no server-assisted build to preserve it for: register_pk is absent (R-SV10 greps it out), not preserved, and this carve-out is now dead for everything the fork ships (the direct-only deployment already sidestepped it). Consequence of having no identities: CPace will key with anyone presenting the same PRS+sid — including the host looping back to itself. The fork MUST add a loopback/relay defense: assign fixed initiator vs responder roles per (PRS,sid) and forbid one host playing both, or enforce unique ADa/ADb and reject a received AD that matches a locally-active session. Resolved (§10.4): pin fixed roles — the controlled side is always responder, the viewer always initiator — and carry the role as the per-side associated data ADa = b"viewer" (initiator) / ADb = b"server" (responder), with only the shared suite/version/port in CI (the draft requires both sides to hold the same CI, so the role cannot live there). Because AD is per-side, sent, and folded into both the transcript and the R-P3 tag, each tag is sender-bound, so a host cannot reflect one role's messages as the other for one (PRS,sid) — reflection fails the role/AD-equality check. (The residual self-loopback — a host genuinely driving both state machines against itself — still completes, but is bounded by the deliberate-connect posture (a box run purely as --server never initiates), not by the AD check.) The AD check MUST be explicit: the responder MUST verify the received ADa == b"viewer" and the initiator MUST verify ADb == b"server", aborting on mismatch — folding the received AD into the transcript is necessary but not sufficient; the literal equality check is what makes each confirmation tag sender-bound.

10.3 Construction details (each is a place real PAKEs have failed)

R-P6MUST Symmetric-mode ordering
Both ends are peers, so message order is undefined ⇒ the symmetric setting: combine with the draft's lexicographic o_cat(lv_cat(Ya,ADa), lv_cat(Yb,ADb)), OR impose fixed initiator/responder roles (which also satisfies R-P5). Never a fixed Ya‖Yb order without roles. Note: the fork pins fixed roles and uses the symmetric o_cat transcript — a deliberate superset of the draft's initiator-responder transcript_ir (safe: o_cat binds both elements + ADs). The ISK_SY vector is therefore the gating KAT; an implementer MUST NOT substitute transcript_ir (it fails the vector and breaks interoperability with a conformant fork peer).
R-P7MUST Abort on invalid / identity point
If the shared point is the group identity, or a received element fails ristretto255 decode(), abort — never continue with a degenerate key. The trap: the all-zeros encoding decodes successfully to the identity, so decode-success alone is insufficient — the K.is_identity() test after the multiply is what catches a malicious identity input (§10.4).
R-P8MUST sid uniqueness; no secret reuse
sid unique per connection (e.g. random_a ‖ random_b); ephemeral secret scalars and sid MUST NOT be reused.
R-P9MUST Pinned domain separation
Pin the exact DSI strings for the chosen draft version and a distinct HKDF label per key; a DSI mismatch MUST make keys not agree, never silently cross-confuse.
R-P10MUST secretbox nonce discipline
The 24-byte XSalsa20-Poly1305 nonce MUST be unique per key and never reused — including across directions. The inherited layer derives the nonce from a per-direction sequence counter alone (tcp.rs get_nonce = LE64(seq), no direction tag) and installs one key with both counters at 0 (set_key Encrypt(key,0,0)) — so a single shared key makes each side's frame n seal a different plaintext under the same (key, nonce): catastrophic XSalsa20-Poly1305 reuse. Per-direction keys (R-P2) close it — the invariant is key-uniqueness, not nonce-uniqueness, so identical per-direction counters from 0 are safe and one MUST NOT "optimize" back to a single key; also specify a derived-counter generation and an abort/rekey policy before counter wraparound. A rekey MUST install fresh per-direction keys — never reset the counters under the existing keys, which would re-create the very (key, nonce) reuse this requirement closes. (The u64 counters make wraparound unreachable in practice — millennia at any real frame rate — so the rekey is defensive belt-and-suspenders against a reset bug, not the load-bearing closure; the closure is key-distinctness, which is why R-A5 asserts the engaged send/recv keys differ, not merely that the derived values do. The pre-increment makes the first nonce LE64(1), not 0 — immaterial to the argument.) Ripple — not a one-file change: the two-key fix reshapes the public Encrypt tuple (tcp.rs) and changes the set_key signature to carry the role/direction (R-P2) across Stream::set_key's three variants (stream.rs) and the WsFramedStream that reuses tcp::Encrypt (websocket.rs). The peer↔peer relay session is keyed at the same choke point (server.rs) so R-P14 covers it; the rendezvous-signaling secure_tcp path (common.rs) is removed by R-D4 (the fork is direct-only, so no rendezvous-signaling path is left to touch).
R-P11MUST No downgrade, no suite negotiation
Hard-pin one suite, one draft version, one DSI set; carry the version label in CI so any mismatch yields non-agreeing keys. No negotiation handshake exists to downgrade. The pinned draft is draft-irtf-cfrg-cpace-21 — the current CFRG revision as of 2026-06 (latest revision 2026-04-22, in IESG review; not yet an RFC), and its published ristretto255 vectors are exactly the ones §10.4 reproduces — which MUST be taken from the draft-irtf-cfrg-cpace-21 tag (or vendored), not the live CFRG master, whose later example-CI change produces different vector values (R-A10). If CPace later reaches RFC, adopt it only as a deliberate, re-vectored change — never an automatic version bump.
R-P12MUST K-secrecy & constant-time
K MUST NOT be exposed or logged (only ISK leaves the PAKE layer). Generator computation, scalar sampling, scalar multiplication, and every tag comparison MUST be constant-time (curve25519-dalek provides this for the curve ops — assert it). Scalar sampling MUST use Scalar::from_bytes_mod_order_wide / Scalar::random, never the deprecated Edwards-only Scalar::from_bits (§10.4); and the tag comparison is the one CT op not inside dalek — use a constant-time comparator (subtle / Mac::verify), never ==. Constant-timeness MUST be machine-checked — a dudect-style statistical timing test in CI over the sampling and the tag compare, plus the type-level guarantees of subtle/curve25519-dalek — so it is verified, not reviewed by eye — removing the timing axis from the review burden (but not the state-machine, nonce-misuse, or MAC-composition axes, which still need expert review — §11/R-V3). Secret-wiping is manual, not automatic: curve25519-dalek 4.1.3's Scalar/RistrettoPoint implement the Zeroize trait only — no Drop/ZeroizeOnDrop — so the zeroize crate's presence is not coverage: the CPace code MUST explicitly .zeroize() the ephemeral scalars (y_a/y_b), the DH point K, and the PRS-derived material, and R-V3's audit MUST confirm it.
R-P13MUST Quantum-annoying, not PQ — record it
CPace is "quantum-annoying" (a quantum attacker solves one CDH per password guess), not post-quantum. The port folds into CI and the role into the transcript-bound AD (§10.4 keeps role out of CI — both sides must hold the same CI); per the draft it is the fresh per-connection sid that forces one CDH per guess, with AD contributing via the transcript; the fork MUST document that long-term confidentiality rests on password entropy — a recorded transcript is offline-recoverable by a future quantum attacker against a weak password. A PQ-hybrid is out of scope for a single-user box unless that threat is.

Estimated change size: the CPace construction core is ~150–270 lines, but the full security-relevant diff — the handshake state machine, the two-key secretbox rewrite, the §10.3 invariants, the §8 excisions, and removing the SignedId/Ed25519 exchange — is ~450–850 lines (§10.1), with a comparably-sized test harness. The code diff is small; the work around it is not — every line in §10.3 is a place a real PAKE deployment has failed, and the verification (§11), independent audit (R-V3), and per-platform build pipeline (§12) dwarf the diff. That asymmetry is the point, not a reassurance.

R-P14MUST On-wire choreography: where the handshake runs and the messages it replaces
The CPace exchange runs inside create_tcp_connection (server.rs) on the unkeyed Stream, inserted unconditionally where the secure-gated SignedId ↔ PublicKey key bootstrap runs — and that bootstrap is wrapped in if secure && … (server.rs) while the direct listener passes secure=false, so on the flagship direct path nothing ran there before: the direct path gains keying it never had, rather than swapping one block for another (on the secure=true rendezvous/relay paths it does replace the old flow — controlled sends SignedId; peer replies PublicKey{symmetric_value,asymmetric_value}; set_key(Encrypt::decode(…)))). That block (server.rs), its client counterpart, the box_ seal/open path (tcp.rs), and the SignedId/PublicKey protobuf messages are removed (R-P5, §8). The new flow is pinned:
  • Message — a dedicated top-level type, not a Message-union variant. Add Cpace as its own top-level protobuf message in libs/hbb_common/protos/message.proto (a oneof over the steps below), framed through the existing bytes_codec but parsed and serialized on its own on the unkeyed stream — parse_from_bytes::<Cpace>, never Message::parse_from_bytes. Routing it through the application Message union (the obvious shortcut) would make the pre-auth decoder reach the entire Message schema — every VideoFrame/file/clipboard field an attacker could populate — whereas the dedicated type keeps the reachable pre-key decoder surface to the handshake's ~4 fixed fields (R-S7). Both roles change: the responder parses only Cpace pre-key, and the initiator (when a box views) sends/parses only Cpace pre-key — which also protects a connecting viewer from a malicious server's malformed pre-key frames. Both sides switch to the normal Message framing only after keying. No other message type is accepted, or even decoded, on the unkeyed stream.
  • Fixed roles & sequence (viewer = initiator, controlled = responder). The generator g needs the full sid = sid_a‖sid_b and CI, so the sid contributions precede the group elements: ① initiator → responder { sid_a(16B), ADa=b"viewer" }; ② responder → initiator { sid_b(16B), ADb=b"server", Yb(32B) }; ③ initiator → responder { Ya(32B), Ta }; ④ responder → initiator { Tb }. Ta/Tb are the R-P3 HMAC-SHA-512 tags (64 B). Two round-trips.
  • Gate. Each side verifies the peer's tag in constant time, derives the two directional keys (R-P2), and calls set_key only on success; no set_key, no Connection::start, no application message, no authorized before both tags verify (R-A1/R-A2). Any decode/length/timeout/tag failure aborts fail-closed.
  • Framing. set_max_packet_length is capped small before the first byte (R-S7); each CPace message is ≤ ~120 B. With KCP dropped (R-S7/R-D4), this dedicated, fixed-shape Cpace message (parsed on its own, never via the Message union — R-P14) is the only attacker-reachable parser before keying.
This choreography is in scope for the §11 adversarial review (R-V1).
R-P14aMUST Strict step-ordering and no-duplicate handshake state machine
§10.2 gives the message sequence; this pins the state machine that enforces it. The handshake MUST be driven by an explicit four-state machine per role (responder: WAIT_1 → INIT2 → WAIT_3 → SEND_4 → KEYED; initiator: INIT → WAIT_2 → SEND_3 → WAIT_4 → KEYED). In each receiving state the implementation MUST accept exactly one Cpace oneof variant and MUST abort fail-closed on any other — a future-step variant, an own-side variant, or a repeat of an already-consumed step (no peer may send two step-① frames). A step arriving before its prerequisites (e.g. step-③ before step-① at the responder, which then holds no sid_b/g/yb) MUST be a wrong-variant abort, never buffered; no state may silently ignore an unexpected frame and keep waiting (that re-opens the half-open / duplicate surface). Each step is consumed exactly once, and set_key is reachable only from the tag-verify success edge (R-P14, R-A1).
R-P14bMUST Bounded reads — the timeout is the half-open resource bound
set_max_packet_length(≤ 4 KiB) MUST be set before the first read (R-S7), and every inbound handshake step MUST be read under a finite timeout (per-step, or one whole-handshake deadline). Verified: the framing codec returns Ok(None) on a partial/dribbled frame (bytes_codec.rs) — it never completes and never errors — so the timeout is the sole bound on a peer that opens a connection and stalls (the inherited code wrapped only its single next(): server.rs / client.rs, CONNECT_TIMEOUT/READ_TIMEOUT = 18 s; four steps need four bounded waits, or one deadline). A timeout, a peer EOF (Stream::next() → None, tcp.rs), an oversize frame (bytes_codec.rs "Too big packet"), or a parse_from_bytes::<Cpace> decode error MUST abort fail-closed. The timeout (stall bound), the ≤ 4 KiB cap (size bound, R-S7), and the per-source limiter (CPU-flood shedding, R-S10) together bound half-open exhaustion to ≤ 4 KiB + one task per connection until the deadline; no per-connection state beyond the in-flight handshake is created.
R-P14cMUST Only key-confirmation failure feeds the limiter; replay needs no state; nothing on the wire to downgrade
Of all pre-key aborts, only an R-P3 key-confirmation tag mismatch MUST increment the per-source limiter (R-S10) — it is the sole event that is an online password guess; decode / length / out-of-order / duplicate / decompress / identity / AD-mismatch / timeout / EOF aborts MUST NOT increment it, or a malformed-frame flood would trip the owner's own block (R-S10's cardinal principle; the limiter is checked before the scalar-mult). Whole-handshake replay is harmless and MUST NOT be defended by sid-tracking state: each side contributes a fresh 16-byte sid nonce and a fresh ephemeral scalar per connection (R-P8), so a replayed sid_a/Ya cannot yield a tag valid under the responder's fresh sid_b/Yb (the mac_key/ISK diverge) and aborts at R-P3. sid freshness is enforced unilaterally and locally by CSPRNG sampling; a "seen-sid" cache is forbidden — unnecessary, and itself a memory-DoS / stateful-config surface (R-S12). The Cpace oneof carries no version / suite / capability field — those live only in the never-sent CI (R-P1, R-P11) — so there is no on-wire field to downgrade, and an unknown/unset oneof case is a decode abort, not a fallback.

10.4 Concrete construction — the byte-level pins (implementable without a cryptographer)

§10.1–§10.3 match draft-21 and reproduce the published ristretto255 vectors, but a faithful, interoperable implementation needs the literal bytes fixed here. CI MUST gate on the draft's published ristretto255 vector — the G_Coffee25519 group in testvectors.json (the JSON's codename for ristretto255), which pins every intermediate (derived generator g = 222b6b19…, symmetric-mode ISK_SY = 544199d7…) and agrees with the draft body's worked example — pinned to the draft-irtf-cfrg-cpace-21 git tag or vendored into the repo so a later CFRG revision (or the eventual RFC) cannot silently shift the expected bytes out from under the KAT. Do not substitute the testvectors.md ristretto255 entry: it is the same vector under a different example CI and so yields different bytes (a6fc82c3…/980dcc5a…). Add the fork's own CI/anchor KAT (the pinned-bytes box below) and the R-P3 self-consistency/negative KAT (R-A10).

ConstantValue
Suite / ISK / MAC DSIb"CPaceRistretto255" · b"CPaceRistretto255_ISK" · b"CPaceMac"
Hash · block · gen-hash lenSHA-512 · s_in_bytes = 128 · generator hash = 64 bytes (not 32)
Groupristretto255 (prime order ℓ = 2252 + 27742317777372353535851937790883648493); 32-byte element encoding
Length prefixlv_cat uses LEB128, and it prefixes the zero-pad field too
Session keysHKDF-SHA-512, IKM = ISK (64 B), salt = the fixed non-secret b"rustdesk-fork/CPACE-RISTR255-SHA512/v1/hkdf", info = b"rustdesk-fork/CPace/secretbox/c2s" & …/s2c, 32 B each
CI (shared, identical both sides, never sent)lv_cat(b"rustdesk-fork/CPACE-RISTR255-SHA512/v1", port) — fork-tag · suite · version · port only. No role here: the draft requires both parties to hold the same CI (draft §"Protocol inputs"), so a per-side role cannot live in it. Pin the port byte-encoding to one form — fixed 2-byte big-endian (network order) (it must be byte-identical on both peers, so a single pinned encoding is required, not a choice) and feed the pinned compile-time direct-access port (21118, R-F4) — not the live socket; add a fork KAT (no draft vector covers CI). For the §17 direct port 21118 the pinned value is CI = 26727573746465736b2d666f726b2f43504143452d52495354523235352d5348413531322f763102527e — the 38-byte tag, then 02 527e (LEB128(2)‖be16(21118)).
ADa / ADb (per-side, sent in clear, authenticated)the fixed-role tags — ADa = b"viewer" (initiator), ADb = b"server" (responder = controlled side). Per-side and folded into both the transcript and the R-P3 tag, so each confirmation tag is sender-bound and a loopback/reflection fails (R-P5). The published KAT vectors use ADa=b"ADa"/ADb=b"ADb"; the fork's runtime values are "viewer"/"server".
# PRS = NFC(password), no case-fold (NFC-only subset of RFC 8265 OpaqueString, R-P1) — fed directly
gen_str = generator_string(DSI, PRS, CI, sid, 128)        # PRS & DSI lv-prefixed inside; zpad fills the block
g       = RistrettoPoint::from_uniform_bytes(SHA512(gen_str))   # 64-byte hash → ristretto map (dalek)
ya      = Scalar::from_bytes_mod_order_wide(&csprng_64())  # == Scalar::random; NEVER Scalar::from_bits
Ya      = (ya * g).compress()                             # 32 bytes, on the wire
P       = CompressedRistretto(Yb).decompress()?           # abort on decode failure …
K       = ya * P;  if K.is_identity() { abort }           # … AND on identity (all-zeros DECODES ok!)
ISK     = SHA512( lv_cat(DSI‖b"_ISK", sid, K) ‖ transcript_oc(Ya,ADa,Yb,ADb) )
                                                          # transcript appended RAW (not re-lv-wrapped) → ISK_SY; fork KAT below = 0421d692…
k_c2s   = HKDF-SHA512(salt, ISK, ".../c2s", 32)           # per-direction secretbox keys (R-P2)
k_s2c   = HKDF-SHA512(salt, ISK, ".../s2c", 32)
mac_key = SHA512( b"CPaceMac" ‖ sid ‖ ISK )               # direct hash, bare concat — NOT lv_cat, NOT HKDF
Ta      = HMAC-SHA512(mac_key, lv_cat(Ya, ADa))           # verify peer's tag in CONSTANT TIME
Three traps a vector-blind implementer hits

(1) Scalar sampling: use Scalar::from_bytes_mod_order_wide (what Scalar::random calls) — a 64-byte wide reduction with no bit-masking. MUST NOT use the deprecated, feature-gated Scalar::from_bits: it masks only the top bit (&0x7F, leaving the scalar unreduced) and is Edwards-only — it will not work with RistrettoPoint::mul. (The draft's alternative clamping sampler clears the top 4 bits with &0x0F; the wide-reduction path needs no clamp — so &0x0F is not the code path here.) (2) ISK layout: the transcript is appended raw after lv_cat(DSI_ISK, sid, K); re-wrapping it as a fourth lv_cat element silently yields a different ISK (on the published-vector inputs, 49ddbd… instead of the correct 544199d7…) and fails the vector. (3) Identity: the all-zeros encoding decodes successfully to the identity, so a decode-only check is insufficient — you MUST test K.is_identity() after the multiply (R-P7).

The fork KAT — anchor A: 16-byte sid (draft-reproducible), port 21118

The §11 review and CI gate against these. KAT-fixed inputs (chosen so the anchor is reproducible from the published draft vector): PRS = OpaqueString("Password"), sid = 7e4b4791d6a8ef019b936c79fb7f2c57 — the draft vector's 16-byte sid, reused so this anchor reproduces from the published vector; a live session always uses a 32-byte sid = sid_a‖sid_b (R-P1), pinned as anchor B below and gated by R-A10 — ya/yb = the draft-21 published scalars, ADa = b"viewer", ADb = b"server", port 21118. In production PRS is the permanent password and sid/scalars are fresh per connection — these bytes pin the construction, not a live session. The port is a pinned compile-time constant (21118, R-F4), so CI — and therefore g and everything below — is fixed: these are the values, unconditionally. (Rebuilding with a different port constant is a build-time change that regenerates them; there is no runtime port mode.)

CI      = 26727573746465736b2d666f726b2f43504143452d52495354523235352d5348413531322f763102527e

The fork KAT — anchor B: 32-byte production sid (R-A10)

The production handshake carries a 32-byte sid = sid_a‖sid_b (R-P1/R-P14), which anchor A's 16-byte draft sid never exercises; this anchor pins the 32-byte path and CI MUST gate on it too. KAT-fixed inputs: PRS = OpaqueString("Password"), sid = a1·16 ‖ b2·16 (example halves sid_a/sid_b), ya/yb = the same draft-21 scalars, ADa = b"viewer", ADb = b"server", port 21118. Independently re-derived byte-for-byte from the draft-21 construction — the generator and ISK length-prefix sid, and mac_key = H("CPaceMac"‖sid‖ISK) stays unambiguous under the fixed 64-byte ISK trailer at either sid length (§10.3, R-A10).

11. Cryptographic verification requirements

Honest caveat — stated every time

The in-tree CPace implementation (§10.1) has had an independent expert review (2026-07-02, findings resolved — docs/CRYPTO-AUDIT-2026-07-02.md): the construction was reproduced byte-for-byte against the published CFRG vector by an independent implementation, and the parts no vector covers — the R-P14a state machine, the two-key secretbox rewrite, the constant-time/identity paths, and the R-P3 confirmation-MAC composition (whose self-consistency KAT cannot catch a self-consistently-wrong composition) — were analysed for protocol/nonce-misuse. That review was, however, AI-conducted and single-reviewer; it does not equal the decades-deep, multi-party third-party scrutiny SSH's channel crypto has. Maturity is therefore the single axis on which SSH still leads on the wire — the off-wire content-parsing residual of the viewer role is the other (§1 / Appendix C #2b), present whenever a box views and absent when it acts purely as --server.

R-V1MUST Adversarial multi-lens review
The handshake design + implementation undergo structured review attacking from independent angles (first-contact MITM, replay, downgrade, timing, nonce/transcript reuse; the single-authentication shape — one password-keyed channel with no second verification to bind or relay around, which forecloses the compound-/tunneled-authentication class (PEAP-MSCHAPv2 / Triple-Handshake) by construction; the five-type coverage, the set_raw path, curve25519-dalek usage). Findings resolved or risk-accepted in writing.
R-V2MUST Spec conformance & implementation audit
Checked against the chosen CPace draft and its published test vectors (group, encoding, generator derivation, ISK derivation, KDF labels — the published vectors stop at ISK; no vector covers the R-P3 key-confirmation MAC, so that step is checked by a self-consistency + negative KAT instead, R-A10). The in-tree implementation and its curve25519-dalek dependency are read for correct usage and rev-pinned; the implementation's un-audited status is disclosed in the release notes.
R-V3MUST Assurance basis: full byte-level spec + published vectors + adversarial tests
The PAKE is a standardized construction (draft-21) specified here to the byte (§10.4), gated on the draft's published vectors and the R-P3 self-consistency/negative KAT (R-A10), and exercised by the active-attacker / wire-capture tests (R-A8/A9). That — not an external sign-off — is the day-to-day CI assurance basis: a competent engineer can implement and verify the vector-covered construction core (encoding, generator derivation, ISK) against the cited vectors and code without a cryptographer. But that does not extend to the whole of §10: the R-P14a state machine, the two-key secretbox rewrite (R-P2/R-P10), the constant-time/identity guarantees, and the R-P3 confirmation-MAC composition (no published vector) require expert review regardless of their small line count. Because this code guards an internet-exposed, root-capable service and replaces the two most serious inherited findings (Appendix C #1–#2), an independent expert audit of the §10.4 construction, the state machine, the constant-time paths, and the KDF/secretbox parameters MUST be obtained before exposed operation and published — necessary, not merely tractable-because-small. The vectors + KATs + adversarial tests above remain the day-to-day CI assurance basis (and what makes the audit checkable); until the audit is obtained, the §11 "not independently audited" disclosure stands and the box MUST be treated as pre-audit. Status (2026-07-02): an independent expert review has been obtained and published (docs/CRYPTO-AUDIT-2026-07-02.md, findings resolved) — satisfying this requirement's substance (an AI-conducted, single-reviewer review; scope and boundaries in the report). What remains forbidden is the inverse: shipping a "clean bill of health" the tests do not actually back — every security claim here is reproducible against the vectors and the file+symbol citations.

12. Build, release & version pinning

R-B1MUST Build & publish the full client matrix
From the one forked codebase, the build MUST produce one package per platform — Android aarch64 (.apk), Windows x86_64 (.exe + .msi), and Debian x86_64 (.deb) — each a single binary usable as viewer and, via --server, controlled side (R-R2b); the internet-exposed box (§17) runs that same binary as a headless --service. It MUST run to completion off GitHub-hosted runners (locally or a self-hosted runner; R-B2); macOS/iOS are dropped (R-R2).
R-B2MUST No code-signing; the build cannot live on GitHub-hosted CI
Upstream's release model signs artifacts with secrets injected into GitHub Actions — unusable here on two counts. (1) GitHub-hosted runners are too unreliable to be the build path: the heavy RustDesk matrix (vcpkg built from source, multi-GB toolchains, long Windows/Android jobs) fails on the order of one run in five, so you cannot depend on getting a green build out of it — flutter-build.yml MUST be runnable to completion off GitHub-hosted runners (locally or a self-hosted runner; R-B1). (2) Artifacts ship unsigned: code-signing is a CA/registry dependency (the model R-P5 already rejects), it adds nothing once the binary's hash is verified directly, and with the build off GitHub there are no CI secrets to hold a signing key anyway. Exception — Android: the OS refuses to install an APK with no signature, so it uses a self-generated local key (apksigner/keytool — no CA, no Play Store), a forced local keypair, not CA code-signing — Android refuses to install an unsigned APK (the v2 scheme is mandatory since Android 11), so this signature is not optional. Pin it: one stable RSA-4096 keystore (SHA256withRSA; validity ≥ 10000 days; fixed alias), generated once and reused for every release — Android ties app identity to the signing key, so a stable key gives clean in-place upgrades whereas a changed/absent key forces uninstall+reinstall (wiping app data). The keystore is a secret: kept out of the repo and the build image, fed to build-android.sh only at sign time (R-B11) — mounted read-only, never baked into an image layer, with its password supplied via file/agent, not env or argv (both leak via /proc and container inspect). Because one stable key signs every release for clean in-place upgrades, its theft lets an attacker ship malicious upgrades and rotation forces a data-wiping reinstall, so the fork MUST document a key-compromise / break-glass procedure and hold the keystore offline (ideally HSM-backed) — the residual is real and currently unaddressed. The signature is Android's install gate, not the trust anchor — the pinned SHA-256 still is. Integrity from build to install is a pinned SHA-256 per artifact, verified on the target over the operator's existing trusted out-of-band channel — the same pinned-SHA check used for any third-party .deb — over the reproducible build from the committed lockfiles (R-R1). Tamper-evidence without a signature. One determinism prerequisite the recorded-SHA bar depends on: upstream bakes a wall-clock, local-timezone build timestamp into every binary (hbb_common::gen_version emits BUILD_DATE via chrono::Local::now(), hbb_common/src/lib.rs, also writing into the source tree at ./src/version.rs), so two builds of identical source on the identical pinned image otherwise yield different bytes and a different SHA-256 — turning the operator's rebuild-and-compare into a guaranteed mismatch and making a malicious build host indistinguishable from a benign rebuild. The fork MUST neutralize it — honor SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH (patch gen_version to read it, or hard-pin BUILD_DATE to the release commit's date) — and CI MUST assert it by a double build: build the same source twice and require byte-identical SHA-256. Without that assertion the recorded-SHA bar is unfalsifiable.
R-B3MUST Publish AGPLv3 source per binary
Each release links the exact source revision (both repos + submodule commit) it was built from.
R-B4MUST Smoke-test every platform; assume nothing builds until watched
Per platform, connect + authenticate + drive each session type must pass before tagging. (Upstream's fork pipeline emits nothing until upload-artifact is wired and the build is watched to green — real work, the more so on the flaky runners R-B2 keeps the build off of.) Plus a socket-surface smoke-test on the running --service: ss -lntup + strace -f -e trace=bind,listen,connect MUST show exactly one v4 TCP listener on the direct port, zero UDP listeners, and no unsolicited outbound connect — the build-time empirical confirmation that the binary binds what Appendix B claims (R-D3/R-D5/R-D6), with R-A4 the permanent runtime backstop.
R-B6MUST Drop the Sciter UI
The repo carries two UI stacks (Flutter + legacy Sciter). Sciter is why Rust is pinned to 1.75 (it breaks on the 1.78 i128-layout change — flutter-build.yml), why a 32-bit i686-pc-windows-msvc job exists, and the source of a large HTML/JS (res/inline-sciter.py, src/ui/*.tis) surface. Building Flutter-only drops the sciter-rs fork, the i686 job, and the .tis tree — and unblocks a modern Rust toolchain, removing a whole class of build/maintenance and attack surface. Decision: the fork nonetheless keeps Rust 1.75 (edition 2021) for now — the deps are pinned, so a bump buys no security and would force re-validating the entire lock; moving to a newer stable is optional later work, gated on a green --locked build + audit (R-R3).

(Version pinning: R-B5a/R-B5b in §3.1 — build from the committed Cargo.lock with the pinned toolchain; track/mirror the 34 rustdesk-org git deps.)

R-B7MUST Reproduce upstream 1.4.7's official build first; the fork is an auditable delta on it
Before any fork change, reproduce RustDesk's official 1.4.7 build for each target — the upstream build.py / flutter-build.yml steps, run with the §3.1 toolchain (Rust 1.75, Flutter 3.24.5, NDK r28c, vcpkg 120deac3, LLVM 15) and the committed lockfiles (R-R1) — so the artifacts differ from upstream's by exactly two things and no others: no code-signing, and the build runs off GitHub-hosted runners (R-B2). One faithful-reproduction subtlety the delta accounting MUST include: upstream's generate-bridge job runs flutter_rust_bridge_codegen — pinned separately at FRB 1.80.1 (bridge.yml) — to emit the uncommitted generated_bridge.dart/bridge_generated.rs that every build job needs; the fork MUST reproduce it as a pinned, ./online-fetched step (§12.1, R-B12), or the "exactly two deltas" claim is false. The build process is inherited, not reinvented — identical to how RustDesk builds 0c86d46 (submodule df6badc) officially, minus those two. Establishing this baseline parity first makes every later difference a known consequence of a §7–§10 requirement, never a build accident, and is the entry point the §15 acceptance is measured from.
R-B8MUST Build hosts: all three targets on the one Linux x86_64 box — Debian & Android in Docker, Windows in a KVM VM
Windows cannot be cross-built from Linux (MSVC + WiX are Windows-only, §12.2), so it needs a genuine Windows environment — but that environment can be a virtual machine on the same box. So the Debian x86_64 .deb and Android aarch64 .apk build in Docker on the Linux x86_64 host (Android cross-compiles from Linux, as upstream's own CI does on ubuntu-24.04), and the Windows x86_64 .exe/.msi builds in an ephemeral KVM Windows 11 guest on that same bare-metal host (§12.2; a dedicated bare-metal Windows 11 machine is an equivalent option). All three thus run on one Linux x86_64 host with no GitHub-hosted runner in the path (R-B2), each as a disposable instance of a pinned, immutable template (Docker image or golden VM image) provisioned to the pinned toolchain (R-B5a) and nothing more, and torn down when the build completes (§12.1).
R-B9MUST One build script per target — environment-validated, fail-loud, no fallbacks, one mode
The build is driven by checked-in scripts, one per target (build-debian.sh, build-android.sh, build-windows.ps1), held to the same engineering bar as the rest of this spec — the "one option, the good option" discipline applied to the build:
  • Validate the environment, then abort. Assert the exact pinned versions — not mere presence — of Rust / Flutter / NDK / the vcpkg baseline / LLVM / JDK, plus the OS + arch, the required system libraries, and the submodule + lockfile state, before compiling a byte.
  • Fail loud; no fallbacks. set -euo pipefail (PowerShell $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'); every check dies with a precise message; no "install latest if missing", "retry another way", or silent default — a single mode of operation, the correct one.
  • Pin from one manifest. Every version comes from a single committed pins file; no command resolves "latest" or a moving channel (R-B5a, R-R1).
  • No second build scaffold. The repository carries no root-level Dockerfile/entrypoint.sh builder and no translated upstream README build path. Debian/Android Docker builds are the digest-pinned scripts/Dockerfile.* images created by scripts/online-fetch.sh; Windows builds are the pinned VM flow. A live-fetch root Dockerfile with rustup, raw Sciter downloads, unchecked CMake downloads, or ad-hoc vcpkg bootstrap is not a supported fallback.
  • Self-verifying + idempotent. Re-running is safe; on success the script verifies the artifact (it exists, is the right type, and matches its recorded SHA-256, R-B2) and prints that — it never reports success it has not proven (the full per-script behavioral contract is §12.3).
R-B10MUST One networked step: online-fetch.sh → a git-ignored, SHA-256-verified ./online
The repository is build-oriented and offline-by-construction. A single checked-in scripts/online-fetch.sh is the only script permitted to touch the network; it materializes every resource the repo does not embed — cargo vendor of the committed lockfile (incl. the 38 git deps), the pub/Gradle caches, vcpkg @120deac3 and its asset cache, the NDK r28c zip, the Flutter SDK, LLVM 15.0.6, the Android SDK (cmdline-tools / platform-34 / build-tools-34.0.0), the pinned base-image digests, and the Windows installers/ISOs — into ./online/, which is git-ignored. Every artifact is checked against its pinned SHA-256 in the single scripts/pins.env manifest (the machine-readable §3.2); any mismatch MUST abort fail-closed. This reconciles R-R1's "pinning ≠ vendoring" with the offline build: the bulky pinned world is cached, not committed — re-creatable from pins.env and re-verifiable, never trusted from the network at build time. The build scripts (R-B8/B9, §12.1–§12.2) then run with the network namespace removed (--network=none) and MUST refuse to run — never silently fetch — if ./online is incomplete or any SHA fails. The isolation MUST be of the compile container itself, not merely "no published ports": its own network namespace removed, no Docker socket bind-mounted in, and the warm caches mounted read-only — with a canary build.rs in CI that attempts an outbound connect and MUST fail the compile stage, so "no fetch at compile time" is proven, not trusted. Run order: cleanup.sh --build-host-network (preflight scrub of any prior harness-created VM/network state) → host-provision.sh (once) → online-fetch.sh (once, or on a pins.env change) → build-* (per build, offline) → cleanup.sh.
R-B11MUST Minimal host provisioning; no system libvirt network, no host DNS/DHCP listener
scripts/host-provision.sh installs only the host-level runtimes the ephemeral envs need. For Docker that means the container engine with no published ports. For the Windows VM that means direct QEMU/KVM components only: qemu-system-x86, qemu-utils, OVMF firmware, swtpm if TPM emulation is used, and the ISO/disk helper tools the scripts call. It MUST NOT install or start libvirt-daemon-system merely to build Windows; MUST NOT define, start, or autostart the libvirt default network; MUST NOT create virbr0; MUST NOT start libvirt's dnsmasq; and MUST NOT change host-wide IP forwarding. The observed bad state from the old build-machine provisioning was exactly this class of side effect: dnsmasq listening on 192.168.122.1:53/tcp and 192.168.122.1:53/udp, dnsmasq DHCP listening on 0.0.0.0%virbr0:67/udp, a host-owned virbr0 address 192.168.122.1/24, and host IP forwarding enabled. None of that is required to compile RustDesk for Windows: the golden provisioner needs the guest to make outbound HTTPS connections once, and the per-build VM needs no network at all. QEMU user-mode networking gives the guest internal DHCP/DNS/NAT inside the QEMU process without a host dnsmasq, host bridge, or host port 53/67 listener; the build must use that or --network none, not a host bridge. This requirement does not ban VM control ports as such: a headless Windows install may need VNC, and a manual provision/debug path may need SSH into the guest. Those are permitted only as host-local control channels — for example 127.0.0.1:5901 for QEMU VNC, 127.0.0.1:2222 forwarded to guest :22, or a Unix monitor socket. The forbidden property is a non-loopback host bind, not the existence of a local VNC/SSH control path.
R-B11aMUST Cleanup is the inverse of provisioning and must remove prior harness-created VM networking
scripts/cleanup.sh is the explicit opposite of host-provision.sh. It MUST remove every harness-created VM artifact and only those artifacts: QEMU pidfiles/processes it launched, Unix monitor sockets, swtpm state/sockets, qcow2 overlays, generated ISOs, transient domains if a session libvirt path is still used, and any loopback-only debug forwards. For host packages, it removes only packages recorded in the persistent .provisioned manifest as installed-by-us; it MUST NOT remove or downgrade packages that pre-existed. But because an older harness version did install system libvirt, cleanup also has a security duty: if the manifest says the harness installed libvirt-daemon-system, cleanup MUST stop and undefine the libvirt default network, remove its autostart, and remove the harness-installed package so virbr0 and libvirt dnsmasq disappear. If libvirt pre-existed and therefore cannot be removed safely, cleanup MUST fail closed with an explicit finding instead of pretending the host is clean. A post-cleanup and post-provision audit MUST assert: no virbr0; no listener on 192.168.122.1:53/tcp or :53/udp; no 0.0.0.0%virbr0:67/udp; no new non-loopback TCP or UDP listener of any kind; no host-wide ip_forward change attributable to the harness. The only acceptable build-control listeners are loopback (127.0.0.1) or Unix-domain sockets, and even those are temporary.
R-B12MUST Pin provenance, not just a hash — close the bootstrap TOFU and the codegen/distfile gaps
R-B10 verifies every ./online artifact against a SHA-256 in pins.env, but a hash is only as trustworthy as its origin. The fork MUST establish each first pin by an audited, dual-sourced bootstrap — cross-checking the publisher's own published hash/signature (Rustup, Flutter, LLVM, the NDK, and the Windows toolchain all publish them) against a second path — and record per-pin provenance, so a compromised mirror/CDN at first-fetch cannot silently poison the anchor (the distinction is pinned vs pinned-and-provenance-verified). Three inputs R-B10's enumeration did not originally reach MUST stay under the same regime: (a) the vcpkg distfiles — the retained libyuv overlay port fetches from googlesource.com, whose Gitiles archives are not byte-reproducible, so online-fetch.sh captures a deterministic git archive | gzip -n tarball into ./online and the portfile consumes it with SHA512; AV1/libaom is no longer a vcpkg dependency (Appendix C #88); (b) the flutter_rust_bridge_codegen binary (R-B7) and the bindgen libclang + the cc compiler versions in the image — codegen-determinism inputs the R-B2 double-build depends on (bindgen output varies with libclang, pinned via LLVM 15.0.6 in R-B5a; name them so an image drift is caught); and (c) the Windows toolchain installers, which are partly evergreen (the VS Build Tools bootstrapper and the Win11 ISO are not stably SHA-addressable) — for these the fork MUST pin the captured offline layout by SHA-256 and document which inputs are publisher-hash-verified vs evergreen-best-effort. Finally, the 44 Cargo + 8 pub git deps and the hbb_common submodule are anchored only by git's SHA-1 commit hash (no content checksum, unlike the SHA-256-pinned crates.io/pub deps) — a residual bounded by git's collision-detection and the R-B5b diff-audit that MUST be documented as accepted; and the three ref: HEAD Dart fork pins (R-B5b) MUST be rewritten to their resolved commit so a re-resolve cannot float.

12.1 The containerized build flow — Debian x86_64 and Android aarch64, in turn

This is the concrete realization of R-B7–R-B9 for the two artifacts that build on the Linux x86_64 host in Docker — the Debian x86_64 .deb and the Android aarch64 .apk, built in turn; the Windows .exe/.msi follows the same discipline in a KVM virtual machine on the same box (§12.2). Both Docker builds run inside a digest-pinned image — the image is the versioned build environment R-B8 demands ("provisioned to the pinned toolchain and nothing more"). This does not contradict §17's "no Docker": that rule governs the controlled host's runtime, never the build. Every toolchain version, vcpkg triplet, feature flag and build command below is taken verbatim from upstream's 1.4.7 build (R-B7, R-B5a) — the fork makes no independent version choices; its only deltas are to pin what upstream uses, run off GitHub on one box, drop code-signing (R-B2), build offline (R-B5b), bind loopback-only (R-D3), and drop the hwcodec/vram features — software codec only (R-R2b).

Build environments are ephemeral instances of an immutable, pinned template

Every target follows the same disposable-instance discipline ("cattle, not pets"). The persistent, version-controlled artifact is the template — the Docker image pinned by digest (Debian/Android) or the golden Windows VM image (§12.2). Each build spins up a fresh, throwaway instance of that template (a docker run --rm container; a direct QEMU/KVM process or transient qemu:///session domain over a copy-on-write qcow2 overlay, with no system libvirt network), runs the validated flow, copies out the artifact and its SHA-256, and is then stopped and deleted — no instance survives the task, and no state carries between builds. That is what makes R-B9's "one mode, no drift" structural rather than aspirational: every build starts from the byte-identical pinned baseline, so the recorded SHA-256 (R-B2) is reproducible, and nothing mutable accumulates on the host.

Why Docker is the faithful path, not a deviation (R-B7)

Upstream already builds the .deb inside a container: flutter-build.yml's build-rustdesk-linux runs every step through rustdesk-org/run-on-arch-action in an ubuntu18.04 container — a deliberately old glibc baseline that keeps the .deb portable. The Android job cross-compiles natively on ubuntu-24.04. So a pinned ubuntu:18.04 image for the .deb and a pinned ubuntu:24.04 image for the .apk reproduce upstream's own environments off GitHub-hosted runners — the "exactly two deltas (no signing, no GitHub runner) and no others" bar of R-B7.

The shared two-stage shape. Each script MUST split a network-enabled fetch stage from a network-isolated compile stage, so "no build.rs / Gradle / vcpkg fetch at compile time" (R-B5b) is proven structurally, not trusted:

  1. Validate (R-B9) — assert the exact pinned versions, the base-image digest, and the submodule + lockfile state; abort on any mismatch before a byte compiles.
  2. Fetch (network on) — cargo vendor the lockfile (incl. the 34 rustdesk-org git deps, R-B5b), build/restore the vcpkg deps from the pinned baseline, warm the Flutter/pub/Gradle caches.
  3. Compile (--network=none) — build --locked --offline; with the network namespace gone, any forgotten fetch fails loudly here instead of silently succeeding.
  4. Package + self-verify (R-B2, R-B9) — produce the artifact, record its SHA-256, re-check it; never claim success unproven.

No new exposure — loopback only. No build stage opens a non-loopback listener: dockerd is reached over its unix socket with no published ports, the network-on fetch stage is outbound only, and the Windows VM (§12.2) uses QEMU user-mode guest networking for outbound provisioning and --network none for the per-build VM. VM control can still require inbound host sockets: VNC is often the only way to inspect a stuck installer, and an SSH host-forward may be the simplest manual guest-control path. That is acceptable only when the socket is host-local: VNC on 127.0.0.1:5901, SSH forwarding on 127.0.0.1:2222 to guest :22, or a Unix-domain monitor/socket. Binding those same services to 0.0.0.0, 10.0.0.200, 192.168.122.1, virbr0, or any other non-loopback address turns a local control mechanism into host attack surface and is forbidden. The build adds nothing to the host's audited public ports (R-D3) and is safe to run on a no-firewall DMZ host only if this invariant is true.

Debian x86_64 — build-debian.sh

# Dockerfile — base pinned by DIGEST (upstream's glibc baseline), not the floating tag
FROM ubuntu:18.04@sha256:<pin>
# provision the EXACT pinned toolchain, nothing more (R-B5a, R-B8):
#   apt build deps (build-essential, cmake, libgtk-3-dev, libxcb*-dev, libasound2-dev, pkg-config, …)
#   Rust 1.75 x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu  · Flutter 3.24.5 (checkout the 3.24.5 ref, not 'stable')
#   vcpkg @ 120deac3 + res/vcpkg overlay ports

# build-debian.sh, run inside the image:
# --- FETCH (network on) ---
cargo vendor --locked vendor/                          # incl. the 34 rustdesk-org git deps (R-B5b)
flutter_rust_bridge_codegen --rust-input src/flutter_ffi.rs …   # REQUIRED upstream step (generate-bridge): emits the UNCOMMITTED bridge; pin FRB 1.80.1 (§3.2/R-B12); codegen bin fetched into ./online, SHA-pinned
$VCPKG_ROOT/vcpkg install --triplet x64-linux          # libvpx,libyuv,opus,… (pin libyuv distfile by SHA512 — R-B12)

# --- COMPILE (run this stage with --network=none) ---
cargo build --locked --offline --lib --release \
      --features flutter,unix-file-copy-paste   # the Rust core — upstream's set minus hwcodec (software codec only, R-R2b)
DEB_ARCH=amd64 python3 ./build.py --flutter --skip-cargo # flutter build linux + dpkg-deb (build_flutter_deb)

# --- PACKAGE + VERIFY (R-B2) ---
mv rustdesk-*.deb rustdesk-${VERSION}-x86_64.deb
sha256sum rustdesk-${VERSION}-x86_64.deb | tee SHA256SUMS  # re-verified on the target over SSH

Faithful to upstream's build-rustdesk-linux (cargo build --locked --lib --features flutter,unix-file-copy-paste --release → python3./build.py --flutter --skip-cargo → build_flutter_deb → dpkg-deb), minus signing, the GitHub runner, and the dropped hwcodec feature (R-R2b). The systemd unit + postinst ship in the .deb unchanged (R-D1, §17).

Android aarch64 — build-android.sh

# Dockerfile — base = upstream's Android runner OS, pinned by DIGEST
FROM ubuntu:24.04@sha256:<pin>
# provision the pinned toolchain (R-B5a):
#   JDK (pinned) · Android SDK cmdline-tools (numbered, NOT ';latest') · one platform
#   build-tools <= 34.0.0 (apksigner trap, below) · NDK r28c = 28.2.13676358 (VENDOR zip + verify SHA — r28c is delisted)
#   Flutter 3.24.5 · Rust 1.75 + target aarch64-linux-android · cargo-ndk 3.1.2 (--locked)
#   nasm + yasm (libvpx SIMD asm — build fails without them) · vcpkg @ 120deac3
yes | sdkmanager --licenses                            # non-interactive accept (re-check on a version bump)

# build-android.sh, run inside the image:
# --- FETCH (network on) ---
export ANDROID_NDK_HOME=…  VCPKG_ROOT=…
./flutter/build_android_deps.sh arm64-v8a              # vcpkg install --triplet arm64-android (oboe,libvpx,libyuv,opus,libjpeg-turbo)
cargo vendor --locked vendor/
flutter pub get                                         # warm pub + Gradle caches now; offline later

# --- COMPILE (run this stage with --network=none) ---
./flutter/ndk_arm64.sh                                  # cargo ndk --platform 21 --target aarch64-linux-android build --locked --release --features flutter (hwcodec dropped — software codec only, R-R2b)
cp $ANDROID_NDK_HOME/…/aarch64-linux-android/libc++_shared.so flutter/android/app/src/main/jniLibs/arm64-v8a/
flutter build apk --release --target-platform android-arm64 --split-per-abi

# --- SIGN OUTSIDE THE IMAGE (keystore bind-mounted at sign time ONLY), then VERIFY (R-B2) ---
zipalign -v 4 app-arm64-v8a-release-unsigned.apk aligned.apk      # align BEFORE signing
apksigner sign --ks $KEYSTORE --out rustdesk-${VERSION}-arm64.apk aligned.apk
sha256sum rustdesk-${VERSION}-arm64.apk | tee -a SHA256SUMS

Faithful to upstream's build-rustdesk-android (build_android_deps.sh arm64-v8a → cargo install cargo-ndk --version … --locked → ndk_arm64.sh → flutter build apk --target-platform android-arm64 --split-per-abi → apksigner), minus the GitHub runner. The key is the fork's self-generated local key (no CA, no Play Store) — R-B2.

Versions come from upstream — we only pin and record

No independent version choices: the Android SDK / build-tools, NDK r28c, Flutter, cargo-ndk and the vcpkg triplet are exactly upstream's 1.4.7 values (R-B5a, R-B7) — we commit them and build --locked. Integrity is the recorded SHA-256 (R-B2), not cross-rebuild byte-identity, so no R8 cross-rebuild-byte-identity gymnastics are required — though the BUILD_DATE wall-clock timestamp (R-B2, hbb_common/src/lib.rs) MUST still be neutralized via SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH, or even a same-host rebuild yields a different SHA-256 and the recorded-hash bar cannot be met. One inherited caveat worth respecting when you pin: apksigner from build-tools ≥ 35 re-pads ZIP alignment, so pin the build-tools version upstream's 1.4.7 build used (or pass --alignment-preserved), and keep zipalign before signing.

The integrity bar for this flow

The target is same committed lockfiles + same pinned image → the same recorded SHA-256, re-verified on the controlled host over SSH (R-B2) — not byte-identical-for-any-third-party reproducibility, a harder bar this spec does not claim. Docker supplies the environmental determinism that keeps the SHA-256 stable across rebuilds on this one host; the committed lockfiles plus --locked --offline under --network=none supply the rest.

12.2 Windows x86_64 — the ephemeral KVM Windows 11 VM

Windows is the one target that cannot be cross-built from Linux: flutter build windows runs only on a Windows host (the desktop embedder needs Visual Studio's MSVC "Desktop development with C++"), the vcpkg x64-windows-static codecs need cl.exe, and the installer needs WiX — all Windows-only. So the .exe/.msi build in a Windows 11 guest, realized here as an ephemeral KVM virtual machine on the same bare-metal Linux x86_64 box (any x86_64 host with hardware-virtualization / KVM support) — so all three targets live on one machine with no second physical host and no GitHub-hosted runner. (Where the build shares the §17 host, all build tooling must obey the same no-firewall/DMZ model as the runtime: no non-loopback listener, no host bridge DNS/DHCP, no assumption that a router or LAN segment is trusted, and no "temporary during build" exception.)

Observed build-machine network regression — not a Windows-build requirement

The live build host showed libvirt's default network, not a RustDesk runtime service: dnsmasq on 192.168.122.1:53/tcp and 192.168.122.1:53/udp, DHCP on 0.0.0.0%virbr0:67/udp, and virbr0 owning 192.168.122.1/24. Those listeners are bad even though they are not 0.0.0.0. First principles: any non-loopback IP assigned to this host is a local destination in the kernel; interface names are not an internet security boundary; and a compromised router is not just "the public internet through DNAT" but the host's Layer-2 neighbor/default gateway, able to send Ethernet frames directly to the host's MAC with destination 192.168.122.1 or to alter LAN routing/NAT. Even if a daemon such as libvirt's dnsmasq attempts receive-interface filtering, this build must not depend on daemon-specific filtering to make an unwanted service safe. The only host-side bind that is inherently outside the LAN/router threat model is loopback (127.0.0.1) or a Unix-domain socket. The observed QEMU debug listeners 127.0.0.1:2222 (optional guest SSH forward) and 127.0.0.1:5901 (VNC) are acceptable only because they are loopback-only and temporary; the canonical per-build path does not require SSH and uses no guest network at all. The unrelated intentional service observed on 0.0.0.0:8472/tcp is outside the Windows builder and not a precedent for build tooling.

VNC/SSH control ports are allowed only as local ports

The Windows build may need a way for the Linux host to control or inspect the guest. That requirement is real: VNC can be critical during unattended installation, and SSH can be useful for a manual/debug provision path. The safe implementation is local-only: -vnc 127.0.0.1:1 (host 127.0.0.1:5901), hostfwd=tcp:127.0.0.1:2222-:22 if guest SSH is deliberately enabled, and a Unix-domain QEMU monitor socket. These are host-to-guest control channels, not services offered to the LAN. The unsafe implementation is the same service bound to any non-loopback address: 0.0.0.0, 10.0.0.200, 192.168.122.1, virbr0, or another physical/virtual interface. In other words: the port is not the defect; the bind address is. Loopback VNC/SSH may be necessary; non-loopback VNC/SSH is forbidden.

The template — built once. Provision a Windows 11 guest with the pinned toolchain and nothing more (R-B5a): VS 2022 Build Tools + "Desktop development with C++", Windows 11 SDK, LLVM 15.0.6, Flutter 3.24.5, Rust 1.75 (x86_64-pc-windows-msvc), vcpkg @120deac3 (x64-windows-static), WiX, and the unattended build/logon task that runs the checked-in Windows build script — then freeze it read-only as the golden backing image. The template provision step is the only Windows VM step allowed to have guest outbound networking, and that networking MUST be QEMU user-mode networking (-netdev user / SLIRP, or libvirt type='user' on a session connection only) with no host bridge, no TAP, no virbr0, no host dnsmasq, no public host forwarding, and no host-wide forwarding. The per-build VM MUST have no NIC at all (--network none / no -netdev) because it receives source and offline dependencies as virtual media and writes artifacts to an output disk. Windows 11's installer requirements are implementation details: OVMF for UEFI, optional swtpm if the chosen flow uses a TPM rather than an unattended installer bypass, and an inbox-supported NIC such as e1000e for the provisioning-only user-mode network. This golden image is the Windows analog of a digest-pinned Docker image — version-stamped, and the reproducibility anchor. Two integrity steps are mandatory: the golden image MUST be verified by the SHA-256 of its qcow2 before each build (a version-stamp string is forgeable; a content hash is not), and the Windows toolchain installers that seed the image are partly evergreen, so the fork MUST apply R-B12's captured-layout pinning rather than assume a stable upstream URL. If SSH is ever enabled for manual diagnosis, its host key MUST be pinned and the host forward MUST be 127.0.0.1-only; SSH is not a required part of the canonical build flow.

# HOST PREFLIGHT: fail closed if an old VM harness left host networking behind
assert_no_listener 192.168.122.1 53 tcp udp
assert_no_listener '0.0.0.0%virbr0' 67 udp
assert_no_interface virbr0

# TEMPLATE (built once): golden Win11 image, pinned toolchain, frozen read-only
golden=win11-rustdesk-golden.qcow2           # the pinned "image" == read-only backing file
# PROVISION ONLY: guest outbound via SLIRP; no hostfwd by default
# If manual/debug guest SSH is required, replace the next line with:
#   -netdev user,id=net0,hostfwd=tcp:127.0.0.1:2222-:22
# Never hostfwd to 0.0.0.0, 10.0.0.200, 192.168.122.1, or any non-loopback address.
qemu-system-x86_64 ... \
  -drive file="$golden",if=none,id=disk0,format=qcow2 ... \
  -netdev user,id=net0 \
  -device e1000e,netdev=net0 \
  -vnc 127.0.0.1:1 \
  -monitor unix:"$state/monitor.sock",server,nowait

# PER BUILD: a disposable instance with NO NETWORK — the exact analog of `docker run --rm --network=none`
qemu-img create -f qcow2 -b "$golden" -F qcow2 build.qcow2      # copy-on-write overlay, throwaway
qemu-system-x86_64 ... \
  -drive file=build.qcow2,if=none,id=disk0,format=qcow2 ... \
  -drive file=win-build-src.iso,media=cdrom,readonly=on ... \
  -drive file=win-build-offline.iso,media=cdrom,readonly=on ... \
  -drive file=win-build-output.img,format=raw ... \
  -net none \
  -vnc 127.0.0.1:1 \
  -monitor unix:"$state/monitor.sock",server,nowait

# The guest logon task runs build-windows.ps1 from the BUILD medium, writes .exe/.msi + SHA256SUMS to OUTPUT.
extract_output_disk_readonly win-build-output.img ./dist
sha256sum -c dist/SHA256SUMS                                  # verify on the way out (R-B2)

# TEARDOWN: stop + delete — no instance survives; the golden image is untouched
qemu_monitor_system_powerdown_or_kill
rm -f build.qcow2 win-build-src.iso win-build-offline.iso win-build-output.img
assert_no_new_non_loopback_listener

build-windows-vm.sh is the Linux-side wrapper that creates the disposable overlay/media/output disk, starts the no-network Windows VM, extracts the output disk read-only, verifies SHA256SUMS, and tears the instance down. Inside the guest, build-windows.ps1 mirrors upstream's build-for-windows-flutter — $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop', assert the pinned versions, then vcpkg install --triplet x64-windows-static from the prewarmed/offline cache → build.py --portable --flutter (cargo features flutter — hwcodec/vram dropped, software codec only, R-R2b; runs flutter build windows --release) → WiX .msi → record the SHA-256 (R-B9). No cross-compile, no code-signing, no per-build network, and no host listener except temporary loopback/Unix diagnostics — integrity is the recorded SHA-256, verified on the target over the trusted channel (R-B2).

12.3 The script contract — idempotent, defensive, fail-closed

Every script of §12 (R-B9/B10/B11/R-B11a) obeys one behavioral contract, so the harness is predictable regardless of the host's prior state. Each is a convergent, idempotent step: run it when the system already matches pins.env and it is a no-op (it verifies, says "already satisfied," exits 0); run it on a fresh host and it establishes only what is absent. One rule makes that safe.

Defensive means: complain loudly and die — not "deal with it"

A script may create what is missing; it MUST NOT mutate what is present-but-wrong to force a match. A wrong-version tool, a SHA that doesn't match the pin, a conflicting artifact it did not create — each is an anomaly, and the correct response is a precise, non-zero, loud abort naming expected vs found — never a silent upgrade, downgrade, overwrite, retry, or skip. Reconciling a genuine change (a bumped pin) is an explicit operator action — cleanup.sh the stale state, then re-run — not something a script does behind your back. Every component distrusts both the environment and the outputs of its sibling scripts and re-verifies them before acting (set -euo pipefail / $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'; every value from pins.env; success proven, never assumed). "Establish-the-absent" is the only mutation any script performs.

ScriptNo-op when already satisfiedEstablishes (absent only)Dies loudly when
host-provision.shevery host runtime present at the pinned version, and the host-network audit is cleaninstalls an absent runtime; records it in .provisioned; installs QEMU/KVM pieces only for Windows, never system libvirt networkinga runtime is present at the wrong version (it never up/downgrades it); OS/arch unsupported; pins.env invalid; virbr0, libvirt dnsmasq, host port 53/67, or any new non-loopback listener exists
online-fetch.shevery ./online artifact present and its SHA-256 matches pins.envfetches an absent artifact, then verifies its SHA-256a present artifact's SHA ≠ pin ("run cleanup.sh, then re-fetch"); a freshly-fetched SHA mismatch; pins.env invalid
build-debian.sh · build-android.sh— always rebuilds in a fresh ephemeral instance (deterministic, so re-running is safe and yields the same bytes)the artifact + its recorded SHA-256./online incomplete/unverified; a host runtime missing or wrong-version; the in-image toolchain ≠ pin; any compile or verify failure
provision-windows-vm.shthe golden Win11 image is present, matches its recorded content SHA-256, and no stale build-host networking existsbuilds the golden image from the ./online installers using guest outbound-only QEMU user networking; creates no host bridge, no host DNS/DHCP, no non-loopback listenerthe golden image is present but stale/mismatched (remove it, re-run); a required ./online Windows artifact is missing; provisioning would require system libvirt default networking or any public/non-loopback host listener
build-windows-vm.sh · build-windows.ps1— fresh no-network overlay each run (§12.2)the .exe/.msi + SHA-256the golden image is absent or mismatched; the in-VM toolchain ≠ pin; the VM has any NIC during per-build; build or verify failure; teardown leaves any non-loopback listener
cleanup.shnothing the harness created remains and the host-network audit is cleanremoves only harness-created instances/images and, explicitly, .provisioned-recorded host packages; reverses old harness-created libvirt default networking if the manifest proves the harness installed ithost-package removal is requested but .provisioned is absent (it refuses rather than guess); libvirt/virbr0/dnsmasq remains but pre-existed and cannot be removed safely; any cleanup would touch unrecorded host state

Net: re-running the whole chain on an already-set-up box does nothing and says so; re-running after a pin bump fails loudly at the first stale artifact and tells you to cleanup.sh first; running it on a foreign or half-broken host stops at the first anomaly with an exact expected-vs-found diagnosis. No step ever "fixes" the host out from under you — and the one mutation it does make (establishing the absent) is recorded so cleanup.sh can undo exactly it (R-B11/R-B11a).

13. Technical-debt register

What the fork inherits and must consciously carry, track, or close — spanning 910 Rust crates, 208 Dart/pub packages, ~736 unsafe sites, a native C/C++ codec stack, and 11 CI workflows. Each row has a disposition; the build and supply-chain rows are actioned in §16, the deployment ones in §17.

Inherited debtWhereDisposition
Supply chain: 34 rustdesk-org/* forks (+ a skywind3000/kcp submodule under kcp-sys; + bjornsnoen/x11-rs, clslaid/x11-clipboard on Linux) — diffed clean at these SHAs (benign/hardening)Cargo.lockMIRROR (preserve hardening; incl. submodule + non-org deps) (R-B5b)
Two UI stacks (Flutter + Sciter) = two build paths, two toolchainsCargo.toml featuresDROP Sciter (R-B6)
Pre-auth ~1 GiB frame DoS (max_packet_length=usize::MAX, never set)bytes_codec.rsFIX (R-S7)
Failure limiter grows unbounded (evicts only on success) & blocks cumulatively/forever — memory-exhaustion + permanent-lockout DoS (a self-inflicted lockout of the owner), acute over IPv6; and emits no log line on failure/block (no fail2ban signal)connection.rsBOUND + DECAY + per-source + LOG (R-S10)
Symlink TOCTOU deferred upstream; full filesystem reach is intended for the authenticated peer (§2) — the defect is the TOCTOU, not the reachfs.rsNO-FOLLOW; full-FS single mode, no toggle (R-S8)
Port-forward/RDP tunnel plaintext after set_rawconnection.rs, port_forward.rsKEEP KEY ON (R-S5)

Build, supply-chain & platform debt (research-derived — actioned in §16/§17)
Build sprawl: a 647-line build.py + 11 workflows build iOS, macOS, 32-bit Sciter, fdroid, nightly & playground variants you will not ship; 7 build.rs scripts execute code at build timebuild.py, .github/workflows/PRUNE to 3 targets (§16)
Two supply chains: 910 Rust crates and 208 Dart/pub packages (61 direct) for the Flutter UI — a second ecosystem to pin & audit (both lockfiles already committed)Cargo.lock, flutter/pubspec.lockPIN by committed lockfiles (§16)
Dependency-advisory gate: the lockfile has been fixed where the Rust 1.75 build allows it (url 2.5.4 / idna 1.0.3, rand 0.8.6, crossbeam-channel 0.5.15, tracing-subscriber 0.3.20, users replaced by uzers in the in-tree dependency surface). Remaining RustSec findings are deliberate deny.toml accepts with per-advisory reasons; scripts/audit.sh runs both cargo-audit and cargo-deny check advisories against a pinned advisory snapshot, deriving cargo-audit ignores only from TOML ignore objects. Dart/Pub is gated by offline osv-scanner with an empty accept-list. Native vcpkg C/C++ codec CVEs are tracked separately because Rust/Dart advisory tools cannot see them.Cargo.lock, deny.toml, scripts/audit.sh, scripts/dart-audit.shGATED (R-R3/R-A7)
~8 git deps are Apple-only (cidre, cidre-macros, cacao, core-foundation/-sys/-graphics/-graphics-types, nokhwa-bindings-macos)Cargo.lockDROP with macOS/iOS (§16, R-R2)
Rust pinned to 1.75 by Sciter (1.78 i128-layout break) — blocks a modern, security-patched toolchainflutter-build.ymlDROP Sciter → bump Rust (R-B6)
Native C/C++ codec surface: vcpkg builds libvpx, libyuv, opus, and libjpeg-turbo. The binary is a full viewer, so native media code is present; but the --server role never decodes peer video (it encodes its own screen) — its only inbound C-decode is opus + zstd + clipboard-image, entirely post-PAKE from the authenticated peer (§2). Encode/decode are software-only by compile-out: hwcodec/vram and ffmpeg's amf/nvcodec/qsv are dropped on every build; AV1/libaom is runtime-quarantined and removed as a dependency; VP8/VP9 remain the supported software video codecs. That surface is reached only when a box views — the universal residual the fork SHOULD sandbox (Appendix C #2b). Audit caveat: these are vcpkg C libraries, not crates — cargo-audit/RUSTSEC (R-A7, Appendix D) cannot see their CVEs, so R-B5a pinning buys reproducibility, not CVE-freedom against this surfacevcpkg.json, libs/scrap, hwcodec--server never decodes; SHOULD-sandbox; PIN (≠ CVE-free)
Memory-safety debt: ~736 unsafe sites across src/+libs/ (FFI to the C libs, screen capture, input injection, IPC)tree-wideTRACK; review any pre-PAKE-reachable ones
Release profile is panic=abort + no overflow-checks (integer overflow wraps silently); the AddrMangle::decode underflow on crafted bytes is mediator-only — unreachable on the direct-only path, and with overflow-checks off it yields a garbage address, not a crash (audited: no other pre-auth integer/panic/OOB defect)Cargo.toml; hbb_common/src/lib.rssaturating arithmetic; overflow-checks in CI
Bundled default rendezvous public key + servers (the rendezvous trust anchor) — vestigial in a serverless deployment but compiled inconfig.rsPIN/replace own values (R-X4)

hbb_common is the one git submodule, and we change it (the PAKE spans it)libs/hbb_commonABSORB in-tree (§16)
Android client declares 12 runtime permissions (manifest :5-16): accessibility input-injection, SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW, MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, legacy READ/WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, RECORD_AUDIO, boot-persistence, …AndroidManifest.xmlDROP legacy storage; keep dual-role minimum (R-X6)
Windows build-machine host-network expansion: the prior harness installed system libvirt and started its default NAT network, creating virbr0, 192.168.122.1, libvirt dnsmasq DNS on :53/tcp/:53/udp, DHCP on :67/udp, and host forwarding. This is not a RustDesk binary exposure; it is a build-host exposure caused by the Windows VM setup. Non-loopback interface-specific listeners are still bad on a no-firewall DMZ host because a hostile router/L2 neighbor can target host-owned private addresses directlyscripts/host-provision.sh; scripts/cleanup.sh; scripts/provision-windows-vm.sh; scripts/build-windows-vm.shREMOVE FROM HARNESS — no libvirt-daemon-system, no default network, no virbr0, no host DNS/DHCP; cleanup reverses old harness-created state; QEMU user-mode outbound only for golden provisioning and no NIC for per-build (R-B11/R-B11a/§12.2)

14. Non-goals

  • The rendezvous/relay servers (hbbs/hbbr) — serverless by design. (Self-hosting that pair is the upstream-sanctioned road to sovereign-and-encrypted, but it adds internet-facing ports and key-managed services and surrenders the "connect by address, nothing pre-configured" property the PAKE fork keeps.)
  • A new GUI / protocol redesign — keep the engine; change the handshake, the invariants, and excise the dangerous paths, nothing gratuitous.
  • Defending a compromised endpoint or a password-knowing adversary.
  • Host network segmentation — the fork must not worsen pivot risk but does not implement the host firewall/VLAN.

15. Acceptance criteria

  1. The unconditional PAKE (§10) is the only connection path — the responder's accept choke point and, when a box views, the initiator's connect path (the unkeyed direct-IP early-returns deleted, R-S13); the secure parameter, the plaintext path, the trust escape-hatch, and the fingerprint ceremony are deleted; CI proves their absence (R-A6).
  2. All six transports are keyed and all five types + auxiliary channels covered (all five reachable; the terminal pinned ON by the pinned policy, R-X8); the reconnect, switch-sides, trusted-device, and post-key password-challenge paths are deleted — authorization collapses onto the single CPace KEYED edge (R-S2), not re-gated — proven by R-A1/A2/A6/A8.
  3. The port-forward set_raw escape is sealed and wire-capture proves the RDP tunnel is ciphertext (R-A9); no transcript yields offline-crackable material (R-S6).
  4. Pre-auth frame length is capped (R-S7); file writes use no-follow opens, with file transfer a single full-filesystem mode and no confinement toggle (R-S8); the controlled-side security policy — permanent-password-only, password approve-mode, the capability set, egress-empty — is a compile-time table the Config::get_option funnel returns and the is_option_can_save guard rejects, not a permissive default a peer, the rendezvous server, or a local write can change (R-S16), with the one-time-password path excised so the permanent password is the sole credential and sole PAKE PRS (R-S9, R-X7).
  5. The updater, plugin loader, ConfigureUpdate body, key/server overrides, and the os_login→PAM desktop-session-start are excised; the terminal is pinned ON by the immutable enable-terminal=Y pin — full control for the authenticated owner is the single mode, while the second-credential PAM/LogonUserW path stays excised; CI proves both (§8, R-A6, R-X8, R-X14); the trust anchor equals the pinned value (R-A4).
  6. Crypto verification R-V1–V2 complete, findings resolved; the R-V3 independent expert review performed and published (docs/CRYPTO-AUDIT-2026-07-02.md, 2026-07-02, findings resolved); release notes state exactly what review was done — an AI-conducted, single-reviewer review (R-V3).
  7. Unsigned (SHA-256-pinned), source-published, smoke-tested binaries for Android, Windows, and Linux — one package per platform, each a single binary usable as viewer or, via --server, the exposed box (R-R2b) — produced off GitHub-hosted runners by env-validating per-host scripts (Debian + Android on the Linux box, Windows on a Windows 11 machine; R-B8/B9), having first reproduced upstream 1.4.7's official build (R-B7), --locked from the pinned toolchain (R-B5a), Sciter dropped (R-B6).
  8. The deployed surface is understood port-by-port: the shipped binary, run as --server, exposes exactly one inbound v4 TCP port and zero UDP, makes no unsolicited egress, has the rendezvous mediator and LAN discovery absent and the terminal pinned ON by policy as the authenticated owner's full control — on every shipped target (R-D3–R-D7, R-X5, R-X8); a startup self-check asserts the live socket surface (R-A4), so the box is defensible even with no firewall — the firewall is a redundant tripwire, not the gate.
  9. The exposed box runs the one binary as --server (R-R2b): §1's "one strict mode" is structural by deletion — the mediator, relay, KCP, LAN discovery, updater and plugin loader are gone from the tree (sound symbol-greps) — while the viewer/decoder code that necessarily remains (the binary is a full viewer) is unreachable from the --server entry (R-A6 call-graph) and held off the wire by R-A4's live socket-surface assertion. The native-decoder residual is universal and documented (Appendix C #2b), the fork SHOULD-sandboxing it.
  10. Sovereign by construction (§18): every shipped binary — on all three platforms — makes zero connections to any global resource and carries no auto-update, version-check, or self-install path; CI proves the §18 token set compiled-absent (sound grep) or call-graph-unreachable (the dual-use STUN/NAT probes) per target (R-SV10), the runtime self-check covers the --server process (zero UDP, one TCP listener), and the build smoke-test (R-B4) confirms no unsolicited outbound connect. The rendezvous/relay/STUN stack, the updater, the api.rustdesk.com version-check, the Telegram push, the audit/account POSTs and the iOS Firebase plist are compiled out, not config-pinned (R-SV1–R-SV9).
