The only surviving network capability is the direct, PAKE-gated peer connection by IP — connect-by-ID is removed on every platform, so R-F2/R-F3 hold for peers that present a reachable address; the relay-only NAT-traversal case is a deliberate, accepted drop (R-SV5, R-F3).
  11. Apple source-conformance (R-R2): iOS and macOS are not built, but a cargo check --target {x86_64,aarch64}-apple-darwin + aarch64-apple-ios pass proves the philosophies hold on the Apple code paths — the shared wire/auth/config/fs core inherited with no Apple #[cfg], and the Apple platform surfaces (the osascript root updater/install and DMG fetch-and-run, the two in-process root-exec primitives, the authenticated desktop _url IPC, the deep-link incl. the iOS transport and the Rust ?key= adoption, the key/get_key() override, the sandbox-off/persistent-TCC entitlements, and the §18 egress) excised or hardened in source — so any future Apple build on a real Mac is the hardened fork, never the un-hardened upstream.
The one-sentence bar

Connecting by address is encrypted and mutually password-authenticated across every transport, type, and content channel, with no plaintext escape and no fetch-and-run or override path left in the building; the program refuses to run if any of that is untrue; and a build exists for every target device — making it, on the wire, as defensible as SSH.

16. Repository structure (the monorepo)

The fork changes a small, bounded part of RustDesk: the rustdesk application and the hbb_common protocol library — every requirement in this document lands in one of those two (Appendix A). It does not need to copy the dependency universe into the repo. So the monorepo holds only the code we fork or author; every other dependency stays an unmodified upstream artifact, pinned by the committed lockfiles.

Pinning ≠ vendoring

The reproducible pin is already done, upstream: the committed Cargo.lock (910 crates — all 38 git deps to an exact commit SHA), flutter/pubspec.lock (the 208 Dart packages), and vcpkg.json (registry baseline 120deac3) fix every transitive version. A --locked build is reproducible without vendoring a single third-party line. We add only the one pin upstream omits: a committed rust-toolchain.toml (today the Rust version lives only in CI, flutter-build.yml).

rustdesk_fork/                  # ONE repo — fork of rustdesk/rustdesk; only what we change lives here
├─ Cargo.toml  Cargo.lock        # committed lockfile pins all 910 crates (38 git deps by SHA)
├─ rust-toolchain.toml           # NEW — pin Rust in-tree (bumped past 1.75 once Sciter is gone)
├─ src/                          # CHANGED: PAKE choke point, excisions, defaults
├─ flutter/                      # CHANGED: deep-link excision (R-X6); pubspec.lock pins 208 pub pkgs
├─ libs/
│  ├─ hbb_common/                # ABSORBED submodule (in-tree): PAKE/secretbox/fs/codec live here
│  ├─ pake/                      # NEW: in-tree CPace (§10.1)
│  └─ scrap enigo clipboard portable   # already in-tree
├─ res/  build.py  .github/workflows/   # CHANGED: systemd unit/packaging; CI pruned to 3 jobs
├─ scripts/                      # NEW: build harness, all R-B9-discipline. pins.env · lib.sh · online-fetch.sh (R-B10) · host-provision.sh + cleanup.sh (R-B11/R-B11a) · build-debian.sh · build-android.sh · provision-windows-vm.sh · build-windows-vm.sh · build-windows.ps1
└─ vcpkg.json                    # pins the native-lib registry baseline (libs pulled at build, unmodified)

# .gitignore: /online/ — the SHA-256-verified fetch cache (pinned, NOT vendored), re-made by online-fetch.sh (R-B10)
# NOT in the repo: the 34 rustdesk-org forks · crates.io deps · the 208 pub packages · the vcpkg
# native libraries — all unmodified, pulled at build, all pinned by the lockfiles above.
R-R1MUST Fork only what we change; pin everything else by the committed lockfiles
The monorepo contains exactly the code we modify or author: the rustdesk app (its in-tree src/, flutter/, libs/scrap…, res/, CI), the hbb_common library absorbed from its submodule in-tree (it is the only submodule, and the PAKE spans it), the new libs/pake, and a rust-toolchain.toml. Every other dependency — the 34 rustdesk-org forks, all crates.io deps, the 208 Dart packages, the vcpkg native libraries — stays unmodified upstream, pinned by the committed Cargo.lock/pubspec.lock/vcpkg.json. CI builds --locked; reproducibility comes from the lockfiles + toolchain pins, not from vendoring. (Mirroring the rustdesk-org forks to your own org — to stop trusting that GitHub account — stays the optional sovereignty step of R-B5b: it changes where a pinned SHA is fetched, not the pin.)
R-R2MUST Build exactly three targets; delete the rest
Ship Debian x86_64, Windows x86_64, Android aarch64 — one package per platform, each a single binary that is viewer and --server (the controlled side), role chosen at runtime by argv, exactly as upstream (R-R2b). MUST delete the iOS, macOS, 32-bit Sciter (i686), fdroid, nightly and playground jobs; and the Sciter fork. The ~8 Apple-only git deps are platform-gated, so deleting the Apple targets drops them with no lockfile change. Fewer targets, smaller surface — and dropping Sciter unblocks a modern Rust (R-B6). Apple — not a build target, but a source-conformance obligation. This project does not build or ship iOS or macOS, and cannot from its build hosts: unlike Windows (licensed to virtualize on any hardware, hence the §12.2 KVM guest), macOS's licence permits virtualization only on Apple hardware, and a real build needs the macOS-only Apple toolchain (ld64, codesign, CocoaPods, the Flutter build macos/ipa embedders). So Apple stays off the build matrix — but every philosophy and hardening here MUST hold on the macOS/iOS code paths, so any future Apple build on a real Mac is the hardened fork, never the un-hardened upstream. CI enforces this with a cargo check --target {x86_64,aarch64}-apple-darwin + aarch64-apple-ios pass running the R-A6 greps on the Apple cfg (no artifact produced). (Retain-and-check, not delete: removing macos.rs/macos.mm/the iOS plist would make absence a sound grep — but it would discard the hardened Apple code a future Mac build must inherit, leaving only un-hardened upstream to re-add. Keeping the conformant code is the one place this spec audits rather than deletes, justified solely by that future-build goal; since no shipped artifact links the Apple cfg, nothing un-vetted ships either way.) The wire/auth core is already shared — no Apple #[cfg] on the PAKE choke point (the server module compiles on macOS), the two-key cipher and frame cap, the R-S16 funnel, the no-follow writes or the decompress bound — so a macOS build inherits all of it and an iOS build the cipher/codec/config/fs subset. iOS cannot be a controlled side (mod server is #[cfg(not(ios))]; enigo is an iOS stub; no iOS capture backend) but ships mod client and the rustdesk:// scheme, carrying the §4.4 initiator + Appendix-C-#2b residual; and a macOS controlled side cannot run cleanly headless — its --server is welded to the AppKit/tray loop in core_main — so an Apple build is for the operator's own attended use, never the internet-exposed role. The Apple platform surface is now audited, and each obligation lives — platform-tagged — at its home requirement, not in this paragraph: the osascript-root updater/install & quarantine-stripping DMG (R-X1, §18·R-SV2), the two in-process root-exec primitives elevate/Elevate (R-X9/R-X11), the authenticated desktop _url IPC, the iOS deep-link transport, and the Rust ?key= adoption (R-X6), the key/get_key() override and the macOS custom_server/--config/--import-config paths (R-X4), the single-backend macOS capture/input that needs only assertion not excision (R-X12/R-X13), the absent-by-construction PAM path — a negative finding to document, not excise (R-X14), the sandbox-off/persistent-TCC entitlements (R-S14), and the universal egress (§18). Each is tagged macOS/iOS where it differs from the built targets, so the obligation is read where it belongs.
R-R2bMUST Keep RustDesk's single binary, both roles — harden unconditionally; reduce surface by deletion, not by a build profile
Upstream ships one binary that is viewer and controlled side, role by argv (core_main.rs). The fork MUST keep exactly this: one package per platform, both roles, the role chosen at runtime. There is no separate "controlled-only" artifact and no viewer/decode/lockdown/sovereign build feature — the hardening is unconditional, the default and only behaviour, so there is no un-hardened build to mis-deploy. This keeps a box usable in both roles at once — the common case for an exposed jump-host: viewed from the internet while it itself views an inner box it controls (§17, R-SV5). Compiling the viewer role out would break that; a "viewer build vs controlled build" split is the wrong tool for what is really a runtime role. "One strict mode" (§1) is therefore made structural by deletion, not by a profile: the surface an exposed box must not carry is removed from the tree on every build — the rendezvous mediator and relay (R-D4), KCP and the UDP hole-punch (R-S13(d)), LAN discovery (R-X5), the updater (R-X1), the plugin loader (R-X2), 2FA (R-X7), the trust-anchor overrides (R-X4). Because these are genuine deletions, R-A6's absence tokens for them (mod kcp_stream, crate::updater, …) are sound symbol greps on the one and only build — strictly stronger than the per-profile greps a split would have given. What necessarily remains, because the box is a full viewer, is mod client, the Flutter UI, and the in-process VP8/VP9 video decoder (scrap's VpxDecoder); it is not compiled out, while AV1/libaom is runtime-quarantined by Appendix C #87 and removed as a dependency by Appendix C #88; the exposed-box safety boundary is established at runtime:
  • No inbound surface from the viewer role. Initiating is an outbound connected socket, never a listener — so a box acting as a viewer opens no port. The exposed box's reachable inbound surface is exactly one v4-only PAKE-gated TCP port (R-D3/R-D5), and R-A4 asserts this against the live process: the running --server MUST show exactly one TCP listener (21118) and zero UDP sockets, or it refuses to serve. That the wildcard (0.0.0.0) sockets the mediator/punch/LAN would bind are truly gone is thus proven on the running process — the runtime twin of the symbol-grep.
  • The decoder is the universal viewer residual. In its controlled role a box never decodes peer video (it encodes its own screen); decode happens only when it acts as a viewer, against a peer it deliberately connected to (§4.4). So the in-process native-decoder RCE surface (Appendix C #2b) is the universal residual every full viewer carries — bounded operationally (connect only to peers you trust), not closable by keying, and not eliminated by any build (there is no build without the viewer). The fork SHOULD close it by running the decode path in a sandboxed low-privilege subprocess; until then it is a documented residual, the one place SSH still leads.
  • Codecs are CPU-only, policy-gated. hwcodec/vram and ffmpeg's hardware paths (amf/nvcodec/qsv) are compiled out on every build, so no GPU/VRAM is ever touched by construction. VP8/VP9 remain the supported software video codecs; AV1/libaom is not linked and is not advertised, selected, encoded, decoded, or exposed in UI (Appendix C #87/#88). The supported software decoder stays linked (a viewer needs it) but is never instantiated in the --server role.
  • The terminal is pinned ON by policy, not compiled out. The root-PTY is granted to the authenticated owner by the un-flippable enable-terminal=Y policy pin (R-S16), so the LoginRequest.Terminal arm is admitted (R-X8) — full control is the single, immutable mode on the sovereign shared-password box (the funnel still rejects every runtime write, so a password-knower cannot flip the policy either way); what stays excised is the separate second OS credential (os_login→PAM / Windows LogonUserW, R-X14/R-S18). Its code stays in the tree (§14; an operator who wanted a different policy edits the pin and rebuilds, R-F4).

Hardware-codec probe IPC is absent. Since hwcodec/vram/mediacodec are forbidden by the fork's software-codec policy, the old helper-probe channel is deleted rather than authority-wrapped: no CheckHwcodec/HwCodecConfig main-IPC message, no --check-hwcodec-config entry, no client/server hardware-codec config sync helper, and no app-source direct probe caller may remain.

"One strict mode" is structural by deletion, not by a profile: the surface an exposed box must not carry is gone from the tree (above), so R-A6's absence tokens for it are sound symbol greps on the one build; the viewer code that remains is held off the wire by R-A4's runtime socket-surface assertion and the unconditional policy, not by a compile-out. Per platform: the same one binary ships on Debian, Windows, and Android (R-R2). Android is a Flutter cdylib, viewer-dominant but capable of the controlled side, running the same hardened core (R-D7a). The clean serverless deployment is the Linux .deb run as a systemd --service (§17) and the Windows controlled service (R-D7b) — both the same binary as the operator's viewer, configured for the server role, not a separate artifact. Each platform produces one package (R-B1, §12).
R-R2aSHOULD One Linux package, install, and update model: the .deb + systemd
R-R2 ships "Debian x86_64," but the repo also carries AppImage (appimage/AppImageBuilder-x86_64.yml: self-contained, update-information self-update, no systemd) and Flatpak (flatpak/rustdesk.json: portal sandbox, --filesystem=home --share=network --socket={x11,wayland} --talk-name=org.freedesktop.Flatpak, no systemd), plus res/PKGBUILD and res/rpm*.spec. These are alternative install / update / sandbox models — the Linux analog of R-X9's MSI/EXE convergence: AppImage's self-updater collides with R-X1/§12 ("the fork ships its own releases"); Flatpak's no-systemd posture collides with R-D1 (a managed system service for unattended boot is the model); its own bubblewrap sandbox satisfies R-D3a, which is launcher-general. The fork SHOULD delete appimage/, flatpak/, and the non-Debian specs, leaving the .deb (built by build.py, persisted by res/DEBIAN/postinst through deb-systemd-helper/deb-systemd-invoke) the sole Linux install/update/persistence model. The runtime launcher must match that model: Linux child-process relaunch uses the current executable path, not an inherited $APPDIR/AppRun AppImage launcher fallback. Keeping either the package artifacts or the runtime launcher invites the drift R-X9 warns of.
R-R3MUST Make the dependency-advisory gate real, reproducible, and fail-closed
The fork's dependency posture is a gate, not a one-time hand screen. Remediate advisories by lockfile/fork bumps when the pinned Rust 1.75 build permits it; otherwise accept only by an explicit deny.toml ignore object carrying a reason. scripts/audit.sh is the Rust gate: it builds a digest-pinned Rust 1.75 audit image, installs the pinned CARGO_AUDIT_VERSION and CARGO_DENY_VERSION, bakes the pinned RustSec ADVISORY_DB_COMMIT, parses only [advisories].ignore TOML objects for cargo-audit --ignore flags, then runs both cargo-audit --no-fetch and cargo-deny check advisories --disable-fetch. Comment-only RUSTSEC mentions are never accepts. scripts/dart-audit.sh is the Dart/Pub gate: it runs pinned osv-scanner offline against flutter/pubspec.lock, with an explicit reason-bearing accept-list file that is currently empty. scripts/native-codec-watch.sh covers the vcpkg C/C++ codec set that Rust/Dart advisory databases cannot see; it is a ledger/sync gate for Appendix C #2b, not a claim that the accepted in-process decoder residual is solved. The full dispositions live in Appendix D, and verify.sh pins the fast structural shape so a future script edit cannot quietly drop a checker, stop sourcing pins, or reintroduce grep-based accepts.
TargetRust tripleCI job (1.4.7)Artifact
Debian x86_64x86_64-unknown-linux-gnubuild-rustdesk-linux.deb (+ systemd unit, §17)
Windows x86_64x86_64-pc-windows-msvcbuild-for-windows-flutter.exe / .msi (unsigned; SHA-256-pinned, R-B2)
Android aarch64aarch64-linux-androidbuild-rustdesk-android.apk (self-signed local key, R-B2)

Net structural delta from upstream: absorb the one hbb_common submodule in-tree, add libs/pake and rust-toolchain.toml, prune CI to three jobs, drop Sciter — nothing else. No new external dependency rides along (the PAKE's crates are already in the lock, §10.1), while the Sciter and ~8 Apple-only forks leave — so the fork's dependency delta is net-negative, and there is no new install step, service, or external-platform hook (§17 reuses upstream's systemd unit + .deb + CLI). Toolchain pins (R-B5a) — Flutter SDK, NDK r28c, vcpkg @ 120deac3, LLVM 15 — ride in the lockfiles/manifests. The helper bins (naming, service) stay; the --config key-override CLI is excised (R-X4); the 206 inherited tests (199 #[test] + 7 #[tokio::test]) stay green.

17. Deployment: serverless direct-IP on Linux (no central server, no Docker)

The controlled Ubuntu box runs the fork's own binary as a native systemd --service (R-R2b) and listens on one TCP port for inbound direct-IP connections, each gated by the PAKE (§10). There is no rendezvous account, no hbbs/hbbr, no relay, and no Docker — the "connect by address, nothing pre-configured" property (R-F3) with the sovereignty of a single owned port.

R-D1MUST Native systemd service; no container, no rendezvous dependency
Ship the verified unit (res/rustdesk.service: ExecStart=/usr/bin/rustdesk --service, Type=simple); the .deb postinst enables and starts it through Debian's systemd helper layer. The controlled side MUST NOT require any container runtime or reachable external server to accept a direct connection.
R-D2MUST The only runtime policy input is the permanent password — all else compile-time pinned
The hardened box has exactly one runtime security-policy input — the permanent password (the PAKE PRS, R-P1). Everything upstream exposes as a runtime --option is here a compile-time pin, not a knob: the direct listener is unconditional (it is the mode — start_direct_only, R-D4), the port is fixed (21118, R-F4), and the whole controlled-side security policy — verification-method = use-permanent-password, approve-mode = password, the capability set, egress-empty — is a compile-time table the Config::get_option funnel returns and the is_option_can_save guard rejects every write to (R-S16), so no resolver reads a permissive default and no local write can change it (R-X7, R-S9, AST-proven by R-A6). None of these is an effective runtime knob, so none can be flipped by a local process or an IPC write (R-S11). The single headless step uses --password (not --config, the excised key/server path, R-X4): 
rustdesk --password   # hidden controlling-terminal prompt; the sole runtime input = the PAKE PRS (R-P1)
A client then reaches <static-IPv4>:21118 with that password → the mutual PAKE → a keyed session. No second credential exists (R-X7); per-IP rate-limiting is in its clean, real-address case (R-S10). Provisioning authority follows the server owner, not the executable path. A user-owned --server (including a same-uid/rootless supervised deployment, R-D8) may accept --password over its own uid-scoped main IPC because the same account owns the credential and later enforces it. An installed service-launched --server is different: its unattended password/PRS is service-owned material, so ordinary Data::Config("permanent-password"), root-to-user UserMainIpcScope routing, whole-config sync, standalone salt reads, and password-storage/salt mirroring MUST NOT provision or copy it. The installed-service provisioning path MUST be a typed service operation that the privileged receiver validates and commits only after OS administrator authorization (polkit on Linux, service-admin/UAC proof on Windows, Authorization Services or a privileged helper on macOS). Until that operation exists, the legacy ordinary IPC path MUST fail closed for service-owned servers. Every other option a stock deployment once seeded into RustDesk2.toml (direct-server, approve-mode, verification-method, enable-hwcodec) is a compile-time pin (R-S16/R-R2b), so the permanent password remains the only runtime security-policy input.
R-D3MUST Account for every open port — one inbound TCP port, zero UDP, understood not merely allowed
The hardened box's entire inbound surface MUST be exactly one TCP port — the direct-server port (21118, R-D2) — and zero UDP; there is no LAN-discovery (21119, R-X5), no relay range, no ephemeral UDP (R-D4), and no second listener of any kind. Every socket the process binds MUST be enumerable and individually justified (asserted at startup, R-A4). "Secure even on a DMZ" is not a firewall claim: it holds because the binary itself cannot open anything else — R-D4 removes the rendezvous/LAN/KCP sockets, R-D5 binds the listener v4-only, R-D6 silences egress — so an operator's firewall merely agrees with a surface that is already minimal, rather than being the thing that makes it safe. The service runs as root (input injection, virtual display, and login-screen capture require it), so R-S11 least-privilege is bounded by that reality — which is precisely why the failure-mode-C excisions (§8: updater, plugin loader, config rewrite, deep-link writes, and — for the terminal — its genuinely separate second OS-credential, R-X14/R-S18) are load-bearing: a privileged, internet-exposed service must carry no fetch-and-run, re-home, or peer-supplied-credential escalation path. The terminal itself is pinned ON for the authenticated owner (R-F1/R-X8) — its root shell is the owner's granted capability, the bar being SSH (§2), not a failure-mode-C surface, which is exactly why only the separate second credential is removed. The host firewall/VLAN and any out-of-band management channel are the environment (a non-goal, §14); this requirement governs the fork's own surface, which must be self-evidently minimal whether or not a firewall is present. The complete socket-by-socket enumeration — every bind site, its original purpose, and the exact mechanism that neutralizes it — is Appendix B.
R-D3aSHOULD Confine the service with a launcher-provided sandbox that preserves the owner's privilege — never one the binary self-applies
R-D3 accepts that the service runs as root for the parts that need it (input injection, virtual display, login-screen capture) and that least-privilege is "bounded by that reality". The §8 excisions remove the reachable escalation paths; this shrinks the blast radius of anything missed. The kernel privilege/syscall sandbox itself — W^X, the capability-bounding set, and a seccomp allowlist — is the launcher's responsibility, and it MUST preserve the owner's full privilege: not NoNewPrivileges and not cap_drop: ALL, since each breaks the sudo/apt and raw-socket net tools (nmap/tcpdump/ping) the full-access owner legitimately runs (R-F1/R-D8 — the call the reference container deployment makes deliberately). The binary MUST NOT self-apply that sandbox, for two reasons. First, the --server hosts the owner's full-access terminal (R-F1) as an in-process child — terminal_service spawns the shell with portable_pty inside the --server, so the shell inherits the --server's prctl latches, which are one-way and cannot be un-set per-child; a self-applied PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS would propagate into that shell and break the owner's sudo — the very escalation the terminal exists to grant (the same reason a root unit must omit NoNewPrivileges). Second, it would override the deployment's deliberate, operator-visible confinement choice with a compiled-in latch the operator cannot relax. The blast-radius boundary is therefore the launcher's confinement of the service: under systemd, the unit's cap-drop + SystemCallFilter (minus the owner-breaking knobs); under a per-user supervisor or a plain container, the container's namespaces + kept-on default seccomp/AppArmor + resource limits + the absence of --privileged/docker.sock/host-network — there the blast radius simply is the container, the host protected by that wall. The only launcher-independent confinement the binary itself SHOULD apply is the resource bound — the R-T1(a) fd/session caps and the §4.2 per-session memory ceiling — because a resource bound shrinks the blast radius without confining the owner's privileges or syscalls; that is the line between what the binary may compile in and what the launcher must own. The shipped systemd unit (res/rustdesk.service) SHOULD carry, as the host model's sandbox layer: a CapabilityBoundingSet dropping caps the service never uses (CAP_SYS_MODULE, CAP_SYS_BOOT, CAP_NET_ADMIN, CAP_AUDIT_*, …); RestrictAddressFamilies=AF_UNIX AF_INET — dropping AF_INET6 (the kernel-policy twin of R-D5's v4-only bind) and AF_NETLINK; ProtectKernelModules, ProtectKernelTunables, RestrictRealtime, LockPersonality, SystemCallArchitectures=native, and SystemCallFilter=@system-service mount umount umount2 plus SystemCallFilter=~@reboot @swap; the full @mount group remains out. The explicit mount/umount/umount2 allowance is for the authenticated owner's root terminal semantics, not clipboard FUSE: Linux CLIPRDR/FUSE mounts through fixed absolute fusermount3/fusermount helpers using _FUSE_COMMFD, wraps the received /dev/fuse fd with fuser::Session::from_fd(..., SessionACL::Owner), rejects AutoUnmount/AllowOther, and owns unmount with a direct no-follow umount2() attempt plus the same fixed-helper fallback. Euid-0 RustDesk processes MUST refuse FUSE initialization before mountpoint setup, so a headless/root --server or root-launched viewer cannot turn file clipboard into a daemon-wide CAP_SYS_ADMIN mount path. Dropping CAP_SYS_ADMIN or mount syscalls from the long-lived service is a separate terminal-model split, not a clipboard dependency. Each is compatible with the root + input/display/capture reality and is the realistic least-privilege available beyond the excisions.
R-D4MUST The fork excises the rendezvous mediator — no phoning home, no pre-PAKE signaling, no ephemeral UDP
Verified gap: the R-D2 config does not by itself make the box serverless. With direct-server=Y and no stop-service, RendezvousMediator::start_all (rendezvous_mediator.rs) still runs its registration loop, calls test_nat_type() and lan::start_listening(), and get_rendezvous_servers() falls back to the hardcoded rs-ny.rustdesk.com (config.rs); stop-service disables the direct listener too, so no config makes the box serverless. The mediator keeps a persistent outbound connection, registers its pk, processes PunchHole/RequestRelay/FetchLocalAddr/ConfigureUpdate and the kcp-sys UDP parser pre-PAKE, and opens ephemeral UDP (the registration socket, STUN probes via test_ipv6→test_bind_ipv6, and KCP) — exactly the malicious-rendezvous adversary of §2. The fork MUST compile the mediator out of every build, so this surface is absent, not firewalled. Implementation precision (do not get this wrong): the direct listener direct_server/get_direct_port you must keep is nested inside the very start_all you are removing (spawned; defined) — a blunt #[cfg] over the module or over start_all deletes port 21118 with it. Instead lift direct_server — plus the ServerPtr it takes (from new_server(), server.rs), the genuinely-shared startup calls it relies on (check_zombie(), server.rs; AV1/libaom startup probing is deleted by Appendix C #87/#88), and on Linux linux_desktop_manager::start_xdesktop() (which start_all spawns when is_server() — the seat0/greeter capture-session discovery of R-S14/R-X14, not rendezvous; omit it and Linux capture never initializes) — into a minimal start_direct_only(), and compile-feature-gate only the registration loop, test_nat_type, lan, hbbs_http::sync, the KCP accept path, and the mediator's ~6 cross-module callers (ipc.rs, flutter_ffi.rs, ui_interface.rs, server.rs, the lib.rs re-export). Two verified subtleties: (a) removing start_all drops its probe calls, but it is not their only reach: the IPC handler also fires test_rendezvous_server (Data::TestRendezvousServer, ipc.rs) and test_nat_type (via the CheckTestNatType Drop guard at ipc.rs), and on the viewer/Android/iOS builds the flutter process entry fires both at startup (main.rs, flutter_ffi::initialize, ui_interface::set_option) — all independent of start_all. R-SV4(d) resolves this for every shipped artifact by making the entire test_nat_type/STUN subtree cfg-absent and deleting those callers, which turns R-A6's "absent" guard into a sound symbol-grep rather than the call-graph-only check a linked-but-unreachable probe would have demanded (a function that stays linked cannot call a cfg-absent one); the viewer-side probe callers (main.rs, cli, flutter_ffi, mod client) are unreachable from the service entry, so even before R-SV4(d) only the IPC reach (removed with R-S11, the socks/ws pin of R-D6 making the Drop trigger unreachable) remained. (b) kcp_stream is imported unconditionally by the viewer path (client.rs, KcpStream::connect), so removing the mediator alone makes "the kcp-sys parser is absent" true for the inbound accept surface but leaves the C parser linked for the viewer. The fork MUST therefore gate mod kcp_stream out entirely — the direct-only deployment connects by address over TCP in both roles, so KCP is needed nowhere — making the kcp-sys C parser absent for both roles, not merely on the inbound path (this resolves the one-binary-both-roles question of R-R2 for this build, rather than leaving it open). (Removing the rendezvous source entirely is a non-goal, §14 — the shipped binary compiles it out; this governs the deployment, not the source's existence.) This also removes the register_pk/key_confirmed liveness dependency, making R-P5's identity-key removal clean.
R-D5MUST Bind the direct listener v4-only; make IPv6 unreachability structural, not configorial
Upstream's tcp::listen_any (hbb_common/src/tcp.rs) builds a dual-stack socket (TcpSocket::new_v6() + set_only_v6(false) → binds [::]:21118, accepting v4 and v6), falling back to a v4 socket only if the v6 bind fails. On a static-IPv4 box that v6 face is attack surface with no purpose. The fork MUST bind the direct listener v4-only — add a listen_any_v4 (the existing v4-fallback body, ~6 lines) and call it from the lifted direct_server (R-D4) — so IPv6 unreachability is a property of the binary, not of a host sysctl or an ip6tables rule that can drift. This is "structural > config" applied to address families. Disabling host IPv6 and an ip6tables default-DROP remain sensible outer layers (environment, §14), but the innermost guarantee is the bind. Bonus: a v4-only box makes R-S10's entire IPv6-prefix limiter (connection.rs) dead code — retiring its hardest, highest-collateral-lockout subsystem and its /64 memory-amplification vector by construction.
R-D6MUST Outbound-silent by construction; the firewall is a tripwire, not the gate
Every fork build makes zero unsolicited outbound connections: the rendezvous registration/heartbeat, NAT/STUN probes, and hbbs_http::sync go with the mediator (R-D4); the fetch-and-run updater and its version-check go with R-X1; ConfigureUpdate/custom_server with R-X3/R-X4; and there is no live telemetry (Firebase is compiled out). Steady state: accept inbound on 21118, dial nobody. This inverts the usual posture — the binary is the primary control (it cannot phone home because the code is gone), and an operator's egress firewall is a defense-in-depth tripwire: a uid/cgroup-scoped OUTPUT default-DROP (v4 and v6) on the service account makes any outbound packet from the RustDesk process an IOC — a regression, or a supply-chain dependency that phoned home (the §3.1/§13 chain is large, so the dependency-advisory gates are release gates and the firewall remains an earned backstop). Verified hole to close so the invariant is unconditional: four audit/record POSTs survive R-D4 because they are config-gated, not compiled out — post_conn_audit (connection.rs, fired in on_open pre-authorization: after the fork keys the stream via the PAKE but before any password check, so it triggers on the mere keyed reach of any peer), post_alarm_audit (connection.rs, on rate-limit), post_file_audit, and session-record upload (record_upload.rs) — each silent only while the api-server option is empty/public, and each routable through an attacker set_socks proxy were api-server ever written locally (R-S11). The fork MUST hard-pin the api-server/audit/record endpoints empty (or compile these POSTs out) on the controlled build, so "the process makes no egress" is a structural fact, not a configuration accident. Two further egress paths the api-server pin does not cover: (1) the 2FA Telegram push POSTs a hardcoded https://api.telegram.org/bot…/sendMessage (auth_2fa.rs, leaking the box id + peer IP), fired on a 2FA-enabled login and gated on the bot/2fa options — not api-server, and not riding the mediator — so the fork MUST hard-pin bot/2fa empty and assert TelegramBot::get()==None (or excise send_2fa_code_to_telegram); and (2) enable-tunnel lets the authenticated peer make the box dial a host:port its PortForward names (connection.rs) — but that is a solicited, owner-driven egress, not the autonomous phone-home this invariant forbids: §17's CPace peer is the sovereign owner, who can already open a tunnel from the desktop (socat/ssh -R) with the shared sudo password, and SSH — the bar — grants port-forwarding after one auth, so enable-tunnel is pinned ON as part of full control (R-X8/R-S16; the tunnel still rides the sealed encrypted channel, R-S5). "Dial nobody" therefore means the binary initiates no unsolicited egress — telemetry, phone-home, probes — which the pins below keep structurally true regardless of the tunnel grant. And the local probe/MITM trigger: socks and allow-websocket MUST be hard-pinned (empty/off) and immutable — a written socks would re-route any surviving egress (R-S11), and toggling either is what flips CheckTestNatType's is_direct and fires the STUN ephemeral-UDP probe from the IPC Drop path (R-S11); pin them in the controlled-side policy table (R-S16) — proxy-url and allow-websocket are in PINNED_SETTINGS, so the read funnel returns them empty/off and is_option_can_save (config.rs) rejects every local override, including the set_socks write whose own guard already keys on proxy-url (config.rs) — so the pin MUST use the proxy-url key, not a key literally named "socks", or it will not block. This is strictly stronger than the signed-custom.txt/OVERWRITE_SETTINGS route, which a fork cannot sign. Either way the probe is then unreachable. The telegram push and the socks/ws pin MUST both be in R-A6's egress grep so the invariant is structural, not api-server-conditional.
R-D7MUST The direct-only hardening is per-target — Android and Windows ship it too, not just Linux
R-D2–R-D6 and §17 are written for the Linux .deb, and R-R2 says only "viewer and --server in each" — leaving silent whether the controlled-side hardening reaches the other two shipped targets. It MUST: the Android .apk and Windows .exe/.msi ship the same direct-only profile as the .deb — mediator excised (R-D4), v4-only (R-D5), egress-silent (R-D6), LAN discovery absent (R-X5), terminal pinned ON (R-X8). On Linux and Windows the exposed box runs the binary as a headless --service (R-R2b); Android (a Flutter cdylib) cannot be headless, so it applies the same controlled-side hardening as a viewer-dominant box (R-D7a). A server-assisted build on either would re-import the §2 malicious-rendezvous adversary (pre-PAKE signaling, ephemeral UDP, the ConfigureUpdate + TestNatResponse re-home twins, phone-home) on the platforms with the weakest sandbox guarantees — a split posture where two of three targets default to the un-hardened surface. Verified feasible: the Windows controlled path is the nearest twin to the .deb (same direct_server/listen_any, gated only by the direct-server option), and the Android controlled entry is literally start_server → RendezvousMediator::start_all() at one site (server.rs) — so repointing to start_direct_only() is a one-line substitution, cleaner than Linux (Android is a cdylib with no CLI arm to audit).
  • R-D7a — Android profile (service-owned, single-mode). Repoint the Android arm (server.rs) to start_direct_only(); delete the Android forced-LAN listener — rendezvous_mediator.rs hard-codes start_lan_listening = true for Android (a default-on MAC / ID / hostname / active-username / platform leak that R-X5's Linux-scoped lan.rs excision does not by itself remove); gate mod kcp_stream and mod lan out; v4-only. The R-X6 manifest hardening (exported components, DEBUG_BOOT_COMPLETED, allowBackup="false", the minimized permission set) is part of this profile. Service-owned listener + capture (teardown by construction). Android is a Flutter cdylib with no headless --service, so the direct listener and the MediaProjection capture MUST be owned by the mandatory foreground service and share its lifetime — MainService.onCreate starts the core (JNI startServer → start_direct_only → direct_server binds the one v4 port) and MainService.onDestroy MUST release the capture resources (ImageReader, Surface, retained VirtualDisplay, and MediaProjection) and then tear down the listener through a JNI stopServer that drives the accept loop's cfg(android) monotonic-generation teardown edge (the LISTENER_REBUILD_EPOCH counter R-T13 also uses for network-change rebuild: it sets listener = None and stops binding). A graceful Stop service closes the socket explicitly; an OS/battery/OEM kill closes it by process death, and onStartCommand MUST stay START_NOT_STICKY so no zombie auto-restart rebinds a listener the user stopped or re-enters capture outside a confirmed PAKE session (R-S14) — the "service stopped, listener still bound" half-state is unrepresentable. Reachable-as-server means the foreground service is running and a permanent password is set: the R-S9 park binds nothing while the password is empty (empty password ⇒ no bound listener), so Stop service is the OS foreground-service lifecycle, not a config write — the dead stop-service writes (the Dart main_stop_service/main_start_service and the JNI startService) are deleted (the listener reads no such option, R-D4; the key is pinned N, R-S16). Honest capability scope. Screen VIEW is attainable — a per-session MediaProjection consent tap (never auto-granted), the mandatory foreground notification (the honest controlled-status indicator, R-G7), Android-14 per-session re-consent and Android-15 auto-stop-on-lock, and a FLAG_SECURE window captures black to the viewer (a capture protection, not an input barrier). Remote INPUT is best-effort only — AccessibilityService gestures/taps (BIND_ACCESSIBILITY_SERVICE, R-X6), no arbitrary key injection for a normal app, gated behind the Android-13+ restricted-settings manual unlock (which fails outright on some OEM builds) and blocked at the lock screen — so reliable, unattended control across reboot or lock is not achievable for a sideloaded, non-Knox/non-MDM build. Android is therefore viewer-dominant in practice, a deployment fact consistent with R-F3, not a defect. The build SHOULD also pin the residual Android local modes to one form: keep-screen-on = during-controlled (excise the never/service-on branches) and excise the dead useVP9 capture branch (raw ImageReader the single encoder).
  • R-D7b — Windows profile. The same start_all→start_direct_only lift in the desktop start_server path; listen_any_v4 (R-D5); and carry R-S11's same-user-DACL tightening of the main IPC pipe, and the Windows run-mode / elevation / service-disable excisions (R-X9), as part of the profile.
  • Assertion scope. R-A4's startup socket self-check and R-A6's call-graph "mediator/KCP/LAN unreachable from the service entry" guard MUST run per target — including from the Android JNI startServer entry, a third service-path root alongside start_os_service/start_server.
R-D8MUST The Debian build preserves user-owned headless direct-IP automation; service-owned provisioning is privileged
The §17 box is the real-world serverless direct-IP pattern this fork is validated against: a headless rustdesk --server controlled side under a process supervisor, provisioned with the permanent password, reached at <ip>:21118 by Direct IP Access. The hardening MUST NOT break user-owned automation: a same-uid/rootless supervised --server owns its own credential, so rustdesk --password may set that credential over the uid-scoped main IPC and exit cleanly with no GUI. A service-launched installed --server is not that case; R-D2/R-S11b require service-owned password provisioning to use a typed privileged service operation and require the old ordinary IPC password write/storage-sync path to fail closed. The options such a deployment once seeded into RustDesk2.toml (direct-server=Y, approve-mode=password, verification-method=use-permanent-password, enable-hwcodec=N) are compile-pinned to the same-or-stricter values (R-S16/R-D4/R-R2b), so seeding them is a harmless no-op. The GUI remains opinionated per §19, orthogonally: editable server/port/approve-mode/codec/proxy controls are removed or pinned, while headless user-owned automation and service-owned privileged provisioning keep separate authority models.
Fit with the hardened host

This is the rebuilt DMZ box: a static IPv4, the fork's RustDesk on a single v4-only TCP port (21118), zero UDP, IPv6 off, no Docker, no rendezvous, and no unsolicited egress (R-D6). The fork is the application; the host firewall, static IP, IPv6 toggle, and any out-of-band management channel are the environment (a non-goal of this spec, §14). The load-bearing claim is that the box is defensible even with no firewall at all, because the binary's reachable surface is that one PAKE-gated port — the firewall is a redundant tripwire, not the thing keeping it safe.

18. Sovereignty: no phone-home, no auto-update (universal — every artifact, every platform, every code path)

§17 makes the exposed controlled box egress-silent (R-D6) and updater-free (R-X1), but for that one artifact and largely by config-pin — "silent only while the api-server option is empty," "pin bot/2fa empty" — historically leaving the code compiled and one local write away (R-S11's legacy proxy IPC showed that write was locally reachable). The operator's own devices, and the Apple code paths (§16, R-R2), would otherwise retain the lot. This section raises the bar to sovereignty: every running client or server reaches no global resource and carries no auto-update — by compile-out, not config-pin, on every artifact, and (per R-R2) enforced on the macOS/iOS source too. The only surviving network capability is the direct, PAKE-gated peer connection by IP (R-F3 as direct-IP, R-S13). Sovereignty becomes a property of the artifact and the tree, not a setting one trusts.

What the running app reaches today — the inventory the spec must drive to zero, tagged by who carries it

Verified by whole-tree sweep. Update (desktop · macOS source): the fetch-and-run updater.rs check_update — a server-supplied URL, content-length-only freshness, privileged --update/msiexec exec — with mod updater compiled in unconditionally. Version-check (ALL platforms incl. Android & iOS, default-on): do_check_software_update POSTs a device-fingerprinted request to the hardwired https://api.rustdesk.com/version/latest (hbb_common's version_check_request) — not the api-server option, so R-D6's pin never touched it — fired ~1 s after launch by the Dart checkUpdate. Rendezvous (all-but-iOS as responder; ALL incl. iOS as viewer): the mediator dials rs-ny.rustdesk.com (RENDEZVOUS_SERVERS) and the viewer's own ungated mod client Client::_start resolves a peer ID through it, probes Google/Cloudflare/Nextcloud STUN (STUNS_V4/STUNS_V6, reached from the viewer's test_udp_uat — not only the mediator), hole-punches, and relays (request_relay). Telemetry/account (POSTs all-but-iOS; Firebase iOS): four audit POSTs — one fired pre-authentication in on_open (post_conn_audit) — the admin.rustdesk.com sysinfo/heartbeat every few seconds (hbbs_http::sync), the api.telegram.org 2FA push that leaks the box's own id + the peer's source ip (send_2fa_code_to_telegram, built in send_logon_response_and_keep_alive), and a live iOS GoogleService-Info.plist (FCM creds + rustdesk.firebaseio.com). All ride modules with no cfg gate — mod server, mod auth_2fa, mod hbbs_http — into every artifact.

R-SV1MUST Sovereignty is a property of the artifact and the source — unconditional, not a feature
All of R-SV2–R-SV9 are unconditional — the fork is one build and sovereign by construction, enforced on every shipped artifact (Debian/Windows/Android) and on the unbuilt macOS/iOS cfg by the R-R2 cargo check pass. Every global-reaching path is deleted, not runtime-gated, so the binary cannot connect because the code is gone — generalizing R-D6's "the binary is the primary control" from the exposed box to every device and the Apple source. The only surviving network capability is the direct, PAKE-gated peer connection by IP (R-SV5).
R-SV2MUST Excise all auto-update and self-install — every artifact and profile (desktop · macOS source)
Generalizes R-X1 to the whole binary and the Apple source: upstream's mod updater compiles in unconditionally. Remove: updater.rs (its check_update — the server-URL download with content-length-only freshness and the privileged --update/msiexec exec) and its mod; the --update handlers in core_main (Windows and macOS); the Windows update_me/update_to/update_me_msi; the macOS root, quarantine-stripping update_me/update_from_dmg/extract_update_dmg in macos.rs (the R-R2 Apple obligation); the Flutter download-new-version/update-me/extract-update-dmg FFI keys and main_update_me; the Dart checkUpdate/UpdateProgress/handleUpdate UI; and the Windows auto-update thread updater::start_auto_update (deleted outright, not merely dropped with the mediator). R-R2a's AppImage update-information self-updater goes (raised to MUST). Re-adding update-checking later MUST verify a pinned SHA-256 before execution (R-B2), never fetch-and-run.
R-SV3MUST Excise the version-check egress to api.rustdesk.com (all platforms incl. Android & iOS)
Silenced by nothing today: do_check_software_update POSTs a device-fingerprinted request to the hardwired https://api.rustdesk.com/version/latest (hbb_common's version_check_request URL const) — not the api-server option — on launch, default-on (enable-check-update resolves through option2bool's "unset = on"), on every Flutter platform including Android and iOS (the Dart checkUpdate → main_get_software_update_url). A version-check is a global connection, independent of any download. Excise do_check_software_update, check_software_update, SOFTWARE_UPDATE_URL, version_check_request/VersionCheckResponse/its URL const, and the Dart checkUpdate/mainGetSoftwareUpdateUrl — all platforms; pin enable-check-update = N only as belt-over-suspenders.
R-SV4MUST No rendezvous, relay, STUN, KCP or LAN egress — both roles, all platforms (incl. macOS/iOS source)
Generalizes R-D4/R-D7 to every artifact and the Apple source, closing the half R-D4 never touched — the viewer's own stack in mod client. Compile out: (a) mod rendezvous_mediator entirely, after lifting the KEEP-listener direct_server into a standalone start_direct_only (R-D4) so the direct port survives; (b) compile out the viewer-side rendezvous/relay/STUN/NAT path in mod client — Client::_start's ID-resolution branch, the punch/relay arms, request_relay, test_udp_uat, udp_nat_connect — retaining only the direct-IP early-returns (is_ip_str/is_domain_port_str, re-keyed by R-S13); (c) compile out mod kcp_stream and mod lan for both roles on all targets — including mod lan's ungated viewer-side callers (lan::discover and lan::send_wol in ui.rs/ui_interface.rs/flutter_ffi.rs), which R-X5's listener-only excision leaves dangling and which would otherwise block the module's removal — note this drops Wake-on-LAN (the main_wol peer-card action → lan::send_wol, UDP broadcast magic packets), a deliberate, accepted loss in the same class as LAN discovery: WoL is LAN-only (moot for the static-IP §17 box) and orthogonal to rendezvous, so an operator who needs it on a LAN segment may retain send_wol in isolation; (d) make the whole STUN/NAT/rendezvous-probe subtree cfg-absent — not just the leaf probes (test_nat_ipv4, stun_ipv4_test/stun_ipv6_test, test_ipv6, test_rendezvous_server) and the STUNS_V4/STUNS_V6 arrays, but the entry probes that call them (test_nat_type, test_nat_type_, test_ipv6_sync), since a caller of a cfg-absent fn cannot itself stay linked. This is load-bearing, not bookkeeping: (a)+(b) do not remove every caller — the process entry fires these probes outside mod client and survives its excision. Delete the startup probe sites R-SV4(b) never touches: main.rs's flutter main (test_rendezvous_server() + test_nat_type()), flutter_ffi::initialize on Android (both) and on iOS (test_nat_type()), and ui_interface::set_option's CheckTestNatType::new() (its Drop fires test_nat_type) — the IPC-side twins go in (e). Two facts make this mandatory, not tidy: test_nat_type→test_ipv6_sync runs before the socks guard, so the R-D6 proxy pin does not suppress its IPv6-STUN egress; and test_nat_type_ is where the TestNatResponse.cu rendezvous-re-home (R-X3's second twin) lives — so this subtree's cfg-absence, not the mediator excision, is what removes that twin on the viewer/Android/iOS builds. With every caller deleted the subtree symbol-grep is sound; (e) compile out hbbs_http::sync and the IPC mediator-control surface (Data::TestRendezvousServer, Data::Deployed, the CheckTestNatType Drop guard that fires test_nat_type before any allowlist check, R-S11). The §14 "rendezvous code existence is a non-goal" carve-out is superseded for shipped artifacts; the three server-push re-home twins (R-X3) vanish — but by three different mechanisms, which a partial fix must not conflate: ConfigureUpdate with the mediator (a), TestNatResponse.cu with the probe subtree (d), and handle_config_options with hbbs_http::sync (e). The off-by-default webrtc ICE STUN set (DEFAULT_ICE_SERVERS) stays compiled out (CI asserts the webrtc feature absent).
R-SV5MUST Direct-IP only, all platforms — the viewer reaches a peer by IP, never by ID
The operator's decision, normative: with rendezvous gone (R-SV4) the only initiation is Client::_start's direct-IP early-return — connect_tcp_local to <ip|domain>:<direct-port> — routed through the CPace initiator handshake (R-S13/R-P14) before any Message parse. The early-returns run before any rendezvous code, so the direct path keeps R-F3 "connect by address." The cost is deliberate, not free: removing the mediator also removes the relay / NAT-punch fallback, so a peer behind CGNAT or an un-forwarded NAT is no longer reachable — R-F2/R-F3 ("from anywhere, any device", multi-machine) hold only for peers that present a reachable address or port-forward (the §17 box does; the operator's other machines must be made directly reachable too). This is the same sovereignty trade §17 already makes, now stated for every artifact: connect-by-address means connect-by-reachable-address. The viewer surface is more than the input box: the persisted peer list / recent-sessions / address book (a PeerConfig keyed by id and loaded by Client::_start) MUST be re-keyed to and shown as <ip|domain>:port entries that feed the direct-IP early-return, and the connect UI MUST reject a bare RustDesk-ID input (no resolver exists), accepting only <ipv4>/<ipv4>:port/<domain>:port — a positive property verified by R-SV10, not merely asserted here. The named widgets (the front-end work §19/R-G2 owns): the home buildIDBoard() "Your ID" board (desktop_home_page.dart, model.serverId), the numeric IDTextInputFormatter/formatID() (id_formatter.dart — today it space-groups a pure-numeric ID and strips a /r relay suffix, with no address validation), the connection_page.dart connect field + RawAutocomplete<Peer>, the peer_card.dart/address_book.dart stores keyed on PeerConfig.id, and the rendezvous status row (OnlineStatusWidget/mainGetConnectStatus, today rendering "Connecting to the RustDesk network…") all key on the deleted numeric ID and MUST move to the address model — so the requirement is actionable, not just a passing test. Connect-by-ID is removed on every platform; connect-by-direct-IP is the entire preserved surface.
R-SV6MUST Compile out the audit/record POSTs and the account/api-server family — not config-pin them (all-but-iOS)
Generalizes R-D6 from config-pin → compile-out, universal (+ Apple source): (a) reduce to a compile-time no-op the four POSTs — post_conn_audit (incl. its pre-authentication call in on_open), post_alarm_audit, post_file_audit, record_upload — so the reqwest sink is unreachable; (b) feature-gate out hbbs_http::sync (the /api/sysinfo + /api/heartbeat POSTs, carrying the R-X3 re-home twin), hbbs_http::account (OIDC), and hbbs_http::record_upload; (c) excise ui_interface's /api/devices/deploy (a residual the R-X4 CLI---deploy excision misses); (d) remove the default https://admin.rustdesk.com from get_api_server so it cannot resolve a global host — the silencing MUST NOT rest on is_public()'s hostname string-match. The api-server pin (R-D6) stays only as belt-over-suspenders.
R-SV7MUST Excise the Telegram 2FA push from every artifact (all platforms)
Strengthens R-X7/R-D6 (option-pin) to universal compile-out. send_2fa_code_to_telegram POSTs a hardcoded https://api.telegram.org/bot…/sendMessage whose text (built in send_logon_response_and_keep_alive) leaks Config::get_id() + the peer's source ip + the live OTP; get_chatid_telegram leaks the bot token. mod auth_2fa has no cfg gate, so it ships in every artifact, gated only by the runtime bot/2fa options (on iOS the leak site send_logon_response_and_keep_alive lives in mod server = cfg(not(ios)), so the Telegram code is present-but-dead there — excised for the zero-grep, not to close a live iOS egress). Compile out send_2fa_code_to_telegram, get_chatid_telegram, the api.telegram.org literals and the leak site — extending R-X7's mod auth_2fa excision tree-wide — so api.telegram.org is absent (Appendix C #18, made universal).
R-SV8MUST No Firebase / FCM / Google-services on any artifact (iOS source · Android)
Closes the unproven R-D6 parenthetical "Firebase is compiled out" and the iOS residual it misses. The iOS GoogleService-Info.plist ships a live API_KEY, GCM_SENDER_ID, IS_GCM_ENABLED and DATABASE_URL https://rustdesk.firebaseio.com — bundled at the Xcode/CocoaPods layer, invisible to cargo/cfg. Delete the iOS plist + any Firebase/FCM AppDelegate registration (verified: no Firebase/FCM pod ships in either Podfile.lock and the iOS AppDelegate.swift has no Firebase registration — so the Podfile half is a present-check, not a deletion: R-A6's per-pod allow-list asserts no Firebase/FCM/Crashlytics/analytics pod ever appears), and remove the iOS push capability FCM rides on: flutter/ios/Runner/Runner.entitlements ships aps-environment (the APNs remote-push channel) and com.apple.developer.networking.wifi-info (SSID/BSSID fingerprint) — entitlements-plist keys invisible to cargo/cfg that MUST be absent or explicitly justified (a sovereign build has no use for a remote-push channel); keep google-services.json absent on Android and apply the build_fdroid.sh gms/firebase strip to the standard gradle build, not only the F-Droid path; macOS is already clean. CI greps GoogleService-Info.plist · google-services.json · firebaseio.com · IS_GCM_ENABLED · firebase_ · crashlytics · aps-environment = zero (the full entitlements enumeration is R-A6's Apple pass). (Verified absent: Sentry/crash/feedback endpoints, and any HTTP external-IP discovery — public-IP discovery is STUN-only, closed by R-SV4.)
R-SV9SHOULD Neutralize the rustdesk.com / github.com/rustdesk UI links (all platforms, both front-ends)
~28 user-visible links to rustdesk.com/* and github.com/rustdesk/* are compiled unconditionally into both front-ends (Flutter settings/home/connection pages; Sciter index.tis/install.tis — the last over plaintext http://; plus the doc-URL constants in config.rs and lang/en.rs). They open a browser (no automatic egress), hence SHOULD — but a sovereign build SHOULD NOT advertise or route to rustdesk.com: delete the website/download/pricing/blog links and repoint docs/privacy to operator resources. The plaintext-http link MUST be fixed or removed regardless.
R-SV10MUST CI proves zero global egress per artifact; the runtime self-check covers every running process
Extends R-A6/R-A4 to every artifact (all platforms) and the macOS/iOS cargo check target, and tightens R-A6's egress clause from "unreachable or option-pinned" to compiled-absent for the single-purpose paths. Sound symbol-grep absent (per artifact): mod updater · do_check_software_update · api.rustdesk.com/version · download-new-version · mod rendezvous_mediator · register_pk · request_relay · mod kcp_stream · mod lan (incl. its lan::discover/lan::send_wol viewer callers) · test_nat_type/test_nat_type_/test_ipv6_sync · test_rendezvous_server · the CheckTestNatType guard · STUNS_V4/STUNS_V6 · the three public STUN hostnames · send_2fa_code_to_telegram · api.telegram.org · post_conn_audit/post_alarm_audit/post_file_audit · record_upload · admin.rustdesk.com · hbbs_http::sync/::account · firebaseio.com · GoogleService-Info.plist · rs-ny.rustdesk.com reached. One mechanism, not two: R-SV4(d) makes the whole test_nat_type subtree cfg-absent on every artifact and deletes its main.rs/flutter_ffi/ui_interface/ipc startup callers (the service, the Android JNI startServer, and the viewer main/flutter_ffi/mod client), so these probes move into the compiled-absent grep above rather than staying linked behind a "prove call-graph-unreachable" argument — a linked test_nat_type could not call a cfg-absent test_ipv6 anyway, so making the whole subtree cfg-absent is what keeps the grep sound. The call-graph check is retained only as defense-in-depth for the transitive-crate tail below, which can phone home under a symbol the first-party grep cannot enumerate. Runtime self-check (R-A4 generalized): on every running process startup asserts zero UDP sockets and one TCP listener. Positive checks (not just absence): a test MUST prove a bare-ID input is rejected and that no path from the connect UI or the persisted-peer reconnect reaches Client::_start's rendezvous branch (R-SV5). The fifth and sixth config funnels: R-S16's funnel pins leave two further namespaces untouched — HARD_SETTINGS (the sixth funnel + preset-password store, closed by R-S16(d)(v)/R-A4) and LocalConfig; the latter matters because enable-check-update resolves through LocalConfig::get_option (compiled out by R-SV3) — CI MUST assert no security-relevant key resolves through LocalConfig::get_option without a pin or a compile-out, mirroring R-S16(d)(iv)'s get_builtin_option treatment. Scope & the transitive residual (honest bound): the symbol-grep proves first-party egress is compiled-absent, but it cannot see a transitive crate phoning home under a different symbol, and this startup check sees only listeners (an outbound connect has no listener row) — so transitive egress rests on R-D6's uid/cgroup egress firewall plus the R-B4 smoke-test, which MUST watch connect() over a driven-session observation window, not a one-shot startup snapshot.
The sovereignty bar, in one sentence

No binary the fork ships — and no Apple build of its source — can phone home or self-update, because the first-party code that could is gone (the transitive-crate tail is held by the egress firewall + the connect() smoke-test, R-SV10); the only thing a sovereign binary reaches by design is the peer you point it at, by IP, through the PAKE.

19. GUI/UX conformance — the front-end must match the hardened core (client + server, per-platform)

§7–§18 harden the Rust core, the config funnels, and the build — but RustDesk's user-facing behavior lives in the Flutter front-end (desktop + mobile; Sciter is dropped, R-B6, so Flutter is the only front-end — verified: src/ui.rs and the .tis tree are #[cfg(not(any(feature="flutter",feature="cli")))] and every shipped target builds --flutter). Hardening the backend under an unchanged UI leaves three conformance hazards, each a distinct class: DEAD (a control whose backend §8/§18 removed — it does nothing), MISLEADING (a setting the funnel now silently overrides — the R-S12 "editable-but-inert" trap — or a security label the channel can no longer earn), and MISSING (the R-SV5 address model the new design needs but never built). This section makes the UI a conformance surface: every excised or pinned backend capability MUST have its UI surface removed (not greyed, not defaulted-off), the security-state and addressing UI MUST be rebuilt to the one keyed-and-direct model — with the same per-artifact/per-platform discipline as §18 and a CI hook (the closing box) that extends R-A6/R-SV10's grep set into the Dart, asset, and lang/ layers so a dead string or widget fails the gate.

The pattern this section closes

Verified by direct read of the pinned Flutter tree: the fork hardens almost entirely at the Rust/config layer — it pins options at the Config::get_option funnel (R-S16), compiles modules out (§8/§18), and rejects writes at is_option_can_save — but the Flutter UI is never touched. So dozens of controls survive as toggles the funnel now ignores, screens for features that no longer exist, an entire ID-centric home screen built on an addressing model the fork deleted (R-SV5), and a security badge that can still render "insecure"/"relayed" on a build where those states are impossible.

R-G1MUST No dead or inert control survives its excised or pinned backend — removed, not greyed
Every setting whose backend §8/§18 removed, or whose value R-S16 pins, MUST be removed from the UI, not left editable-but-inert. The hazard is concrete and verified: is_option_can_save (config.rs) silently rejects a pinned write, and the inherited UI's only response is the soft isOptionFixed grey-out (e.g. the kOptionAllowWebSocket toggle in settings_page.dart wraps its onToggle in isOptionFixed(...)) — the exact R-S12 "defaulted-off-but-present" pattern, now in the front-end: a control that looks like it changes policy but cannot (or visually flips then reverts, with no "set by policy" signal). The fork MUST delete — on every front-end — each widget whose key is in PINNED_SETTINGS or whose subsystem is compiled out; the specific surfaces are enumerated in R-G2–R-G8. The discipline is R-X7a's, generalized from the one Android selector it names to the whole UI: remove the surface, do not disable it. Where a pinned value must still be shown (a preserved content-capability state), it is read-only and labelled as policy, never an actuating control.
R-G2MUST Rebuild the ID-centric home screen on the direct-address model — the largest ripple (R-SV5) (D·M)
RustDesk's home screen is built around the device's numeric RustDesk ID and a rendezvous-registration status — an addressing model R-SV5/R-SV4 deleted. Verified surfaces that MUST change: the home "Your ID" board (desktop_home_page.dart buildIDBoard(), showing model.serverId) MUST surface the box's reachable <ip|domain>:port instead of a numeric ID; the connect box (connection_page.dart _buildRemoteIDTextField() / IDTextEditingController / RawAutocomplete<Peer>, and the mobile _idController) MUST accept and validate only <ipv4>/<ipv4>:port/<domain>:port and reject a bare ID (the positive property R-SV10 already mandates as a test); the numeric IDTextInputFormatter/formatID() (id_formatter.dart) — which space-groups a pure-numeric ID and strips a /r relay suffix, doing no address validation (a dotted IP/domain:port falls through unchanged, but the formatter neither validates it nor rejects a bare ID) — MUST be replaced by host:port validation; the connection-status row (connection_page.dart OnlineStatusWidget/_buildConnStatusMsg(), driven by mainGetConnectStatus) — which renders the literal connecting_status = "Connecting to the RustDesk network…" / not_ready_status — MUST be removed or repurposed to "listening on :21118" for the controlled side (there is no network to register with, R-SV4), and mainIsUsingPublicServer's public-server warning goes with it; the peer list / recent / address book (peer_card.dart · address_book.dart · peers_view.dart, all keyed on PeerConfig.id) MUST be re-keyed to and shown as address entries that feed Client::_start's direct-IP early-return; and the "Discovered"/LAN tab (peer_tab_page.dart DiscoveredPeersView, no lan.rs listener — R-X5/R-D7a) and the ID-sharing QR on the home board (dead with the ID — R-SV5) MUST be removed; the QR scanner (scan_page.dart) routes a scanned rustdesk:// deep-link to handleUriLink and a config QR to the server-settings dialog, so it is governed by the R-X6/R-X4 deep-link-and-config excision (a scan-to-connect-by-ID flow is moot under R-SV5). This is the front-end realization of R-SV5's "the viewer surface is more than the input box," named to the widget.
R-G3MUST Collapse the security-state iconography to the single keyed-and-direct state — a rendered "insecure"/"relayed" badge is a security mislabel (D)
The session badge is selected from the wire-derived secure×direct state — remote_tab_page.dart/view_camera_tab_page.dart load SvgPicture.asset('assets/${secure}${direct}.svg') from ConnectionType (shared_state.dart: strSecure='secure'/strInsecure='insecure' × strDirect=''/strIndirect='_relay'), giving four assets that all ship: secure.svg, secure_relay.svg, insecure.svg, insecure_relay.svg; and getConnectionText() (common.dart) returns one of four strings incl. "Direct and unencrypted connection" / "Relayed and encrypted connection". Under an always-on PAKE (every session is secure) and no relay (R-SV4), the "insecure" and "_relay" states are impossible — so a UI that can ever render them is both dead and dangerously misleading about the channel's actual guarantee. The fork MUST collapse to a single always-secure-direct indicator (or none): delete the insecure.svg/secure_relay.svg/insecure_relay.svg assets, the _secure/_direct state fields, and getConnectionText's three non-direct-encrypted branches. Misreporting channel security is worse than omitting it.
R-G4MUST Delete the removed-feature screens with their backends — not gate them behind a runtime isDisable* flag (D·M)
Each excised subsystem ships a UI surface that MUST be deleted, not merely hidden by an isDisable* runtime flag: the account / sign-in + OIDC flow (login.dart loginDialog()/mainAccountAuth/kAuthReqTypeOidc/queryOidcLoginOptions(), the auth-*.svg provider icons, user_model.dart UserModel, gated today only by isDisableAccount()) — account/OIDC compiled out, R-SV6; the 2FA section (settings_page.dart tfaTiles — Enable-2FA, Telegram-bot, trusted-devices — and the enroll-QR in dialog.dart) — R-X7/R-SV7; the one-time-password board (desktop_home_page.dart buildPasswordBoard2() — "One-time Password" + "Refresh Password" → mainUpdateTemporaryPassword(), already inert once showOneTime is false under the permanent-only pin — and the OTP length / numeric toggles) — R-X7; the "ID/Relay Server" editor (the Network-tab fields for id/relay/api-server and the trust-anchor key, desktop_setting_page.dart · mobile settings_page.dart "ID/Relay Server") — high-severity: editable server+key fields on a sovereign box are both dead and the R-X4/R-X3 trust-anchor-injection surface; the SOCKS/proxy editor (changeSocks5Proxy()) — a local-MITM primitive (R-D6/R-S11); the "Direct IP Access" toggle + port field (kOptionDirectAccessPort) — the listener is the mode and the port is a compile-time literal (R-D4/R-F4); the WebSocket / insecure-TLS-fallback / UDP-punch toggles (kOptionAllowWebSocket, allow-insecure-tls-fallback) — R-D6/R-SV4; the update banner + "check for update on startup" toggle (kOptionEnableCheckUpdate; the mobile _buildUpdateUI; UpdateProgress/handleUpdate) — already named in R-SV2/R-SV3, removed here at the UI; Change-ID — meaningless under R-SV5; and any plugin settings entry — R-X2; and the OS-credential UI (R-S18): the "Request Elevation" toolbar menu (toolbar.dart → showRequestElevationDialog + the elevationModel) and the OS-account login dialog that drive the viewer's os_login/ElevationRequestWithLogon senders — the client half of the second-OS-credential class — go with their senders. Each is removed at the widget, so a future build cannot re-expose it by flipping a runtime flag.
R-G5RETIRED Host-key-pin dialogs — retired with R-S17 (no net-new pin UI)
Withdrawn with the host-key pin (R-S17, retired). The fork is pure password-PAKE, so there is no first-connect seed prompt, no mismatch/re-pin warning, and no manage/forget-host view to build — the one net-new UI earlier drafts required does not exist. The inherited passive fingerprint surface it would have replaced (FingerprintState + "Copy Fingerprint" in toolbar.dart, and the About-page "Fingerprint:" line) shows a peer fingerprint that is never compared and now serves no ceremony, so it is dead UI and is removed under R-G1's general rule (remove the surface, do not disable it). The controlled box needs no headless fingerprint affordance (no --get-fingerprint); its sole runtime input remains the permanent password (R-D2). The ID is retired, not reassigned; R-G6/R-G7/R-G8 keep their numbers.
R-G6MUST Rewrite error/status semantics for direct-only — no relay fallback, and a clear "disabled on the peer" message (D·M)
Removing the relay (R-SV4) changes failure semantics, but the inherited UI still recommends the relay that no longer exists: the connect-failure guidance teaches relay fallback via relay_hint_tip ("…try connecting via relay … add the \"/r\" suffix … or \"Always connect via relay\""), the connect-box help id_input_tip teaches the <id>@<server>?key= / <id>@public / /r syntax, and peer_card.dart _forceAlwaysRelayAction() (kOptionForceAlwaysRelay) offers a per-peer "Always connect via relay" — all dead and misdirecting on an unreachable peer (which is now terminal, not "retrying via relay"). The fork MUST rewrite the failure copy (unreachable ⇒ "check the address / port-forward"), delete relay_hint_tip · the relay clauses of id_input_tip · web_id_input_tip · websocket_tip · the "Always connect via relay" action and its kOptionForceAlwaysRelay field, and the _wolAction() Wake-on-LAN card action (R-SV4(c)). Separately, a session type or capability a peer refuses (this fork's own controlled side now grants every capability — R-S16/R-X8 — but a viewer may still reach a stock or differently-built peer that disables one) returns a generic error today; the viewer MUST surface a specific "this capability is disabled on the peer" message, not a confusing connect failure.
R-G7MUST The controlled-side surviving UI is the password-mode accept + a single foreground notification — and the Android overlay/boot surface goes (D·A)
The exposed Linux/Windows box runs headless as a --service (R-R2b — the Flutter UI is present in the one binary but no GUI is shown), so its only "UI" is the tray / foreground-service notification; the attended desktop and Android front-ends still show a controlled-side surface that MUST conform. The connection-manager accept window (server_page.dart / server_model.dart) MUST drop the click-to-accept path (the approveMode != 'password' branch) — approve-mode is pinned password (R-S9/R-S16) — and MUST remove the per-connection permission chips for every pinned capability (terminal/record/block-input/privacy/restart included — Y-pinned full control, R-S16), since the runtime SwitchPermission widener is removed (R-S16(d)(ii)) so those chips imply a runtime permission change they cannot perform; the password-mode accept and the capability display stay. Headless CM has no display: verified, the --server spawns a connection-manager process per incoming connection (run_me(["--cm"]), connection.rs; on Linux it picks --cm-no-ui when the box is headless). That CM — the GUI --cm and the no-UI --cm-no-ui — lives in mod flutter (start_cm/start_cm_no_ui, flutter.rs), present in the one binary; the headless --service simply has no display, so it shows no CM window (R-R2b). So the headless --service MUST handle connection management without a CM window: under approve-mode = password the accept is automatic, so the per-connection CM spawn runs as --cm-no-ui and no X display is needed to manage a connection — only seat0 capture does. (--cm-no-ui is the no-UI CM the headless service uses; an attended desktop or Android controlled side may instead show the GUI --cm, R-X10.) On Android, R-X6 already cuts the FloatingWindowService + SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW overlay and the DEBUG_BOOT_COMPLETED broadcast; §19 adds the UI consequences: the Floating-window setting + its size/transparency/keep-screen-on sub-options (settings_page.dart) and the "Start on boot" toggle are removed (the latter's BootReceiver dependency on SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW is severed, re-homed on RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED alone, not left silently broken), the controlled-status surface collapses to the single non-configurable foreground notification (MainService.kt) — the honest controlled-status indicator, from which the in-app reachable-status is derived (R-D7a) — and the consent/onboarding ceremony (MediaProjection capture, accessibility, battery-optimization) MUST remain — it maps to the preserved controlled capabilities (whose honest screen-VIEW-works / remote-INPUT-best-effort scope R-D7a states) and START_NOT_STICKY keeps capture bound to a fresh PAKE session (R-S14) — but carries no account/login/ID step.
R-G8SHOULD De-brand the front-end — neutralize rustdesk.com links and the "RustDesk network" nomenclature (realizes R-SV9 at the UI)
R-SV9 makes link removal a SHOULD; §19 names the front-end specifics. The home/settings pages compile rustdesk.com routes unconditionally — desktop_home_page.dart opens https://rustdesk.com/download, the GitHub release URL, and three rustdesk.com/docs/en/client/linux/… permission/x11/login-screen help links; the en.rs constants carry more (powered_by_me = "Powered by RustDesk", the doc-URL strings). A sovereign build SHOULD delete the website/download/pricing/blog links and repoint docs/privacy to operator resources, and MUST fix the "RustDesk network" nomenclature that R-G2 already removes from the status row (and the Android notification title in MainService.kt) — the same de-sovereign-ization R-SV9 mandates, here at the widget and string level. Any remaining plaintext-http link MUST be fixed regardless (R-SV9).
The UI-conformance gate — extends R-A6 / R-SV10 into the Dart, asset & lang layers

§19 is machine-checked, not left to review: the R-A6/R-SV10 grep set MUST reach past the Rust tree into the front-end. CI MUST assert zero hits for the dead lang/ keys (connecting_status · not_ready_status · relay_hint_tip · the /r/@public clauses of id_input_tip · web_id_input_tip · websocket_tip · enable-2fa-title/enable-bot-tip · powered_by_me), the deleted assets (insecure.svg · secure_relay.svg · insecure_relay.svg · the unused auth-*.svg provider icons), the removed widgets/symbols (the numeric IDTextInputFormatter · buildPasswordBoard2's OTP path · the "ID/Relay Server" + changeSocks5Proxy editors · the verification-method/approve-mode selectors on every front-end · _forceAlwaysRelayAction/_wolAction · the update banner) and the rustdesk.com/github.com/rustdesk link literals — exactly as R-A6 already greps download-new-version/enable-2fa on the Rust side. CI MUST also assert the positive addition: the connect box rejects a bare ID (R-SV10). The Flutter UI thus stops being the one layer the spec hardened everywhere except.

20. TCP transport correctness, cancellation safety & DMZ resource hardening

§7–§11 secured the keying and the cryptography; this section secures the TCP transport mechanics the keyed channel rides on — the async accept/read/write model, cancellation (a future dropped mid-operation), stream-protocol packetization across the unkeyed→keyed boundary, connection lifecycle & shutdown (graceful, ungraceful, half-open, half-closed), and the OS-dependent readiness/cancellation semantics — on a root service exposed to the entire public internet (§17). TCP-server correctness is not a solved triviality: it is dominated by failure cases — cancellation of every kind, framing on a byte stream that has no message boundaries, graceful vs. ungraceful teardown, and select/epoll/kqueue/IOCP behaviour that differs per operating system. This section makes those explicit requirements rather than folklore. UDP is deliberately off (the NAT the client sits behind would block it, R-S13(d)), so the fork is committed to TCP and must implement it correctly, not naively.

The transport invariant this section enforces

The cryptographic core (§7–§11) is only as strong as the transport that carries it, so the standing invariant is: no TCP-layer manipulation — segmentation, injection, reset, partial delivery, coalescing, connection reuse, or a dropped/cancelled I/O future — may bother or infiltrate the keyed channel. The design MUST guarantee, and the §11 adversarial review (R-V1) MUST confirm, that on a public-internet DMZ box a network attacker cannot: skip or bypass keying (the mandatory CPace handshake is the only path and runs unconditionally; an unkeyed stream is refused — R-S1/R-A1); reach a plaintext or single-key fallback (no downgrade exists on the inbound path — R-S5/R-P11); induce a (key,nonce) reuse (fresh sid, scalars, keys, and counters per connection, with distinct per-direction keys asserted on the engaged cipher — R-P2/R-P10/R-A5, closing Appendix C #1); forge a frame or recover plaintext (every keyed frame is AEAD-authenticated and every desync/injection path fails closed); replay a captured handshake (a fresh responder sid_b/Yb diverges the ISK — R-P14c); or infiltrate bytes across the unkeyed→keyed boundary (R-T6). These are properties of construction: the requirements below are what make them hold and keep holding under refactoring. One OS-dependent subtlety is load-bearing and is pinned as a requirement rather than left to assumption — the async runtime's guarantee that dropping a TCP read/accept future consumes no bytes on any backend (epoll, kqueue, and Windows IOCP alike: the reactor performs a synchronous in-process recv/accept, with no kernel-owned overlapped buffer in flight) is the foundation the cancellation-safety requirements (R-T5/R-T14) rest on, and the fork MUST rely on it deliberately and documented, never re-derive it with a hand-rolled overlapped read that would break it per-OS. TCP-server correctness is not a solved triviality — it is dominated by cancellation of every kind, framing on a boundary-less byte stream, graceful vs. ungraceful teardown, half-open/half-closed states, and per-OS select/epoll/kqueue/IOCP semantics. UDP is deliberately off (the NAT the client sits behind would block it, R-S13(d)), so the fork is committed to TCP and MUST implement it correctly, not naïvely.

20.0 Implementation discipline — a transport control must not introduce a new failure

R-T0MUST Every transport control is held to the bar it enforces — no new DoS, no normal-path regression, no partial mitigation
Every requirement in this section governs an internet-facing, adversary-driven path, so each control MUST be implemented to the same bar it enforces: it MUST NOT introduce a new denial-of-service, a performance regression on the legitimate path, or a partial mitigation that merely relocates the problem.
