When used, the helper is launched by checked absolute CreateProcessW, waited with a bounded timeout, and its exit code is checked; ERROR_SUCCESS_REBOOT_REQUIRED is accepted as success-with-reboot and other nonzero exits fail the custom action. RemoveAmyuniIdd is Return="check", signals reboot-required state through WiX, and is scheduled only for uninstall/upgrade. The stale bare-netsh ShellExecuteW firewall helper examples and their commented reactivation path are deleted. verify.sh asserts the native status contract, complete-enumeration/not-present branch, MultiSZ hardware-ID scan, HRESULT propagation, checked helper fallback, reboot signaling, uninstall/upgrade scheduling, checked WiX return policy, and absence of the old async/stale-shell/result-discard shapes.
34Linux shared helper command provenance — after the root/service helpers in src/platform/linux.rs were hardened, the shared Linux helper crate still retained a generic public shell API, a find_cmd_path resolver that fell back to which and bare command names, a Flatpak host-spawn branch despite the .deb+systemd-only Linux model, PATH-selected crash-notification helpers, and a Linux service-uninstall delayed reopen implemented as sh -c "sleep ...; exec ...". These were not a confirmed local privilege escalation by themselves, but privileged/service code must not depend on ambient PATH search, generic shell strings, or shell quoting for helper provenance.FIX R-S11c-10m removes the stale shared shell API and the shell-quoting helper, resolves shared loginctl and notification helpers only from fixed absolute root-owned non-group/world-writable candidates, deletes the Flatpak host-spawn fallback from the shared helper, and replaces delayed service reopen with an internal argv-only --reopen-after-service-stop <seconds> mode that fails closed on malformed or out-of-range delay values. verify.sh asserts the fixed-path resolver, root/mode checks, absence of which/CMD_SH/run_cmds/shell_quote/flatpak-spawn, and the argv-only delayed reopen path.
35Linux headless CM uid lookup — the Linux headless connection-manager bootstrap waited for a discovered seat username, then resolved that user's uid by running a PATH-selected id -u <username> subprocess from src/server/connection.rs. The username was argv-passed rather than shell-interpolated, so this was not command injection, but root/service code deriving launch authority for --cm-no-ui must not depend on ambient PATH selection or an external account command when structured account data is already available.FIX R-S11c-10n deletes the id subprocess. The headless CM bootstrap resolves the username through hbb_common::users::get_user_by_name on a blocking worker and carries the returned uid into the existing launcher path. verify.sh asserts the structured lookup and rejects Command::new("id"), id -u, and the old uid_cmd path.
36Linux clipboard FUSE stale unmount provenance — the Linux unix-file-copy-paste FUSE clipboard path prepared its mount directories with no-follow ownership checks, then tried to clear a stale mount by running a PATH-selected umount program from libs/clipboard/src/platform/unix/fuse/mod.rs. The mount path is fixed under the fork's /tmp/<app>/cliprdr-* tree and was passed as argv, so this was not shell injection and not remotely chosen command text; the defect was that a service/root clipboard path used ambient executable lookup for a kernel unmount operation.FIX R-S11c-10o deletes the umount subprocess. Stale clipboard FUSE cleanup now builds a checked C string for the already-owned mount path, tries umount2(..., UMOUNT_NOFOLLOW), and falls back only to the fixed trusted fusermount -u -q -z -- helper path also used for normal teardown. Ordinary "not mounted" outcomes remain best-effort cleanup, while unexpected unmount failures are logged and the following fixed-helper mount remains the fail-closed mount authority. verify.sh asserts the no-follow syscall wrapper, fixed-helper fallback, NUL-path regression test, README disposition, and absence of the old PATH-selected umount command.
36aLinux clipboard FUSE root-process denial — with fuser's pure-Rust backend, spawn_mount2 first opens /dev/fuse and calls mount(2) directly, falling back to PATH-selected fusermount only after permission denial. The normal installed desktop path launches the serving --server as the active user through sudo -u, but the headless/root fallback and root-launched viewer/client cases would have run the same FUSE initializer as euid 0, turning file clipboard into a long-lived root CAP_SYS_ADMIN mount path.FIX R-S11c-10q makes Linux clipboard FUSE initialization fail closed in euid-0 RustDesk processes before mountpoint preparation or fixed-helper mounting. Non-root user-owned server/viewer paths keep file clipboard; root/headless RustDesk processes keep text clipboard and file transfer, but not CLIPRDR file-copy FUSE. FIX R-S11c-10r removes fuser's direct mount/PATH-helper abstraction from the Linux clipboard path: RustDesk resolves only fixed absolute root-owned, executable, non-group/world-writable fusermount3/fusermount candidates, passes _FUSE_COMMFD, validates the SCM_RIGHTS fd response, wraps the received fd with fuser::Session::from_fd(..., SessionACL::Owner), rejects AutoUnmount/AllowOther, and owns unmount plus thread join without fuser's panic-on-join path. verify.sh asserts the early euid gate, fixed-helper fd-passing shape, owner-only mount options, regression tests, hardening ledger, and advisory accept reason.
37Linux self-relaunch AppImage fallback — the shared run_me_with_env helper used by CM, whiteboard, tray and same-user service child launches still honored an inherited Linux $APPDIR/AppRun branch. In this fork, Linux is a .deb + systemd deployment and the AppImage package model is excised; a service-owned relaunch helper must not select a launcher path from ambient process environment when the current executable is the authority-bearing object already running.FIX R-S11c-10p deletes the APPDIR/AppRun runtime branch. run_me_with_env now relaunches only std::env::current_exe() while preserving explicit caller-provided environment variables for CM and whiteboard authority tokens. verify.sh asserts the current-executable relaunch shape and rejects any reintroduced APPDIR/AppRun fallback in src/common.rs, alongside the R-R2a non-.deb packaging gate.
38Windows runtime process command provenance — service-adjacent runtime paths in src/platform/windows.rs still selected process authority through ambient or heuristic mechanisms: is_process_consent_running ran cmd /C tasklist | findstr consent.exe, server startup cleanup ran cmd /c taskkill /F /IM RuntimeBroker_rustdesk.exe, and the service-start IPC bind-failure fallback tried to close the endpoint occupant and then terminate a basename/argv-matched "main window" process through NtTerminateProcess after opening it with PROCESS_ALL_ACCESS. The command text and process names were fixed, so this was not peer command injection, but runtime service code should not depend on ambient shell lookup, shell pipelines, or guessed process identity for process-state decisions.FIX R-S11d-3 replaces the live probe/cleanup sites with native process APIs and deletes the IPC bind-failure process-control fallback. Runtime process lookup now enumerates exact executable names through CreateToolhelp32Snapshot, Process32FirstW, and Process32NextW, closes Windows handles with a local RAII guard, and terminates stale broker processes through OpenProcess(PROCESS_TERMINATE) plus TerminateProcess. If the server cannot bind its IPC endpoint, it logs the occupied endpoint and exits fail-closed instead of sending Data::Close or killing a guessed process. verify.sh asserts the native enumeration/termination shape, the consent.exe and broker call sites, absence of the old shell probes in the runtime blocks, and absence of the old main-window process-kill fallback.
39Windows MSI runtime-generated executable cleanup completion authority — the MSI RemoveRuntimeGeneratedFiles deferred non-impersonated custom action was scheduled on uninstall/upgrade to remove RuntimeBroker_rustdesk.exe from the installed Program Files directory, but it discarded the deletion result, treated an empty or root install folder as a successful skip, and declared the WiX action Return="ignore". A privileged cleanup action that owns an installed executable payload must either prove the payload is absent/deleted or fail closed; silent continuation can leave stale executable state behind after an update or uninstall.FIX R-S11d-4 makes runtime-generated executable cleanup checked. Empty install-folder data and root install-folder targets are fatal packaging errors; the broker cleanup requires DeleteRuntimeGeneratedFile(...) to succeed, where absent files remain a successful no-op and directories/reparse-target hazards remain refused by the existing handle-based no-follow deletion path; and RemoveRuntimeGeneratedFiles is declared Return="check". The scheduled no-op CustomActionHello sample action is deleted from the MSI surface. verify.sh asserts the checked delete result, checked WiX return policy, absence of the old ignored return, and absence of the sample custom action.
40Windows EXE elevated batch command provenance — after the EXE installer resolved the outer elevated cmd.exe through GetSystemDirectoryW and protected the generated command file while it was consumed, the privileged batch body still invoked external tools by bare name: reg, netsh, sc, taskkill, cscript, XCOPY, chcp, timeout, and prior-MSI MsiExec.exe handoffs. A user consent to RustDesk's installer must not let the elevated shell resolve service, registry, firewall, process, shortcut, or copy tools from the caller's current-directory/PATH state.FIX R-S11d-5 treats the elevated EXE batch body itself as a privileged process-launch surface. The installer resolves chcp.com, cscript.exe, msiexec.exe, netsh.exe, reg.exe, sc.exe, taskkill.exe, timeout.exe, and xcopy.exe from GetSystemDirectoryW, requires each file to exist, quotes the absolute path, and threads that trusted tool set through install, uninstall, service install/uninstall, broker update, service creation, shortcut-script execution, registry/firewall/SAS setup, prior-MSI uninstall, and bulk-copy fragments. Prior-MSI uninstall does not replay registry command text: R-S11d-35 validates and reconstructs it from a product code and Windows Installer product-name proof. verify.sh asserts the resolver/tool set, prior-MSI reconstruction/proof, raw uninstall-string replay absence, and absence of bare external tool command lines in the elevated batch surface.
41Windows EXE shortcut finalization provenance — even after cscript.exe itself was bound to System32, the EXE installer still created predictable shortcut files under the caller's temp directory, then elevated-copied those .lnk files into all-users locations such as Public Desktop, Common Programs, Common Startup, and the Program Files install directory. A local same-user process must not be able to race or pre-shape caller-writable shortcut staging that the elevated installer later promotes into protected or all-users persistence locations.FIX R-S11d-6 removes temp .lnk staging from the elevated EXE install/service-install path. The installer resolves final shortcut roots with SHGetKnownFolderPath for FOLDERID_PublicDesktop, FOLDERID_CommonPrograms, and FOLDERID_CommonStartup, writes VBS command files that call WScript.Shell.CreateShortcut on the final protected destination directly, rejects unsafe quote/CR/LF script literals, and treats any shortcut-script failure as an elevated batch failure. verify.sh asserts the known-folder roots, direct final-path helper, checked cscript.exe runner, final shortcut call sites, and absence of temp .lnk staging/copy leftovers.
42Windows MSI firewall custom-action completion authority — AddFirewallRules and RemoveFirewallRules were deferred non-impersonated MSI custom actions that modified privileged Windows Firewall policy, but WiX declared them Return="ignore", the custom-action DLL discarded the helper result, add overwrote the inbound-rule result with the outbound-rule result, and remove issued two blind removes while overwriting the first result. MSI could therefore record install/uninstall success while firewall policy was only partially changed or stale rules remained.FIX R-S11d-7 makes firewall policy completion authoritative. WiX declares both actions Return="check"; the entry point validates the mode byte and non-empty executable path, uses string-copy rather than format-string copy for rule-name derivation, propagates the helper HRESULT, and fails MSI on helper failure. The helper removes existing same-name rules before add, requires both inbound and outbound rule creation to succeed, best-effort removes partial state before returning add failure, and removes by bounded Item/Remove loops until absence is proven. Already-absent rules are success; COM/policy/query/remove failures are fatal. verify.sh asserts checked WiX returns, HRESULT propagation, invalid-data rejection, absence of discarded helper results and format-string copies, bounded remove-until-absent semantics, and absent-rule no-op HRESULTs.
43Windows RDP viewer credential command provenance — the viewer-side RDP tunnel convenience launched cmdkey and mstsc by ambient executable search while passing the saved RDP password to cmdkey as an argument, and it left the seeded credential behind after the RDP client was started. This was not a service/SYSTEM escalation path: the action is local viewer-side and same-user. It was still the wrong credential-handling model for a hardened fork, because a same-user launch directory/PATH substitution could observe the RDP credential or replace the intended local Microsoft tools, and process-global environment plumbing made concurrent local RDP launches race over the saved credential values.FIX R-S11d-8 removes cmdkey from the RDP helper and uses native Windows Credential Manager APIs. mstsc.exe is resolved through the same GetSystemDirectoryW-based trusted-tool resolver used by the Windows installer hardening. The helper passes RDP username/password as in-process data, never through rdp_password environment variables or /pass: argv; writes a session-scoped generic TERMSRV/localhost credential with CredWriteW only when both username and password are present; snapshots any pre-existing credential with CredReadW; serializes seeded launches with a process-local lease; prompts mstsc when no complete credential was seeded; and restores the previous credential state or deletes the temporary credential after mstsc exits or if the launch fails. verify.sh asserts trusted mstsc resolution, absence of cmdkey and bare RDP command launch, absence of password argv/env plumbing, native CredReadW/CredWriteW/CredDeleteW, session-scoped generic credential policy, lease/drop restoration, prompt fallback, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
44Windows terminal default-shell command provenance — the Windows terminal helper selected its default shell from a current-directory pwsh.exe candidate, then from Windows PowerShell, then from the caller's COMSPEC environment variable or a bare cmd.exe fallback. The terminal feature intentionally grants an authenticated terminal user a shell, and installed-service mode runs the helper/shell as the current logon user rather than SYSTEM. The defect was narrower but still real: a remote-triggered terminal-open path must not choose the shell executable from caller current-directory or environment state.FIX R-S11d-9 makes Windows terminal default-shell selection absolute-only and fail-closed. The helper resolves System32 with GetSystemDirectoryW, tries only C:\Program Files\PowerShell\7\pwsh.exe, C:\Program Files\PowerShell\6\pwsh.exe, System32\WindowsPowerShell\v1.0\powershell.exe, and System32\cmd.exe, and returns an error if no trusted shell is present. Both the helper ConPTY path and the direct terminal path propagate that error instead of launching a bare fallback. verify.sh asserts fallible shell resolution, the trusted candidate set, absence of COMSPEC/bare pwsh.exe/bare cmd.exe fallback, fail-closed propagation, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
45Windows portable RuntimeBroker cleanup command provenance — the Windows portable launcher killed a stale RuntimeBroker_rustdesk.exe with Command::new("taskkill") before copying the runtime broker payload. The arguments were fixed and the cleanup is local same-user portable-launch/install behavior, not remote-triggered and not service-owned. If a user deliberately starts the portable installer elevated, however, the cleanup process inherits that approved local elevation; the launcher must not let that process resolve through caller current-directory/PATH state.FIX R-S11d-10 resolves taskkill.exe from GetSystemDirectoryW, checks that the resolved path is a file, and uses that absolute path without any ambient fallback. Cleanup remains best-effort because an absent stale broker process is acceptable; resolver/spawn failures are reported and the copy path continues as before. verify.sh asserts trusted taskkill.exe resolution, GetSystemDirectoryW use, absence of bare taskkill launch, reported spawn errors, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
46Windows unsupported 32-bit WMIC process-probe deletion — inherited non-x64 Windows process helpers kept a wmic.exe fallback for command-line process probes because 32-bit Windows builds could not reliably read process command lines through the structured process snapshot path. This fork's release matrix ships Windows x86_64 only; retaining an unsupported compatibility branch that starts an external process-query tool is a wrong provenance surface, even if it is not in the live release artifact.FIX R-S11d-11 deletes the unsupported WMIC branch instead of hardening it. The Windows non-64-bit detours in check_process and the shared platform process helpers are gone; get_pids_with_args_by_wmic, get_pids_with_first_arg_by_wmic, get_pids_with_first_arg_check_session, their parsers, and their parser tests are removed. Non-mobile process probes use the structured sysinfo enumeration path only. verify.sh rejects any reintroduced WMIC helper, by_wmic helper, or Windows non-64-bit process-probe cfg detour under src/common.rs / src/platform, and asserts this requirements/ledger disposition.
47Service-owned voice-call input IPC mutation — Data::SetVoiceCallInput was a typed main-channel operation, but it was still admitted unconditionally by the desktop IPC mutation policy. On a service-owned receiver this let an ordinary local main-IPC peer mutate service-owned runtime audio-selection state through the same generic channel whose credential and policy writes had already been receiver-scoped. This is not a remote protocol path and not a root shell by itself; it is the remaining service-owned main-channel state mutation that was not tied to receiver ownership.FIX R-S11c-14 gates Data::SetVoiceCallInput through MainIpcAuthority::allows_main_channel_voice_call_input_write, which admits user-owned receivers only. Service-owned receivers now reject the operation on Linux, macOS, and Windows main IPC; the typed operation remains available for user-owned desktop receivers. verify.sh asserts the authority helper, the gated policy arm, and absence of the old unconditional Data::SetVoiceCallInput(_) => true arm.
48Local credential-bearing store file hardening — per-peer config load used a direct confy::load_path(Self::path(id)) call rather than the fork's hardened config wrapper, and address-book/group stores wrote raw encrypted compressed payloads with direct File::create plus ignored write_all errors. Those stores carry connect-relevant local material such as per-peer saved credentials and address-book/group access tokens. The address-book/group files are raw encrypted byte payloads, not TOML; changing them to the TOML config writer would silently change their on-disk format.FIX R-S11b-4d routes PeerConfig::load through a typed hardened loader so Windows ACL preparation, corrupt-config preservation, and recovery-file hardening apply, and peer enumeration removes a peer file only after a successful load of that exact enumerated file as a semantically default PeerConfig, never after loading a different canonicalized peer path, never after transient unreadable/corrupt/default status, and never when saved options or connect-equivalent credentials are present. Address-book and group persistence keep the existing encrypted raw-byte format, but write through a raw temp-and-replace helper that prepares the Windows config ACL, writes owner-only 0600 files on Unix, uses MoveFileExW with replace-existing/write-through semantics on Windows, hardens the final Windows file, and logs store/load/remove failures. Present-but-unreadable or corrupt TOML/raw encrypted payloads are preserved as sibling recovery files instead of being deleted; recovery files are immediately rehardened through the same helper, using O_NOFOLLOW plus descriptor fchmod(0600) on Unix and the protected config DACL on Windows. verify.sh asserts the typed peer-config load status, exact-path loaded-and-semantically-default empty-peer cleanup, raw-byte helper shape, Windows ACL preparation, Unix owner-only mode, replace-existing/write-through primitive, corrupt-payload preservation and hardening, absence of direct File::create(Self::path())/write_all(...).ok() in these stores, and the raw/TOML recovery permission, symlink-rejection, replacement, transient-load, RDP-password, and alias-path cleanup-policy regression tests.
49Windows privacy broker and user shortcut process provenance — privacy mode launched RuntimeBroker_rustdesk.exe with CreateProcessAsUserW using a null lpApplicationName and a path-only mutable command line, leaving Windows module-name parsing to the command-line string. Separately, the user-facing "create shortcut" action generated a VBScript containing the peer id/executable path and launched cscript to create a Desktop shortcut. The privacy broker path can be remote-triggered after authentication through privacy mode; the shortcut path is same-user UI convenience, but it still should not create script literals or depend on script-host execution for a native shell-link operation.FIX R-S11d-12 binds the privacy broker executable as the explicit lpApplicationName, passes a null command line, verifies the broker and injection DLL are files, and supplies the broker directory as the current directory. The user shortcut path now validates the direct connect id, resolves the current executable and Desktop known folder, and creates the shortcut through native COM IShellLinkW/IPersistFile rather than per-shortcut VBScript or cscript. verify.sh asserts explicit broker application-name launch, absence of the old command-line module parsing variable, native ShellLink shortcut creation, id validation, Desktop known-folder use, absence of script-backed shortcut code in create_shortcut, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
50Windows service and session-token process launch provenance — the installed LocalSystem service and root/session handoff helpers launched --server, --tray, connection-manager, and whiteboard children through a single mutable command-line string while passing null lpApplicationName to CreateProcessAsUserW. That left executable identity to Windows command-line module parsing on the service-owned privilege transition and on token-switched user-session launches.FIX R-S11d-13 makes the process-launch API carry the executable path separately from argv text and carries an explicit current directory derived from that executable. Token-switched launches now require an absolute existing executable file, pass that path as the explicit lpApplicationName, pass the executable parent as lpCurrentDirectory, build argv with Windows command-line quoting, reject NUL-bearing application/current-directory/argv/environment data, and use the command line only as argument text. The obsolete preformatted command-string wrapper is gone. verify.sh asserts the explicit application-name/current-directory FFI shape, absence of null-application CreateProcessAsUserW and fixed MAX_PATH command buffers, absolute-file validation, quoted argv construction, the service-owned --server launch through the new helper, user-session launch reuse of the same helper, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
51Windows service/session token source provenance — the token helper for service-owned and token-switched process launches selected authority by enumerating processes and accepting the first same-session basename match: explorer.exe for user launches, winlogon.exe for service-owned launches, with a sihost.exe username fallback. The selected process was opened with PROCESS_ALL_ACCESS and its token with TOKEN_ALL_ACCESS. A process basename is not an authority proof for a LocalSystem service deciding which token to hand to CreateProcessAsUserW.FIX R-S11d-14 replaces user-token process hunting with WTSQueryUserToken for the target session. The service-owned SYSTEM path accepts only same-session winlogon.exe after validating its full image path against %SystemRoot%\System32\winlogon.exe, validating the token session, and validating the token user as LocalSystem. The launcher requests minimum process/token rights for query and CreateProcessAsUserW rather than all-access handles, deletes the Explorer/sihost fallback path, and removes the old Explorer-name error suppression. verify.sh asserts the WTS user-token source, trusted winlogon image/SID/session validation, minimum handle rights, absence of basename user-token fallbacks, absence of launcher all-access token opens, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
52Windows EXE elevated batch completion accounting — the EXE install/uninstall/service-install path launched generated .bat files through elevated cmd.exe, but the wrapper discarded the elevated process exit status and relied only on a best-effort .undone marker. The marker itself was created with ignored errors, and the Windows install_service / uninstall_service wrappers returned success after run_cmds failed. A privileged installer wrapper must not report success merely because the elevated shell started.FIX R-S11d-15 makes the completion marker mandatory, then requires both a successful elevated process exit status and removal of the marker before run_cmds returns success. Leftover markers are deleted before returning failure. Windows service install/uninstall now report false when the elevated command fails instead of converting failure into success. verify.sh asserts mandatory marker creation, elevated status checking, marker checking, absence of the ignored-status shape, service wrapper failure reporting, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
53Windows EXE elevated batch cmd-state hardening — the EXE install/uninstall/service-install path had been narrowed to trusted System32\cmd.exe, protected generated command files, checked exit status, and mandatory completion markers, but the elevated shell still started as cmd.exe /C and still consumed generated batch/marker paths rooted in the caller temp directory when that directory contained expansion-sensitive text. A medium-integrity caller's HKCU Command Processor AutoRun state or a generated path containing %, delayed-expansion, control, quote, or shell metacharacters must not affect elevated installer command execution.FIX R-S11d-18 makes the elevated EXE batch runner invoke trusted cmd.exe with /D /V:OFF /S /C, validates generated batch and completion-marker path literals against quote, percent, delayed-expansion, shell metacharacter, CR/LF, and control characters, tries only validated command-file directories in order (caller temp, ProgramData, then the existing user-accessible folder), continues to the next safe candidate when file creation fails, and quotes completion-marker deletion through the same safe path guard. verify.sh asserts the cmd.exe argument shape, path-literal guard, rejected character set, safe temp/ProgramData/user-accessible candidate handling, create-error tracking, marker quoting, absence of the old bare /C invocation, and this requirements/ledger disposition. R-S11d-19 separately closes the env-expanded uninstall cleanup roots, R-S11d-20 separately closes elevated batch command postconditions, and R-S11d-21 separately closes the MSI CC_CONNECTION_TYPE public-property gate.
54Windows EXE uninstall cleanup known-folder authority — the EXE uninstall and service-uninstall elevated batches deleted all-users shell artifacts through %ProgramData%, %PROGRAMDATA%, and %PUBLIC% expansions, and get_install_info still carried a stale %ProgramData%\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu\Programs\... start-menu path that fed uninstall cleanup. The cleanup targets are protected shell folders; their authority must come from Windows known-folder resolution, not from the elevated process environment.FIX R-S11d-19 removes the stale start-menu field from get_install_info, adds shared fallible helpers for the Public Desktop app shortcut, Common Programs app folder, and Common Startup tray shortcut, makes get_uninstall fallible, quotes all shell-folder cleanup targets through the elevated batch path guard, propagates known-folder/quoting failures to install and uninstall callers, and makes service uninstall resolve and quote the Common Startup tray shortcut before composing the elevated command. verify.sh asserts the narrowed install-info tuple, fallible uninstall builder/callers, known-folder quoted cleanup paths, service-uninstall cleanup path, absence of %ProgramData%/%PROGRAMDATA%/%PUBLIC% roots in src/platform/windows.rs, and this requirements/ledger disposition. R-S11d-20 separately closes elevated batch command postconditions; R-S11d-21 separately closes the MSI CC_CONNECTION_TYPE public-property gate; R-S11d-36 separately closes non-MSI install-directory cleanup authority.
55Windows EXE elevated batch command postconditions — the EXE install/update/uninstall/service-install/service-uninstall batches had authoritative wrapper-level completion after R-S11d-15, trusted tools after R-S11d-5, and ambient cmd.exe hardening after R-S11d-18, but required commands inside the batch body could still fall through to marker deletion after an ignored failure. Cleanup commands also mixed best-effort absence with persistent privileged state: service, registry, firewall, install-directory, and all-users shortcut deletion must succeed or prove the target is absent before the elevated batch reports success.FIX R-S11d-20 makes required install/update operations fail fast and verifies file/directory shortcut postconditions: broker and install payload copies check destination files, xcopy /C is removed, install-directory creation checks final existence, registry and firewall additions are checked for command failure, shortcut script execution checks the final .lnk, and service creation/failure/start keeps checked sc exit handling. Uninstall cleanup uses absent-tolerant helpers that verify absence for service deletion, HKCR/HKLM key deletion, firewall rule deletion, install-directory removal, Start Menu removal, Public Desktop shortcut removal, and Common Startup tray-shortcut removal. Prior MSI uninstall delegation is reconstructed by R-S11d-35 before it enters the batch, then checks the delegated msiexec exit status and accepts only success, reboot-required 3010, and product-absent 1605. R-S11d-21 separately closes the MSI CC_CONNECTION_TYPE public-property service-mode gate, R-S11d-22 separately closes EXE certificate-cleanup completion, and R-S11d-23 separately closes EXE Amyuni IDD cleanup completion. The MSI Amyuni cleanup authority is R-S11d-2/R-S11d-7. verify.sh asserts the fail-fast helpers, existence/absence postcondition helpers, checked copy/update/install/uninstall/service call sites, removal of xcopy /C, reconstructed MSI uninstall exit handling, absence of the raw uninstall-string fallback, absence of raw install-dir create/service-delete/uninstall-registry-delete leftovers, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
56Windows MSI service-mode package authority — the MSI custom-client preprocessor could emit a public CC_CONNECTION_TYPE property, and the package used CC_CONNECTION_TYPE="outgoing" to suppress the deferred non-impersonated CreateStartService action, tray launch, and startup tray shortcut. A public MSI property or transform is runtime caller/package-input state; it must not decide whether a per-machine package creates and starts the LocalSystem unattended service.FIX R-S11d-21 deletes the MSI --conn-type preprocessor argument and CC_CONNECTION_TYPE property generator, removes all CC_CONNECTION_TYPE conditions from the MSI package, and makes service creation/start, tray launch, and startup tray shortcut installation follow package policy rather than caller connection type. verify.sh rejects CC_CONNECTION_TYPE, --conn-type, conn_type, and gen_conn_type under res/msi, asserts the unconditional service/tray conditions, and pins this requirements/ledger disposition.
57Windows EXE certificate cleanup completion authority — EXE uninstall invoked --uninstall-cert from the elevated batch, but certificate cleanup was not a checked operation: failure to resolve the current executable merely skipped the command, the CLI arm discarded uninstall_cert() errors, the FFI wrapper always returned Ok(()), and the native helper returned void. The registry helper also checked a never-updated readResult after reading the certificate Blob, so the fixed WDK test-certificate thumbprint could remain trusted while uninstall still reported success.FIX R-S11d-22 makes the native certificate helper return BOOL, removes the raw allocation/string-cast helper code, reads the certificate Blob through checked RegQueryValueExW status, compares the bounded WDK test-cert suffix before deleting the fixed thumbprint key, preserves the historical malformed-ROOT-store cleanup with an explicit wide prefix, and opens certificate-store registry keys with read-scoped access. Rust treats a false helper result as an error, the --uninstall-cert CLI exits nonzero on that error, EXE uninstall fails if the current executable cannot be resolved, and the elevated uninstall batch wraps --uninstall-cert in the fail-fast command helper. verify.sh rejects the old void-return, ignored-result, stale-read-result, and KEY_ALL_ACCESS shapes and pins this requirements/ledger disposition.
58Windows EXE Amyuni IDD cleanup completion authority — EXE uninstall invoked --uninstall-amyuni-idd from the elevated batch, but Amyuni driver cleanup was not a checked EXE-uninstall operation: failure to resolve the current executable skipped the command, the CLI arm discarded amyuni_idd::uninstall_driver() errors, the top-level --uninstall dispatcher logged uninstall_me() failure without exiting nonzero, and the AMD64 runtime helper launched deviceinstaller64.exe with ShellExecuteW without a process handle, wait, exit-code check, timeout, or operation-specific reboot-required policy. EXE uninstall could therefore report success while the optional Amyuni payload was present but cleanup failed or was still unobserved; install/update could also treat reboot-required helper completion as success while immediately trying to use the driver.FIX R-S11d-23 gives the EXE path the same completion authority class as the MSI Amyuni cleanup, with an operation-aware install/remove split. EXE uninstall resolves the current executable once for uninstall helpers, treats that failure as fatal, quotes it through the elevated-batch path guard, and wraps --uninstall-amyuni-idd in the fail-fast command helper. The top-level --uninstall dispatcher exits nonzero when uninstall_me() fails, and the Amyuni helper CLI arm logs cleanup errors and exits nonzero. The runtime AMD64 helper builds its own quoted mutable command line, starts the checked absolute helper with CreateProcessW and an explicit application path, waits up to the same two-minute bound used by MSI, reads the child exit code, accepts ERROR_SUCCESS_REBOOT_REQUIRED only for remove/cleanup, rejects it for install/update before the driver is used, and propagates launch/wait/exit-code failures. Optional payload absence before selecting the helper remains a no-op; selected native/helper cleanup failure does not. verify.sh rejects the old skipped-command helper, ignored CLI/top-level errors, ShellExecuteW runtime launch, open-verb string helper, unobserved helper completion, and install/remove reboot-policy confusion.
59Desktop service lifecycle completion authority — the explicit desktop service CLI, platform wrappers, and Debian package lifecycle had several ways to report success without proving the service action completed. core_main ignored install_service()/uninstall_service() returns; Linux service install imported active-user whole-config files into root service state before start, install/uninstall discarded systemctl enable/disable, and uninstall treated stop failure as success; Debian maintainer scripts masked deb-systemd-invoke/deb-systemd-helper stop/start/reload/disable/purge failures with || true; build.py could hide Debian staging/control-script copy and dpkg-deb failures; macOS service install/uninstall treated every successful osascript spawn as the same branch regardless of nonzero exit, checked only one plist postcondition, discarded local launchctl status, and the privileged uninstall script masked daemon unload failure with || true before deleting plists.FIX R-S11c-16 and R-S11c-10j make service lifecycle completion status-authoritative. The CLI exits nonzero when install/uninstall wrappers return failure. Linux no longer imports active-user Config/Config2 files into root service state during install; service-owned unattended password provisioning remains the typed _service + polkit path, and systemctl enable, start, disable, and stop failures are fatal wrapper failures with logged status. Debian maintainer scripts use deb-systemd-helper for enable/disable/purge state and deb-systemd-invoke only for unit stop/start, while manager reload is the checked fixed command /bin/systemctl --system daemon-reload; none of those failures are masked: upgrades stop an existing old unit before transition, configure checks enable/manager-reload/start, removal/deconfigure checks stop/disable/manager-reload before deleting entry points/unit files, and purge checks helper-state plus the stock /root/.config/RustDesk service-config tree removal. Debian packaging starts from clean tmpdeb staging, uses checked control-script copies, splits dpkg-deb from cleanup, and the release build extracts the emitted .deb to compare preinst/postinst/prerm/postrm with source and run the same maintainer-script lifecycle validator before hashing artifacts, rejecting masked lifecycle failures, invalid helper actions, or missing manager reloads. macOS routes AppleScript and launchctl calls through checked status handling, requires both daemon and agent plist postconditions, propagates synchronous uninstall result, removes/reloads the current-session LaunchAgent label with verification, and the privileged scripts verify daemon unload/load state and plist removal instead of masking launchctl errors. verify.sh asserts the cross-desktop CLI, Linux, Debian-package, and macOS invariants; build-debian.sh validates built Debian control scripts; apple-conform-check.sh mirrors the macOS source-conformance half.
60Windows MSI service-state and SAS policy persistence — the per-machine MSI defaulted STOP_SERVICE to 'Y', then ran an impersonated immediate custom action that read stop-service from the installing user's AppData config and used that user-writable value to decide whether a deferred elevated custom action would create/start the LocalSystem service. The same installer family also wrote persistent HKLM SoftwareSASGeneration=1 from both MSI and EXE install paths without recording ownership, rollback data, or the original machine-policy value. Runtime SAS already had a temporary set/restore model, but it conflated absent with DWORD 0 and still called SendSAS after open/read/set failures.FIX R-S11d-16 deletes the MSI config-derived service switch and all supporting custom actions/exports/source: STOP_SERVICE, SetPropertyServiceStop, SetPropertyFromConfig, SetPropertyIsServiceRunning, TryDeleteStartupShortcut, and ReadConfig. MSI service creation now follows the fork's pinned service policy: create/start on install/repair/upgrade, and stop/delete only during uninstall or upgrade cleanup. Persistent installer writes to SoftwareSASGeneration are deleted from MSI and EXE paths; no blind uninstall delete is added for previously written machine state. Runtime SAS now serializes local policy mutation, accepts only documented policy values, distinguishes absent from present values, fails before SendSAS on open/read/set failure, restores present DWORD values including 0, preserves Ease of Access SAS policy by changing value 2 to 3 instead of 1, deletes only values it created as absent, and returns restoration errors to the caller. verify.sh asserts the MSI conditions, absence of the deleted service/SAS custom actions and config reader, absence of persistent installer SAS writes, runtime serialized fail-closed temporary policy handling, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
61Linux polkit policy/package assurance — Linux installed-service password changes correctly use a typed local _service request plus polkit, but a source-only grep for the action id does not prove that the fork ships exactly the intended administrator-authentication policy in every Debian package path, nor that a future permissive .rules override or misplaced policy payload would fail release builds. Distro-local administrator policy overrides are outside app-side LPE, but the fork's own package must not be the source of permissive service-owned credential policy drift.FIX R-S11e keeps the existing R-S11b-2c authority path unchanged: SO_PEERCRED-derived peer process subject, absolute /usr/bin/pkcheck with --action-id, --process, and --allow-user-interaction, then root-service final commit to the service-owned main server. The new assurance gate parses res/com.carriez.RustDesk.policy as XML and requires exactly one action, com.carriez.RustDesk.set-unattended-password, with allow_any, allow_inactive, and allow_active all set to auth_admin; it rejects yes, auth_self, and keep-style authorization values, rejects repo-shipped matching .rules overrides, asserts that all Debian build paths stage the policy into usr/share/polkit-1/actions, and rejects the obsolete executable usr/share/rustdesk/files/polkit stub. build-debian.sh now validates each emitted .deb before hashing it: the package must contain the policy at usr/share/polkit-1/actions/com.carriez.RustDesk.policy as a root-owned regular file that is not group/world writable, its XML semantics and bytes must match the source policy, and no legacy executable polkit stub may be packaged. verify.sh and build-debian.sh both call the same policy checker.
62Windows helper launch environment authority — token-switched helper launches carry CM and whiteboard launch-token proof material in environment variables. If the target user's inherited environment already contains a same-name RustDesk launch-proof variable, appending the launcher-owned value produces an ambiguous environment block whose effective value depends on environment-block lookup behavior rather than the service's generated authority. This is helper launch ambiguity and denial rather than a proven SYSTEM shell, but the authority boundary is still wrong: launch proof material belongs to the launcher, not ambient user profile state.FIX R-S11c-15 makes the Windows LaunchProcessWin environment merge explicit. The C++ path parses environment keys, removes inherited same-name entries with case-insensitive comparison before appending launcher-owned variables, and sorts the final environment block before CreateProcessAsUserW, matching the Win32 environment-block contract. verify.sh asserts the key parser, case-insensitive key comparison, base-entry removal for extra variables, final sort, and that the merge helper stays outside C linkage.
63Windows portable installer source-staging authority — the self-extracting rustdesk-*install.exe unpacked its embedded app into the caller-writable per-user LocalAppData cache, then launched the embedded app's interactive or silent install flow; the elevated EXE installer copied the embedded app's parent directory into Program Files, and the raw --silent-install, Flutter --install, and in-app install paths reached the same copy sink without proving they came from protected staging. That made mutable or arbitrary current-executable directories part of the privileged install-source chain, and path/write/generated-file failures in the packer were not fatal.FIX R-S11d-17 moves the install source boundary to protected staging and makes the app-side installer consume that proof before copying: the packer relaunches itself with UAC, waits for and checks the elevated child exit code, requires the protected leg to be elevated, creates a private per-run sibling directory under the width-correct Program Files root, rejects any overlap with the final install root, validates embedded paths as relative Windows-safe components, treats decompression/create/write/sync and generated RuntimeBroker copy failures as fatal, and launches interactive and silent install operations only from that protected staging tree. The installed app's install_me sink now requires the exact protected marker, an already elevated child process, a regular current executable, and a regular non-reparse Program Files\RustDesk-staging-* source directory whose parent is the width-correct Program Files root and whose path is outside the final install root; the copy command receives that verified source directory directly instead of deriving authority from arbitrary current_exe().parent(). Child install failures exit nonzero for the waiting packer, the protected UI hides and blocks run-without-install, installer command batches run directly when already elevated, and cleanup removes only manifest-known files after reparse checks on success and post-extraction failure paths. verify.sh asserts the protected relaunch, elevated child exit-code check, staging root, final-root overlap rejection, payload validation, synced create-new writes, protected install marker, app-side exact marker/elevation/source proof, verified-source-directory copy construction, interactive and silent child nonzero failure exits, no-install block, already-elevated command path, fatal generated-file copy, manifest cleanup, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
64Windows stale RustDesk IDD install helper completion — the raw Windows --install-idd CLI arm invoked the old RustDesk IDD install/update helper under allow_err!, so driver create/install/update failure could be logged and still exit as success. In this fork the supported virtual-display implementation is Amyuni, whose install/update path is the runtime checked deviceinstaller64.exe/SetupAPI path, not the inactive RustDesk IDD CLI helper.FIX R-S11d-24 makes --install-idd reject-only: it logs that the command is unsupported in this build and exits nonzero without touching driver state. The supported Amyuni install/update path remains covered by R-S11d-23's checked helper launch, wait, exit-code, timeout, and reboot-required policy. verify.sh asserts the reject-only CLI arm, nonzero exit, absence of allow_err! and the old helper call from that arm, the Amyuni implementation marker, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
65Windows Amyuni SetupAPI install reboot-required completion — the active Amyuni virtual-display install path already rejected reboot-required completion from the checked deviceinstaller64.exe helper before using the driver, but the direct SetupAPI fallback called win_device::install_driver(), received the same reboot_required authority signal, and ignored it before reporting the driver install usable.FIX R-S11d-25 makes the direct SetupAPI install fallback follow the helper-path install policy: after win_device::install_driver(), reboot_required is fatal because the driver cannot be treated as immediately usable. Remove/cleanup reboot-required remains accepted under the cleanup policy; this fix is scoped to install/update before monitor plug-in proceeds. verify.sh asserts the direct SetupAPI install call, the reboot_required branch, fatal install reboot-required error, absence of the old discarded install result shape, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
66Windows app-name identity contract — signed custom-client "app-name" and MSI --app-name are system identifiers, not arbitrary display strings: the same value is used as an executable/path component, URI-scheme prefix, service name, HKCR/HKLM registry key component, firewall rule label, shortcut path component, and elevated EXE batch text. In current master this was not a local caller-writable LPE because the live sources are signed custom-client config and build-time MSI packaging, but unsafe signer/build input could still cross into privileged shell/service/protocol sinks without an identifier grammar.FIX R-S11d-26 defines app-name as a constrained ASCII system identifier: 1-64 characters, first character letter, last character letter or digit, interior characters letters/digits/hyphen only. Signed custom-client parsing rejects non-string or invalid "app-name" before applying any payload settings, and MSI preprocessing rejects invalid --app-name before resource/package generation. verify.sh asserts the Rust validator, that the signed-config APP_NAME write is guarded by it, the MSI regex/front-door check, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
67Windows custom-client public staging deletion — a dormant Windows custom-client update helper copied custom.txt from a public-style staging directory under RustDeskCustomClientStaging into the current executable directory and then loaded it as runtime custom-client configuration. The cluster had no live caller in current source, generated bindings, scripts, packaging, tests, or docs, and signed custom-client parsing now rejects invalid identity fields before applying payload settings. Still, a public staging-to-install-directory copy has no supported authority model in this fork: custom-client identity and policy come from signed runtime configuration or build/package inputs, not from a mutable public filesystem mailbox.FIX R-S11d-27 deletes the Windows public staging helper rather than hardening or leaving it as dormant compatibility code. get_public_base_dir, get_custom_client_staging_dir, remove_custom_client_staging_dir, prepare_custom_client_update, the RustDeskCustomClientStaging path, and the executable-directory custom.txt copy/load path are absent from src/platform/windows.rs. verify.sh fails if those symbols, the staging directory name, or the executable-dir copy sink reappear, and gates this requirements/ledger disposition.
68Windows dormant diagnostic message-box deletion — a legacy Rust diagnostic macro had a Windows-only branch that routed text into a platform message_box helper. The macro had no callers, and the helper was not a cross-platform trait requirement, exported FFI surface, or Flutter bridge API. The dormant helper still contained environment-controlled behavior: NO_DIALOG suppressed the dialog, PRINT_OUT printed text, WRITE_TO_FILE wrote to an environment-selected path, and the non-dialog path wrote text to the clipboard while discarding errors. This was not a live privilege escalation because the macro/helper were unreferenced, but privileged builds should not retain dormant environment-selected file/clipboard side-effect code.FIX R-S11d-28 deletes the unused my_println! macro and the Windows message_box helper. The peer protocol/UI MessageBox flow is unaffected. verify.sh fails if my_println!, macro_rules! my_println, the Windows message_box helper, NO_DIALOG, PRINT_OUT, WRITE_TO_FILE, or the old diagnostic message strings reappear, and gates this requirements/ledger disposition.
69Windows service-adjacent path known-folder authority — Windows service-adjacent code still derived profile, root recording, and installer fallback paths from the process SystemDrive environment. These paths were not a proven unprivileged-to-SYSTEM primitive: the recording path is local session-recording state, the active-user home path is a UI fallback, and installer command staging already validated candidate paths before writing. The authority model was still wrong for privileged-capable Windows processes: protected system roots and profile roots must come from Windows known-folder APIs, not mutable process environment text.FIX R-S11d-29 removes SystemDrive from the Windows profile/recording/installer fallback path surface. Active-user home fallback now resolves FOLDERID_UserProfiles, rejects active usernames that are not a single safe path component, and joins the username under that known folder. Root recording directory selection calls the shared FOLDERID_ProgramData helper and fails closed with a logged error if ProgramData cannot be resolved. Installer command fallback keeps the existing temp/ProgramData/user-accessible candidate model, but its ProgramData and Windows Temp candidates now come from FOLDERID_ProgramData and FOLDERID_Windows. verify.sh rejects any reintroduced SystemDrive use in these sources and asserts the known-folder helpers, username component guard, root-recording path, Windows Temp fallback, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
70macOS runtime service ACL inspection provenance — the macOS _service IPC installed-app/helper trust check and the service-owned LaunchAgent password-snapshot plist trust check rejected extended ACLs by spawning absolute /bin/ls -lde and parsing its formatted output. This was not a demonstrated command-injection primitive because the command path was absolute and the checked path was argv-passed, but it was still the wrong authority model for root-side runtime trust: filesystem ACL state must come from the kernel/libc ACL interface, not from an external formatter process.FIX R-S11c-17 makes runtime ACL inspection native and shared. src/ipc/auth.rs now owns a single macos_path_has_no_extended_acl helper that builds a checked C path, calls acl_get_link_np(..., ACL_TYPE_EXTENDED), validates the returned ACL with acl_valid_link_np, rejects any first entry returned by acl_get_entry, and frees the ACL through a drop guard. NUL paths, ACL retrieval failure, and ACL validation failure reject the path. src/ipc.rs uses that shared helper for the LaunchAgent plist parent/file trust check instead of carrying a second ls parser. verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh assert the native ACL API shape and reject MACOS_LS / Command::new(MACOS_LS) in the Rust runtime trust path. The separate installer shell ACL surface is closed by R-S11c-18.
71Windows elevated post-install relaunch executable authority — after privileged EXE install/service-install/service-uninstall batches completed, run_after_run_cmds() relaunched the GUI/tray by calling get_install_info(). That helper intentionally honors legacy uninstall registry keys before the current app key, so stale HKLM uninstall metadata could choose an elevated post-install child executable even though the privileged install path had already fixed the service root to Program Files.FIX R-S11d-30 removes the registry-selected relaunch helper. Post-elevated relaunch now receives the exact fixed installed executable chosen by install_me, install_service, or uninstall_service, revalidates that it equals fixed_service_install_dir_and_exe(), requires it to be an existing file, and propagates spawn failures instead of hiding them behind allow_err!. verify.sh asserts all three callers pass the fixed executable, the helper rechecks the fixed root, rejects any get_install_info() use in the relaunch helper, and fails if the old best-effort helper returns.
72Windows privacy broker served-session authority — the topmost-window privacy implementation launched RuntimeBroker_rustdesk.exe by taking a user token from WTSGetActiveConsoleSessionId(). That is the physical-console session, not necessarily the Windows session currently being served by the installed RustDesk service when RDP/ICA session sharing is enabled. The service-owned server already runs inside the selected served session, so the privacy broker must follow that receiver session rather than re-selecting a different interactive session at broker-launch time.FIX R-S11d-31 removes the physical-console lookup from the privacy broker. The broker launch now resolves the current process session id of the service-owned --server process handling the authenticated Remote session, fails closed if that session id is unavailable or invalid, obtains the user token for that served session, and launches the broker there. verify.sh rejects WTSGetActiveConsoleSessionId in the privacy broker source and asserts the served-session helper, token error shape, explicit broker executable launch, null command line, current directory, and file-existence checks.
73macOS privileged installer ACL enforcement provenance — after R-S11c-17 moved runtime ACL trust to native libc ACL APIs, the admin-authorized macOS installer still proved helper ACL-free state by piping absolute /bin/ls -lde formatter output into /usr/bin/awk. This was not a command-injection primitive because paths were quoted and the tools were absolute, but a privileged installer should not make service-helper authority depend on parsing a human-readable listing format.FIX R-S11c-18 removes the installer ls -lde / awk ACL parser. The bundled helper source is verified as a non-symlink executable with the pinned helper code-signing requirement, then the deployed /Library/PrivilegedHelperTools directory and com.carriez.rustdesk_service helper are made ACL-free with checked /bin/chmod -N postconditions before root:wheel, mode, executable-bit, byte-compare, and code-signing checks. verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh require that deployed-helper ACL postcondition and fail if installer ACL authority reintroduces /bin/ls -lde or the old NR > 1 parser.
74macOS LaunchAgent live argv authority — the service-owned password runtime snapshot path already required launchd to report the requesting pid under the expected root-owned LaunchAgent plist, and it parsed that plist to require the exact ProgramArguments array. The live peer-process proof, however, accepted any argv whose second and third entries were --server and --service-owned-server, leaving extra live arguments outside the proof even though they were not part of the trusted LaunchAgent job shape. This was not a demonstrated local-to-root path because installed-app/helper identity and root-owned launchd plist proof were already load-bearing, but privileged credential delivery should not mix an exact plist proof with a prefix-shaped live argv proof.FIX R-S11c-19 makes the live LaunchAgent process proof exact-length. src/ipc.rs routes the macOS snapshot peer check through macos_service_owned_server_live_argv_is_expected, which admits only a three-entry process command vector with --server followed by --service-owned-server; extra live arguments fail before the LaunchDaemon returns a root credential snapshot. The existing root-owned LaunchAgent plist parser remains exact over Label, ProgramArguments, RunAtLoad, and KeepAlive. R-S11e-9 subsequently binds the installed-app/helper identity proof to the socket audit token. Regression tests reject extra live argv and extra plist arguments, and verify.sh/apple-conform-check.sh gate the exact live argv helper, its wiring, and both extra-argument tests.
75Unix terminal default-shell command provenance — the non-Windows terminal service selected the executable for an authenticated remote terminal by first reading the process SHELL environment variable, then falling back to fixed shell names and finally returning /bin/sh even when no trusted shell had been proven. In the shipped Linux systemd unit this was not a demonstrated unprivileged-to-root primitive because the service unit does not pass SHELL and an authenticated terminal intentionally grants the owner the service user's shell, but root/service-adjacent command provenance must not depend on ambient environment or an unverified fallback.FIX R-S11c-20 removes SHELL from Unix terminal shell selection. src/server/terminal_service.rs now chooses only fixed absolute Unix shell candidates, canonicalizes the selected path, requires both the candidate/canonical parent directory and the executable to be root-owned and not group/world-writable, requires the executable bit, and fails terminal open if no trusted candidate exists. Linux candidates cover /bin and /usr/bin bash/zsh/sh, macOS uses the system zsh/bash/sh paths, and Android uses /system/bin/sh. Regression tests reject relative and parent-traversal shell paths and prove a trusted absolute candidate in the Linux verifier environment; verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh reject any reintroduced SHELL, bare shell, or unconditional /bin/sh success fallback.
76Linux pkcheck executable provenance — Linux installed-service password changes already used a typed _service request, a SO_PEERCRED-derived pid,start-time,uid subject, the exact RustDesk polkit action, and the packaged auth_admin policy gate, but the root service still launched the authorization checker as a raw /usr/bin/pkcheck path string. On a normal Debian/Ubuntu host replacing that executable or its parent already requires root-equivalent compromise, so this was not a demonstrated local-to-root primitive; it was a weaker command-authority proof than the fork now applies to other root/service-adjacent helpers.FIX R-S11e-1 keeps the polkit subject/action semantics unchanged and binds the executable authority before launch. src/ipc.rs now resolves only the fixed absolute PKCHECK_PATH, rejects relative or parent-traversal path shapes, canonicalizes the candidate, requires both candidate/canonical parent directories to be root-owned and not group/world-writable, requires the canonical executable to be a root-owned regular file, not group/world-writable, and executable, and fails the service-owned password change closed if no trusted helper is present. Regression tests cover path-shape and metadata policy, and verify.sh rejects the old direct Command::new("/usr/bin/pkcheck") launch shape.
77macOS _service client-side server authentication — macOS service-owned password provisioning used receiver-side _service caller authentication, but clients connected to the shared service socket path before proving that the peer was the real privileged helper. A local same-user process that won the socket race while the helper was absent could impersonate _service: the service-owned LaunchAgent could receive attacker-chosen runtime password storage/salt, and the GUI/CLI password-change client could send the proposed plaintext password to the fake server before Authorization Services completed. This was local-only, but it crossed the service credential boundary before the client had authenticated the server.FIX R-S11e-2 makes macOS _service client connection fail closed unless the connected server proves as the deployed privileged helper before any _service message is sent or read. connect_with_path() routes the macOS POSTFIX_SERVICE case through ensure_macos_service_server_is_trusted, which requires peer uid 0 from the socket, resolves the socket-bound LOCAL_PEERTOKEN through Security.framework, verifies the live peer code against the pinned privileged-helper requirement with strict validation, then accepts only the deployed /Library/PrivilegedHelperTools/com.carriez.rustdesk_service helper path with root:wheel non-group/world-writable directory/file state, executable bit, no symlinks, and no extended ACLs. The effective peer pid is diagnostic metadata, not the code authority. There is no compatibility fallback to an unauthenticated, PID-only, path-only, or subprocess-codesign service server. verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh gate the helper, root uid check, audit-token code proof, connect-path wiring, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
78Linux helper canonical target provenance — Linux root/service-adjacent helper resolvers had removed shell/PATH lookup, but still checked fs::metadata() through fixed candidate paths and returned the original path string to Command::new. If a fixed helper candidate resolved through a symlink chain into a directory writable by an unprivileged local user, a privileged process could verify one target and execute a swapped target. The meaningful impact is installed Linux service mode for helpers such as sudo/env in the root-to-user server launch and any shared loginctl or notification helper used from a privileged process.FIX R-S11e-3 gives Linux fixed-helper resolvers the same authority shape as the trusted pkcheck and terminal-shell paths. App-side helpers in src/platform/linux.rs and shared helpers in libs/hbb_common/src/platform/linux.rs now reject relative or parent-traversal candidates, require the candidate parent directory to be root-owned and not group/world-writable, canonicalize the helper, require the canonical path and canonical parent to remain clean absolute root-owned non-writable state, require the canonical executable to be a root-owned regular file with no group/world write bits and at least one executable bit, and return the canonical PathBuf that is passed to Command::new. Regression tests cover bad path shapes and metadata policy; verify.sh gates canonicalization, candidate/canonical parent checks, executable-bit checks, canonical return wiring, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
79Windows elevated command-file reopen identity — Windows EXE install/uninstall/service-install/service-uninstall hardening kept generated elevated batch/VBS command files open with a read-only FILE_SHARE_READ handle while the elevated consumer ran, but the implementation obtained that read handle by closing the writer and reopening the pathname. A same-owner local process watching the command directory could race that close/reopen gap, replacing the file or modifying it in place before the read lock was acquired. Windows sharing protects only while a handle is open, and the creator/owner of a new file can still control its DACL, so a DACL-only or pathname-only proof is not a complete authority binding.FIX R-S11d-32 binds the elevated command-file handoff across the close/reopen boundary. Before dropping the writer, src/platform/windows.rs now records the command file identity from GetFileInformationByHandle, computes a SHA-256 digest over the exact bytes written, syncs the file, and only then reopens the pathname read-only with FILE_SHARE_READ. The reopened handle must report the same volume/file index and the same byte length/digest before any elevated cmd.exe or cscript.exe path can consume it; identity or content drift fails closed. The reopened read handle remains live for the existing elevated-command lifetime, preserving write/delete sharing denial after verification. Regression tests cover byte-exact digesting and identity equality, and verify.sh gates the identity query, digest proof, verified reopen helper, write-handle identity capture, reopened read-lock wiring, drift failures, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
80macOS _service accept-loop blocking-proof offload — macOS receiver-side admission for the world-connectable _service socket already required the active/root peer uid gate, installed-app peer proof, trusted PrivilegedHelperTools helper proof, native ACL checks, and pinned code-signing requirements before any privileged service message could be honored. The accept loop itself, however, runs on a current-thread Tokio runtime and performed filesystem metadata, ACL, peer executable resolution, and code-signing proof inline before spawning the per-connection task. A local connector could therefore stall service-IPC accepting while those proofs ran or blocked. This was a local availability hardening issue, not a credential-write bypass: failed or slow proof did not authorize a message or leak the service-owned password.FIX R-S11e-4 moves the macOS _service blocking proof off the accept loop without weakening the admission decision. The accepted socket's uid and macOS peer-process identity are captured as a typed ServiceScopedIpcAuthorization snapshot; that identity includes LOCAL_PEERTOKEN for the later Security.framework code proof and LOCAL_PEEREPID only as metadata. The accept path obtains a nonblocking bounded authorization slot and passes it to the macOS connection task, where the fail-closed audit-token executable/code-signing verifier runs in tokio::task::spawn_blocking. If the authorization budget is exhausted, the listener drops the connection before spawning a task. Otherwise, the task returns before the first stream.next().await if the blocking task fails, the uid gate fails, or the executable/trust proof fails, so no _service frame is read before authorization succeeds. Linux keeps its existing synchronous service admission path, and the macOS _url sender proof remains separate. verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh gate the snapshot type, snapshot verifier, authorization-slot budget, spawn_blocking task authorizer, start-loop ordering, absence of the old combined Linux/macOS pre-spawn service gate, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
81Linux service-owned main-server commit receiver proof — Linux installed-service password changes authorized the local requester with polkit and then sent Data::CommitServiceOwnedUnattendedPasswordChange from the root service into the uid-scoped main IPC server selected by process/path routing. The receiver rejected non-root commit senders, but the sender disclosed the proposed password before proving that the connected main IPC peer was the service-launched --server --service-owned-server process rather than a same-user process running the real binary with the service-owned marker. This was not a remote bypass and did not weaken polkit requester authorization, but the service-owned credential commit receiver still lacked a sender-side receiver proof.FIX R-S11e-5 makes the Linux service-owned password commit authenticate the main-server receiver before sending the commit. The root service now launches both active-user and headless/root service-owned servers with a service-parent environment claim. Before commit_service_owned_unattended_password_change sends the password, it proves the connected peer through SO_PEERCRED pid/uid, current-executable identity, exact three-argument --server --service-owned-server command shape, the service-parent environment value, and a live /proc ancestor chain back to the current root service process. Missing, stale, wrong-argv, wrong-parent, or non-descendant receivers fail closed before the password leaves the service. verify.sh gates the launch-parent propagation, receiver authenticator, exact argv helper, ancestor proof, structural commit-path ordering, regression test, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
82Linux _service client-side server authentication — Linux service-owned password changes sent Data::RequestServiceOwnedUnattendedPasswordChange to the shared _service Unix socket immediately after connecting. The real root service authenticated clients at accept time and the later root-service-to-main commit now authenticates the service-owned main-server receiver, but a local process could receive the proposed plaintext password by creating the shared _service socket while the legitimate root-owned service socket/parent was absent, for example after service stop, crash, disablement, or /tmp cleanup before service restart. This did not bypass polkit or remote authentication and required a local timing condition, but the client disclosed a service-owned credential before proving the receiver was the root RustDesk service.FIX R-S11e-6 makes Linux _service client connection fail closed unless the connected peer proves as the installed root service before any service-owned password request is sent. connect_with_path() routes the Linux POSTFIX_SERVICE case through ensure_linux_service_server_is_trusted, which derives peer pid/uid from SO_PEERCRED, requires uid 0, reuses current-executable identity proof, requires the exact live argv[1] == "--service" command shape, and requires the peer executable plus parent directory to be root-owned, executable where applicable, and not group/world-writable. Missing, non-root, wrong-executable, wrong-argv, or writable-path receivers fail before the password-bearing request leaves the client. verify.sh gates the receiver authenticator, exact argv helper, root-owned executable/parent predicates and tests, connect-path wiring, request-before-send ordering through authenticated connect_service, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
83Voice-call close/reset authority — R-S19 already confined voice-call connect and peer-audio playback to Remote/ViewCamera sessions, but the peer close message and the connection-manager Data::CloseVoiceCall path still called close_voice_call() without first proving that this connection was a Remote/ViewCamera voice-call owner. Separately, ordinary connection teardown reset the global voice-call input device for any session type. A FileTransfer or Terminal peer could not start a call or inject audio, but it could still drive cross-session voice-call UI/input reset state outside its AuthConnType.FIX R-S19 now treats voice-call close/reset as the same typed capability as voice-call start/playback. src/server/connection.rs routes voice-call start, accept/refuse, peer close, CM close, and teardown through connection-owned voice state: can_drive_voice_call() admits only Remote/ViewCamera; close_voice_call() returns false unless the connection is Remote/ViewCamera and has pending or active voice-call state; CM close notifies the peer only after that state closes; and the global input-device reset on teardown runs only for an active voice-call owner. verify.sh gates the helper, Remote/ViewCamera authority, result-bearing close path, state-owned close condition, CM result check, and teardown reset guard.
84Linux FUSE file-content response provenance — Linux file-clipboard FUSE mounted one global response queue per side. FUSE file nodes carried the source conn_id and sent FileContentsRequest back to that connection, but incoming FileContentsResponse messages were queued as bare clipboard messages and matched only by the 32-bit stream_id. A second authenticated file-clipboard-capable session could fill the bounded queue, and a guessed or colliding stream id could satisfy another connection's local paste read. This was not unauthenticated, not remote-to-root, and not unbounded memory growth; it was a cross-session capability/provenance bug in the Linux file-clipboard path.FIX R-S19 now routes Linux FUSE file-content responses through bounded active routes keyed by the connection and fresh read stream that originated the request. FuseServer::read_node generates a fresh stream_id, registers a (conn_id, stream_id) route, and only then sends FileContentsRequest; src/clipboard_file.rs passes the serving conn_id into handle_file_content_response; and the handler dispatches FuseFileContentResponse { conn_id, clip } only to the matching active route. Responses that do not match the fresh active key are dropped before they can occupy another read path. Regression coverage proves bounded route admission, wrong-connection and wrong-stream rejection, stale route removal, duplicate active-route rejection, and a real read_node request/response path where wrong-session replies are ignored before the correct response supplies the bytes; verify.sh gates the keyed router type, caller wiring, fresh per-read stream ids, active-route registration, bounded nonblocking admission, stale global-queue absence, and regression tests.
85Windows MSI deferred install-root provenance — the MSI package already rooted App.InstallFolder privately under ProgramFiles6432Folder and removed the browse/public install-folder UI, but deferred no-impersonation custom actions still trusted their CustomActionData shape inside the LocalSystem DLL. RemoveRuntimeGeneratedFiles only rejected empty/root strings before deleting the fixed broker name, RemoveAmyuniIdd built the fallback helper path from raw install-folder text before CreateProcessW, and CreateStartService returned success if service data lacked the expected semicolon delimiter. The current package did not expose a normal low-privilege way to supply those malformed values, but privileged MSI custom actions should validate the exact root/helper/service authority they consume, not rely solely on WiX sequencing staying private forever.FIX R-S11d-33 makes the MSI deferred custom-action DLL fail closed on malformed install-root and service-command authority. res/msi/CustomActions/CustomActions.cpp now normalizes deferred install folders with GetFullPathNameW, rejects empty/relative/root/path-too-long values, requires the install directory to be an immediate child of FOLDERID_ProgramFiles or FOLDERID_ProgramFilesX86, requires the Program Files parent and any existing install directory to be non-reparse directories, and feeds the normalized install folder into runtime broker cleanup and Amyuni fallback path construction. The Amyuni fallback now joins usbmmidd_v2 with an explicit separator, requires the helper directory and deviceinstaller64.exe to exist as non-reparse directory/file objects, then launches the already checked absolute helper path. CreateStartService now treats missing or extra delimiters as fatal, requires the service name to match the constrained system-identifier grammar, parses only the exact quoted <exe> --service command shape, normalizes the executable path, proves it is the matching service executable under a trusted MSI install folder, requires the file to exist and not be a reparse point, and passes the normalized command to CreateServiceW. verify.sh positively gates the Program Files directory declaration, absence of installer directory-setter UI/actions, native install-folder validator, service-command validator, malformed-data fatal path, normalized service command, normalized runtime cleanup, non-reparse Amyuni helper proof, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
86Permanent-password PRS read-state authority — the direct listener and CPace auth boundary already failed closed when the stored permanent-password PRS was missing or undecryptable, but the old string accessor collapsed all unavailable states into an empty string. That made an operator-cleared credential, an empty half-state, and an undecryptable machine-UUID-sealed password_prs indistinguishable to status/reporting code, and allowed the direct listener park to look like ordinary no-password state rather than a durable at-rest credential read failure.FIX R-S9 now carries permanent-password PRS reads as typed authority: PermanentPasswordPrsRead::Available, Empty, or UndecryptableStorage. Config::read_permanent_password_prs() is the status source; get_permanent_password_prs() only string-adapts unavailable states for existing CPace callers, where empty still means fail closed before keying. has_permanent_password() keys on PRS availability, not stale config.password. The service-owned runtime snapshot helper preserves the typed status, and direct_server logs undecryptable stored PRS distinctly while keeping the listener parked/dropped until a valid password is provisioned.
