No fallback is admitted from stale runtime memory, config.password, preset/hard settings, or prior service snapshots. Regression tests cover decryptable, empty, half-state, and undecryptable storage, and verify.sh gates the typed enum, undecryptable branch, server status helper, direct-listener log, and absence of a silent unwrap_or_default() collapse in the PRS string accessor.
87AV1/libaom runtime quarantine — current public aom advisories are not a proven viewer-decoder-only issue; they localize to libaom encoder/control surfaces, while the old fork still exposed AV1 as a selectable, advertiseable, negotiable runtime codec. A password-correct hostile peer could still try to send Av1s frames, and stale local preferences/UI/server config could still steer the app toward AV1 unless the policy was enforced at every codec boundary.FIX Appendix C #2b remains open for the other native viewer decode paths, but AV1/libaom itself is no longer a runtime codec. scrap::codec advertises no AV1 encode/decode capability, ignores stale AV1 preferences by falling back to the normal software policy, creates an invalid decoder for CodecFormat::AV1, and fails inbound Av1s before any native decoder path. The receive loop treats AV1 as locally unsupported before creating a decoder/recorder worker. The server encoder config maps stale AV1 requests to VP9, the Flutter desktop/mobile/toolbar codec UI removes AV1, the FFI codec menu JSON omits the av1 key, codec-preference no longer accepts av1, and the old test_av1/av1-test startup benchmark path is deleted. verify.sh gates the policy function, capability tests, decoder-disabled test, UI/session/config absence, server VP9 fallback, benchmark/probe deletion, and this requirements/status/watch disposition.
88AV1/libaom dependency removal — runtime quarantine was necessary but incomplete while the native library, FFI bindings, overlay port, and offline build paths still carried aom. A future refactor, build Dockerfile, or stale build script could have reintroduced a linked libaom path without changing user-visible UI.EXCISE AV1 remains only a protocol/wire enum. The aom entries are deleted from vcpkg.json; res/vcpkg/aom, libs/scrap/src/common/aom.rs, and aom_ffi.h are deleted; libs/scrap/build.rs no longer generates aom_ffi.rs; EncoderCfg has no AV1/libaom variant; Linux/Android/Windows/Apple/dev-check scripts, README build commands, build Dockerfiles, and tracked build scaffolds do not install, stub, or reference aom; and scripts/native-codec-watch.sh now watches only the retained vcpkg package set while recording aom as retired. verify.sh fails if a source module, FFI binding, bindgen package, overlay path, manifest entry, build Dockerfile, build scaffold, or ledger record reintroduces libaom.
89Windows MSI deferred firewall/service action target provenance — after R-S11d-33, the MSI custom-action DLL proved install-root authority for service creation, runtime broker cleanup, and Amyuni fallback execution, but two deferred no-impersonation action classes still treated action data as the target authority. AddFirewallRules/RemoveFirewallRules accepted a mode byte plus raw executable path and wrote firewall policy for the derived rule name/path. TryStopDeleteService accepted a raw service name, stopped/deleted that service, and then killed processes by the derived executable name. The current package authored those values from the private Product/InstallFolder model, so this was privileged-state correctness hardening rather than a newly proven ordinary-user LPE in the shipped MSI.FIX R-S11d-34 makes the remaining MSI deferred action targets native-proven before LocalSystem state changes. Firewall actions normalize the executable path, require its parent to be the trusted MSI Program Files install folder shape, require a constrained .exe rule identity, require the executable to exist for add, and pass only the normalized path into the COM firewall helper. Service stop/delete action data now carries the same quoted <exe> --service command shape as service creation; the native action validates the service identifier, normalizes the package executable, opens the service with query/stop/delete rights, proves the installed service ImagePath normalizes to the same trusted command before stop/delete, verifies deletion by service absence, and runs the remaining process cleanup only by exact normalized image path rather than executable name alone. verify.sh gates the product-executable validator, firewall normalized-path call, service-delete binary proof in WiX, live service-config proof, handle-bound trusted delete helper, image-path-bound cleanup, and absence of the old raw firewall path/name-only cleanup.
90User-owned permanent-password main IPC receiver authentication — the typed user-owned password path correctly prevented service-owned receivers from accepting Data::SetUserOwnedPermanentPassword, but the client still trusted ordinary main IPC before the password left the UI/CLI process. A same-UID local process that won or squatted the per-user main IPC socket while the real user-owned daemon was absent, stale, or not yet started could answer permanent-password-user-owned-writable with Y and then receive the proposed plaintext password over Data::SetUserOwnedPermanentPassword. This was local credential disclosure and route confusion, not a root/SYSTEM privilege-escalation or remote-authentication bypass.FIX R-S11e-7 makes the user-owned password query/write path authenticate the connected main IPC receiver before trusting the capability answer or sending the password. connect_user_owned_password_main connects to main IPC and, on Linux/macOS, requires the peer uid to equal the caller's effective uid, the peer pid to resolve to the current executable, and the live argv to be exactly the user-owned argv[1] == "--server" shape, rejecting service-owned markers and extra mode args. Windows keeps the existing named-pipe server proof in connect_with_path. can_set_permanent_password and set_permanent_password prefer the service-owned path whenever it is available, so installed-service password changes no longer let a user-owned main IPC answer choose the route. verify.sh gates the authenticator, exact argv helper and test, authenticated password capability query and password-bearing write, service-owned-first ordering, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
91macOS service-owned password Authorization Services right ordering — macOS service-owned password changes used the custom com.carriez.RustDesk.set-unattended-password right, but the UI created the external authorization form before the trusted helper had necessarily normalized that right. On a fresh authdb, the UI-side existence check could fail before the helper received the request that would create the right. With a stale or weaker preexisting right, the authorization prompt could be acquired against that stale definition before daemon-side verification updated the database. This was a first-use/policy-definition correctness gap, not a proven password-write bypass from source alone, because the daemon still verified the external form noninteractively before writing the root credential.FIX R-S11e-8 makes the trusted root helper own right normalization before the UI prompts. After authenticated connect_service, the macOS client sends a no-secret MacosServiceOwnedPasswordRightReadyRequest over _service; the helper calls the existing right setup path and returns MacosServiceOwnedPasswordRightReadyResult(bool). The client calls service_owned_unattended_password_authorization() only after that readiness result is true and sends the password only after authorization succeeds. The native creator no longer accepts existence-only state: MacCreateServiceOwnedUnattendedPasswordAuthorizationExternalForm requires RustDeskSetUnattendedPasswordRightMatchesExpected, which reads the Authorization Services right definition and validates class=user, group=admin, shared=false, allow-root=false, authenticate-user=true, session-owner=false, extract-password=false, and timeout=0. verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh gate the service-only readiness message, readiness-before-authorization ordering, exact native right-definition validation, absence of the old existence-only helper, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
92macOS _service audit-token peer code identity — after the macOS _service server-auth, receiver-auth, and service-owned password snapshot fixes, the remaining peer-code proof still derived authority from a socket peer uid plus effective pid/path revalidation and filesystem code-signing checks. That was better than trusting a socket path, but it was still not the correct macOS identity primitive: the code authority for a connected Unix-domain peer is the socket-bound audit token, while pid and path are process metadata that can be re-observed later. The offloaded receiver path widened that conceptual gap by snapshotting uid/pid before a later blocking proof, and the LaunchAgent password-snapshot path mixed installed-app code trust with a later launchd pid/plist proof.FIX R-S11e-9 binds all macOS _service code-identity decisions to the connected socket's audit token. src/ipc/auth.rs captures getpeereid uid, LOCAL_PEEREPID metadata, and LOCAL_PEERTOKEN into MacosPeerProcessIdentity; resolves live peer code with Security.framework SecCodeCopyGuestWithAttributes using kSecGuestAttributeAudit; validates the pinned app/helper requirements with SecCodeCheckValidity and strict validation; and uses the resulting SecCodeCopyPath path only for the secondary installed-location, ownership, mode, symlink, and ACL checks. The Rust runtime no longer shells out to /usr/bin/codesign for these decisions. The same audit-token identity now backs macOS _service client-side server authentication, receiver-side service-scoped admission snapshots, and the service-owned password runtime snapshot requester before any password-bearing or credential-bearing message is trusted. There is no unauthenticated, PID-only, path-only, or subprocess-code-signing fallback. verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh gate the direct security-framework dependency, LOCAL_PEERTOKEN/LOCAL_PEEREPID use, legacy LOCAL_PEERPID absence, audit-token identity capture, native strict code validation, Rust MACOS_CODESIGN absence, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
93macOS residual process launch provenance — the connected-session wake path still spawned /usr/bin/caffeinate -u -t 5 after CPace keying, and the macOS root-to-user-bootstrap helper built launchctl asuser <uid> /usr/bin/env KEY=VALUE ... <current_exe>. Neither path gave a peer arbitrary executable or shell text, and the installed macOS service-owned server is the LaunchAgent user process rather than the root LaunchDaemon. The residual was correctness/provenance: a remote-authenticated connection should not create a helper process just to declare user activity, and a root-capable bootstrap launcher should not carry a generic environment-expansion executable or accept future arbitrary environment keys.FIX R-S11e-10 removes both residual process-launch shapes. The macOS wake path now calls the native IOKit power-management API through MacDeclareRemoteUserActivity, using IOPMAssertionDeclareUserActivity(..., kIOPMUserActiveRemote, ...) from src/platform/macos.mm; src/server.rs no longer spawns caffeinate. run_as_user_with_env now launches only /bin/launchctl asuser <uid> <current_exe> ..., sets the launcher-owned token variables with Command::env, and rejects every environment key except the CM and whiteboard launch-token/parent keys. The macOS build links IOKit explicitly, and verify.sh/apple-conform-check.sh gate the native IOKit helper, remote-user activity type, server wiring, absence of caffeinate, absence of the /usr/bin/env bridge, the environment-key allowlist, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
94Windows service-owned password commit receiver proof — Windows service-owned password changes already required an elevated client on _service, and the main receiver already refused to write CommitServiceOwnedUnattendedPasswordChange unless it was service-owned and the committing pipe peer was LocalSystem. That protected the final write, but the LocalSystem service still connected to the generic main pipe and serialized the plaintext service-owned password before proving the connected main-pipe receiver was actually the service-owned server. A user-owned same-executable --server process could therefore receive the secret-bearing frame and then reject it without committing it.FIX R-S11e-11 makes the LocalSystem sender authenticate the main-pipe receiver before sending the password-bearing commit. authenticate_windows_service_owned_main_server resolves the named-pipe server pid, requires the executable path to match the current executable, requires the server process token to be LocalSystem, and requires the exact --server --service-owned-server argv shape through the exact process-argument lookup. commit_service_owned_unattended_password_change calls that authenticator immediately after connect(..., "") and before Data::CommitServiceOwnedUnattendedPasswordChange(value) is serialized. The existing receiver-side main_channel_admits_state_mutation and LocalSystem peer gate remain as defense in depth. verify.sh gates the authenticator, exact argv helper, LocalSystem receiver proof, pre-send ordering, retained receiver-side policy, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
95Desktop URL IPC raw deep-link material handoff — the desktop uni-links listener's non-Flutter forwarding branch sent uri.toString() into bind.sendUrlScheme and then Rust _url IPC when a connection-manager or secondary process forwarded an OS-delivered rustdesk:// link to the main process. The parser later stripped ?password=, ?key=, and relay syntax before connect, but the raw URL had already crossed local IPC, contradicting R-X6's rule that embedded credential/trust-anchor material is never carried through handoff. This was local same-user IPC disclosure and authority hygiene, not remote reachability or root privilege escalation.FIX R-X6/R-S11c-9 now canonicalizes the desktop URL IPC payload before forwarding. listenUniLinks(handleByFlutter: false) calls urlLinkToForwardUrl, which reuses urlLinkToCmdArgs, rejects config/password write authorities and relay syntax, accepts only the supported connect authorities, and renders a fresh address-only rustdesk://<authority>/<id> URL for bind.sendUrlScheme. The raw uri.toString() forwarding shape is absent, so desktop _url IPC never carries embedded password/key/relay query material. verify.sh gates the helper, listener wiring, address-only renderer, and raw-forwarding absence.
96Windows EXE prior-MSI uninstall command reconstruction — after the elevated EXE installer learned to bind a prior MSI UninstallString to trusted System32 msiexec.exe, the delegation still trusted mutable registry command text. If the string contained msiexec.exe away from the command prefix, the binder failed and the raw string was still wrapped by checked_msi_uninstall_command; even a prefix-bound string could carry a shell tail such as & attacker in the preserved arguments. This was not a standalone default-ACL standard-user-to-SYSTEM proof, but it was an authority-confusion path where failed provenance binding degraded into elevated batch execution of prior-install metadata.FIX R-S11d-35 deletes the prefix-only binder and raw fallback. The prior MSI path now accepts only a msiexec.exe /X {PRODUCT-CODE}-class grammar, validates the braced GUID, rejects unsupported arguments and duplicate product codes, proves the product name with MsiGetProductInfoW(..., INSTALLPROPERTY_PRODUCTNAME, ...), requires it to match the current app name, and reconstructs "<System32>\msiexec.exe" /X {PRODUCT-CODE} from the trusted tool path. The registry string contributes no shell metacharacters, extra argv, alternate executable, or command tail. verify.sh gates the Windows Installer API feature, parser, GUID validator, product-name proof, reconstructed command, and absence of both command_with_system_tool and checked_msi_uninstall_command(reg_uninstall_string).
97macOS clipboard-file paste no-follow finalize — the macOS CLIPRDR file-paste path already sanitized hostile-peer FILEDESCRIPTOR names and bound FileContentsResponse frames to the originating connection, but its local paste worker still created directories/files, progress xattrs, cancellation cleanup, final rename, and final metadata by re-resolving paths under the Finder-observed paste directory. The normal installed macOS server is a user LaunchAgent, so this was not a hidden remote-to-root path; it was a peer-controlled filesystem-write correctness gap adjacent to R-S8/R-A5 where local symlink/path races could be followed by the paste worker instead of being rejected.FIX R-S11e-12 makes macOS clipboard-file paste use handle-relative no-follow filesystem operations. paste_task.rs opens the paste target directory with an openat(O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW) component walk, keeps that directory handle as the paste anchor, creates missing peer-requested parent directories through mkdirat plus no-follow re-open, reserves zero-size and .rddownload files with openat(O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW), stores only relative download state for later cleanup, updates/removes Finder progress xattrs with fsetxattr/fremovexattr on the open file descriptor, unlinks cancelled temp files with unlinkat, and finalizes completed downloads with renameatx_np(..., RENAME_EXCL) while retrying numbered final names on real name collisions. The old path-based File::create, create_dir_all, std::fs::rename, path xattr, path metadata reopen, path remove, and masked initial update_next(0).ok() shapes are absent. verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh gate the fd-relative primitives, no-follow/exclusive flags, requirements/ledger disposition, and absence of the deleted path-based write fallback.
98macOS clipboard-file paste placeholder temp authority — the macOS file-paste placeholder URL path still created pasteboard placeholder files directly as global /tmp/.rustdesk_* names, counted all matching names in /tmp, and cleaned the source placeholder by path. The installed macOS controlled side normally runs as the console user's LaunchAgent, so this was not a hidden remote-to-root path; it was local same-user pasteboard/temp authority ambiguity adjacent to R-S11e-12, where another local process could pre-shape or flood the global placeholder namespace and where the cleanup authority did not match the creation authority.FIX R-S11e-13 makes pasteboard placeholder state private to the pasteboard context. pasteboard_context.rs creates a unique rustdesk-clipboard-<euid>-<uuid> directory under the current user's temporary directory with mkdir(0700), opens it with O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC, normalizes/verifies owner-only mode and euid ownership through fchmod/fstat, and keeps the directory handle as the placeholder authority. item_data_provider.rs creates placeholder files only through that handle using openat(O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC) mode 0600; placeholder counting is scoped to the private directory and fails closed on read errors; source cleanup uses unlinkat through the same handle; and the observer callback captures the private authority instead of relying on a global function pointer. The old global /tmp/.rustdesk_* creation/counting model, source-placeholder path cleanup, and path-create fallback are absent. verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh retain the touched Apple clipboard sources and gate these invariants.
99Linux root/headless FileTransfer owner authority — follow-up review confirmed that, where the Linux controlled --server or its connection manager runs as root/service-owned in root, login-screen, or headless service modes, authenticated FileTransfer filesystem operations execute with that process authority. This is not an ordinary local-to-root IPC bug or remote unauthenticated escalation: the peer must complete CPace first, local CM file authority is bound to the server-validated Remote/FileTransfer connection token, and R-S8 already defines FileTransfer as the authenticated owner's single unconfined full-filesystem mode. Denying, demoting, or directory-confining this path would contradict R-S8/R-F1/R-F2 unless those requirements are deliberately changed.ACCEPTED / GATED R-S11e-14 records the intended authority model rather than adding a runtime restriction. The code keeps FileTransfer admitted after the local file-transfer permission gate, classifies it as AuthConnType::FileTransfer, grants CM file authority only for server-validated Remote/FileTransfer sessions, and confines FileTransfer to clipboard+file by clearing input, block-input, privacy, restart, recording, and host-audio capability. Normal active-desktop Linux service mode still launches the serving child as the active user; root/headless service modes keep FileTransfer at the serving process credential by design. verify.sh gates the R-S8 no-confinement requirement, this ledger row, the FileTransfer login shape, CM file-authority type binding, Unix headless username/refusal guards, and the FileTransfer capability confinement set.
100Windows EXE non-MSI uninstall install-root cleanup authority — after the EXE installer fixed service installs to Program Files and R-S11d-35 stopped replaying prior MSI uninstall command text, the non-MSI uninstall cleanup still used InstallLocation from the selected HKLM Uninstall registry key to build the elevated rd /s /q install-directory cleanup command. This was not promoted to a proven default-ACL standard-user-to-SYSTEM issue because HKLM uninstall metadata is normally administrator-owned, but it was still the wrong authority boundary: legacy install metadata may identify registry cleanup state, not choose a destructive elevated filesystem root.FIX R-S11d-36 separates uninstall registry discovery from install-directory cleanup authority. The registry helper may still select the compatibility uninstall subkey for UninstallString, share_rdp, and key deletion, and prior MSI uninstall remains reconstructed by R-S11d-35. The non-MSI EXE cleanup path now resolves the install directory only through fixed_service_install_path(""), quotes it through the elevated batch path guard, and uses that fixed Program Files service root for the absence-checked rd /s /q command. get_uninstall no longer calls get_install_info(), no longer consumes InstallLocation, and has no registry-path fallback for filesystem deletion. verify.sh gates the helper split, fixed-root batch path, uninstall command body, absence of registry-derived path deletion, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
101macOS hardened-runtime JIT entitlement minimization — macOS source conformance kept a single DebugProfile.entitlements file for both Debug and Profile and also granted com.apple.security.cs.allow-jit to the shipped Release.entitlements. Apple defines that hardened-runtime entitlement as permission to create writable/executable MAP_JIT memory, while Flutter Profile and Release builds are AOT. Carrying the JIT exception outside Debug was therefore an unnecessary executable-memory entitlement on retained macOS source.FIX R-S14 splits the macOS entitlements by build configuration. Debug.entitlements is the only file with com.apple.security.cs.allow-jit; Profile.entitlements keeps the profiler network-server entitlement but no JIT exception; Release.entitlements keeps only App Sandbox off, audio input, and network client. The Xcode project points each configuration to its own entitlement file, the old combined DebugProfile.entitlements is deleted, and apple-conform-check.sh exact-matches all three macOS entitlement maps plus the empty iOS entitlement map.
102Android service-destroy capture-resource teardown — the first Android F5 fix stopped and cleared MediaProjection on service destruction, but MainService.onDestroy() still did not run the same capture-resource cleanup as explicit app Stop. A system/OEM service-destroy path could therefore skip explicit ImageReader.close(), Surface.release(), and retained VirtualDisplay.release(), relying on process death or projection invalidation rather than the foreground service owning every capture resource it created.FIX R-S14/R-D7a now routes both MainService.onDestroy() and explicit destroy() through releaseCaptureResources(). That sink calls stopCapture() to close the ImageReader, release/null the Surface, stop raw video/audio state, then releases the retained reusable VirtualDisplay and stops/clears MediaProjection. stopCapture() nulls the released Surface, making the shared teardown idempotent when explicit Stop later reaches onDestroy(). verify.sh gates the shared lifecycle sink, both callers, the virtual-display/projection release chain, the nulled surface, and the retained START_NOT_STICKY restart barrier.
103Linux Flutter runner Rust core library load provenance — the Linux Flutter runner used dlopen("librustdesk.so") to load the Rust core. A no-slash dlopen target is resolved by the dynamic loader's library search order, including LD_LIBRARY_PATH for non-secure-execution processes, before the bundled RUNPATH. The default systemd unit did not set LD_LIBRARY_PATH, so this was not promoted to a proven default local-user-to-root path, but the installed root-capable runner must bind its core library to the Debian bundle it is executing rather than ambient loader state.FIX R-S11c-10s makes the runner load $ORIGIN/lib/librustdesk.so, matching the installed bundle layout and the existing $ORIGIN/lib RPATH. It uses immediate local binding, exits nonzero when the bundled core or rustdesk_core_main is missing, and deletes the legacy load-failure helper that probed PATH for package managers. verify.sh gates the executable-relative core path, fail-closed startup, retained bundle RPATH, absence of the bare dlopen("librustdesk.so") shape, and absence of the package-manager/PATH advice path.
104Android final APK manifest authority — the source manifest hardening was insufficient because Android's manifest merger can add library declarations to the single manifest packaged in the APK. The existing signed artifact demonstrated the gap: file_picker merged READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, AndroidX ProfileInstaller merged an exported ProfileInstallReceiver protected by android.permission.DUMP, and two RustDesk-internal no-filter components relied on implicit default non-export rather than explicit policy. This was not a proven ordinary-app-to-root path, but it contradicted the fork's Android authority model: only the launcher/deep-link activity should be externally launchable, forbidden permissions must not reappear through library merge, and shipped package authority is the final APK manifest, not source XML alone.FIX R-X6/R-S14 now makes the Android manifest policy artifact-backed. The app manifest declares tools:node="remove" for merged READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW, and androidx.profileinstaller.ProfileInstallReceiver; PermissionRequestTransparentActivity and MainService are explicitly android:exported="false". scripts/build-android.sh runs scripts/verify-android-apk-manifest.py after apksigner verify and before hashing the signed APK. That verifier parses the final APK with the pinned SDK aapt2 dump xmltree, requires package/minSdk/targetSdk and allowBackup=false, rejects forbidden or unknown final permissions, requires the exact component inventory, allows only MainActivity exported with launcher and rustdesk deep-link filters, requires every service/receiver/provider/internal activity to be explicitly non-exported, and fails if ProfileInstaller or any unexpected component returns. verify.sh gates the source removal markers, explicit internal export flags, helper syntax, build-script invocation, and component/permission policy strings.
105Android legacy JAR-signature META-INF authority — the Android release APK still declared minSdk 22, so Android 5.x/6.x installs relied on v1/JAR signing while the APK necessarily carried runtime Java resources under META-INF/, including R8-shrunk kotlinx.coroutines service-loader entries. apksigner verify --verbose warned that those entries were not protected by the JAR signature; local mutation confirmed an API 22/23 verification range still accepted a modified META-INF/services entry, while API 24+ rejected the same APK through v2/v3 whole-APK verification. Removing the service-loader files would change coroutine runtime behavior, so the wrong compatibility promise was the API 22/23 support floor.FIX R-X6/R-S14 retires the v1/JAR authority path. Android support now starts at minSdk 24; the release signer passes --min-sdk-version "$ANDROID_MIN_SDK", explicitly disables --v1-signing-enabled, explicitly enables v2 and v3 signing, and verifies with apksigner verify -Werr --min-sdk-version "$ANDROID_MIN_SDK" --verbose. The build asserts v1 is false, v2 and v3 are true, and no not protected by signature warning is present before hashing or double-build comparison. scripts/verify-android-apk-manifest.py requires the final manifest minSdkVersion to be 24, and verify.sh gates the pin, Gradle value, manifest verifier, signing flags, warning-as-error verifier, and this disposition. Runtime META-INF resources remain only on platforms where v2/v3 protect the APK as a whole; Android 5.x/6.x compatibility is intentionally dropped.
106Linux pkcheck request-time peer identity binding — Linux service-owned password changes correctly used the race-resistant pkcheck --process pid,start-time,uid form, but the request handler still assembled that subject from a split observation: pid and uid came from the connected socket while start time came from a direct /proc read in the handler, after the accept-time executable proof had already run. This was not promoted to a proven default local-to-root path because the request still required the active user/root service UID gate, same-executable accept proof, and polkit auth_admin; it was still the wrong authority shape for a privileged mechanism authorizing an untrusted IPC subject.FIX R-S11e-15 makes the pkcheck subject come from the same live peer identity proof used by the IPC authority model. linux_polkit_subject_for_peer now calls peer_process_identity(stream, POSTFIX_SERVICE), which revalidates the SO_PEERCRED pid/uid, proves the current executable path, checks the live /proc uid still matches the socket uid, and carries the process start time used for pid,start-time,uid. The old direct linux_proc_start_time(peer_pid) subject assembly is gone. verify.sh gates the live-identity subject, the start-time accessor, requirements/ledger disposition, and absence of the split-observation regression.
107Windows CM non-file clipboard helper authority — in installed/root Windows server mode, the clipboard service connected to the authenticated _cm endpoint and sent a bare Data::ClipboardNonFile(None). Endpoint proof established that the caller was the launched RustDesk server/helper process, but it did not prove that any active Remote connection currently held clipboard-read authority. A helper peer with process-level CM proof could therefore ask CM to read the interactive user's text/image clipboard through check_clipboard_cm() without carrying the per-connection conn_id, AuthConnType, or cm_auth_token that protects CM file operations.FIX R-S11c-22 makes non-file clipboard reads a typed connection-bound CM capability. The Windows clipboard service now chooses a subscribed connection's CmClipboardAuthority lease and sends Data::AuthorizedClipboardNonFile { id, conn_type, cm_auth_token }; Data::ClipboardNonFile remains response-only and a bare None request is rejected. CM validates the tuple through the main server with ValidateCmConnection before calling check_clipboard_cm(), and the server registry records a live cm_clipboard bit that is Remote-only, requires the same can_sub_clipboard_service() capability used for subscription, and is refreshed when peer clipboard/keyboard disables change. FileTransfer/ViewCamera/Terminal/PortForward, stale-token, wrong-type, no-subscriber, disabled, and endpoint-proof-only requests do not read the desktop clipboard. verify.sh gates the authorized request variant, Remote-only clipboard authority, subscriber lease, live registry bit, validation-before-read ordering, bare-request rejection, and absence of ClipboardNonFile(None) sends.
108Linux Debian package payload ownership authority — the direct Debian packaging paths in build.py staged the root service executable, service unit, polkit policy, and maintainer scripts under tmpdeb, then built the archive with plain dpkg-deb -b. Plain package creation preserves staging-tree ownership, so a direct non-root build could produce a .deb whose /usr/share/rustdesk/rustdesk payload and parent tree installed as the builder's numeric uid/gid. The checked release artifacts were root-owned and the official wrapper builds inside a root Docker container, so this was not promoted to a confirmed shipped artifact defect, but the source did not encode the required invariant for a package whose postinst links /usr/bin/rustdesk to that payload and starts it as User=root.FIX R-S11c-10t makes Debian payload ownership an archive invariant. Every build.py Debian package creation path now uses dpkg-deb --root-owner-group -b, so builder uid/gid never becomes installed package authority. scripts/verify-debian-package-authority.py source-gates the three package creation paths, inspects the data and control tar members directly, requires the root-loaded /usr/share/rustdesk authority tree, service unit, polkit policy, and maintainer scripts to be root:root, non-group/world-writable, and type-correct, rejects links in authority trees, and carries synthetic negative tests for user-owned payloads and writable parent directories. scripts/build-debian.sh runs the artifact verifier before hashing release packages, and verify.sh runs the source and negative gates.
109Windows Flutter runner Rust core DLL load provenance — the Windows Flutter runner loaded the Rust core with bare LoadLibraryA("librustdesk.dll") before Rust-side service, IPC, or policy checks ran. Windows normally searches the application directory first, so this was not promoted to a proven default standard-user-to-SYSTEM path for the installed Program Files service bundle, but a root-capable service executable must not delegate its core module selection to the ambient DLL search order if the bundled DLL is missing, moved, or the process search path is changed.FIX R-S11c-23 makes the Windows runner bind the Rust core to the executable bundle. wWinMain now resolves the current executable path with GetModuleFileNameW(nullptr, ...), constructs the sibling absolute librustdesk.dll path, and loads it with LoadLibraryExW using LOAD_LIBRARY_SEARCH_DLL_LOAD_DIR, LOAD_LIBRARY_SEARCH_APPLICATION_DIR, and LOAD_LIBRARY_SEARCH_SYSTEM32. Startup fails closed if the executable directory cannot be resolved or the bundled core cannot be loaded. verify.sh gates the executable-relative path, restricted load flags, absence of the bare DLL load, and this disposition.
110Linux XDO libxdo dynamic-library provenance — the patched libxdo-sys wrapper kept RustDesk runnable on systems without libxdo, but its Linux input backend tried bare names libxdo.so.4, libxdo.so.3, and libxdo.so through libloading::Library::new. In active-desktop service mode that backend normally runs as the active user, but in root/headless service-owned server mode it is reachable from the root --server input path. The default systemd unit does not set attacker-controlled loader variables and default system library directories should not be user-writable, so this was not promoted to a proven default local-user-to-root exploit; it was still the wrong authority shape for service-reachable native code loading.FIX R-S11c-10u makes the XDO backend load only fixed absolute system-library candidates for versioned libxdo.so.4 or libxdo.so.3. Before calling Library::open, the resolver requires the candidate path, candidate directory chain, canonical target path, canonical target directory chain, and target file to be absolute, root-owned, and not group/world-writable; the target must be a regular file. Protected distro symlinks are accepted only when their canonical target is also protected, while /tmp, /usr/local, relative paths, unversioned libxdo.so, writable parents, writable files, shell helpers, ldconfig, pkg-config, and ambient name search are absent. The library is opened with immediate local binding, and missing or untrusted libxdo leaves XDO functions disabled rather than falling back. verify.sh runs the focused libxdo-sys trust tests and source-gates the fixed candidates, ownership/mode predicates, protected symlink handling, absence of the old bare-name loader, and this disposition.
111Windows service-owned server child executable provenance — after R-S11d-13 bound CreateProcessAsUserW to an explicit lpApplicationName, the installed service still chose the service-owned child image with std::env::current_exe(). A service-owned --server --service-owned-server child must not inherit executable authority from whatever image is currently running as the service; it must be the fixed Program Files service executable installed by the hardened installer/MSI model.FIX R-S11d-37 makes the service-owned child image a fixed-root runtime invariant. src/platform/windows.rs derives the executable from fixed_service_install_path(""), opens the fixed/running service directories and executable files no-follow, rejects reparse/symlink-backed objects, proves by Win32 handle identity that the running service directory equals the fixed service root and that the running service image equals the fixed service executable, and then launches only that fixed executable with --server --service-owned-server. core_main.rs refuses a Windows service-owned --server marker unless the process is LocalSystem and the same fixed-root proof succeeds. The service-owned main-IPC authenticator now proves the named-pipe server executable against the fixed service executable before the LocalSystem and exact-argv checks; it no longer authenticates this password-bearing receiver against the caller's current executable. verify.sh gates the fixed-root helper, no-follow handle-identity checks, service-owned entry guard, fixed-exe main receiver proof, exact argv proof, explicit child current directory, and absence of the old current-exe service-owned receiver helper.
112Desktop Dart FFI Rust core library provenance — after the native Linux and Windows Flutter runners were hardened to preload the Rust core from executable-relative bundle paths, flutter/lib/models/native_model.dart still reopened the FFI binding with bare DynamicLibrary.open("librustdesk.so") on Linux and bare DynamicLibrary.open("librustdesk.dll") on Windows. The Dart FFI layer is normally reached after the runner's safe preload, and this was not promoted to a proven default local-to-root path, but a privileged-capable desktop process must not leave its second Rust-core handle selection to platform library search semantics.FIX R-S11c-24 makes desktop Dart FFI follow the same bundle authority as the native runners. Linux resolves Platform.resolvedExecutable, takes its parent directory, and opens lib/librustdesk.so; Windows opens sibling librustdesk.dll. Android keeps the packaged librustdesk.so loader name, and macOS keeps DynamicLibrary.process() to avoid duplicate Rust global state. verify.sh gates the executable-dir helper, resolved-executable use, Linux/Windows executable-relative paths, Android/macOS exceptions, init helper use, and absence of bare desktop librustdesk opens.
113Windows inactive RustDesk IDD loader excision — after the active Windows virtual-display implementation was pinned to Amyuni and --install-idd was made reject-only, the inactive RustDesk IDD implementation still remained in source, workspace metadata, the lockfile, Windows build scripts, and Flutter peer/UI compatibility code. Its wrapper dynamically loaded dylib_virtual_display by ambient library name and the Windows build copied that DLL into the app bundle, so a privileged-capable package retained a native plugin authority path for an unsupported implementation.FIX R-S11d-38 deletes the inactive RustDesk IDD implementation instead of hardening it. The libs/virtual_display and libs/virtual_display/dylib crates are removed from the workspace and lockfile, the Windows build no longer builds or copies dylib_virtual_display.dll, virtual_display_manager owns the small MonitorMode shape and dispatches only to Amyuni, and the client/UI recognizes only amyuni_idd for virtual-display controls. verify.sh gates absence of the deleted crate directory, dependency/workspace/lockfile/build artifacts, legacy RustDesk IDD platform-addition/UI names, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
114Obsolete generated Docker build helper — build.py::generate_build_script_for_docker() wrote, chmodded, and executed fixed /tmp/build.sh, and cloned Flutter Rust Bridge into fixed /tmp/flutter_rust_bridge. An elevated or shared-host invocation would have followed or acted on attacker-controlled public temporary names. The function was not reachable from the parser, had no caller or supported workflow, and was therefore not promoted to a shipped-runtime local privilege escalation. It also downloaded an obsolete Flutter archive and shallow-cloned mutable Flutter Rust Bridge and vcpkg repositories outside the pinned offline build inputs.FIX R-S11c-10v deletes the generated Docker build helper rather than preserving an unreachable, non-reproducible bootstrap mode behind safer temporary-file mechanics. build.py no longer contains the helper, fixed public temporary paths, obsolete Flutter archive, mutable Flutter Rust Bridge clone, or mutable vcpkg clone. Supported builds continue through the repository's pinned shell-script facilities. verify.sh gates the deleted helper and bootstrap inputs plus this requirements/ledger disposition.
115Verifier private scratch workspace authority — the supported host-side scripts/verify.sh, also run as the first scripts/verify-release.sh source gate, redirected checker output through 65 predictable /tmp/rd_verify_*.$$, /tmp/r_s11b3_*.$$, and /tmp/r_s11c23_hits.txt names. Shell redirection follows a preexisting symlink before the checked command executes, so an elevated verifier invocation on a shared host could truncate or write an attacker-selected file. No repository caller elevates the verifier, so this was not promoted to a default build-host or shipped-runtime local privilege escalation.FIX R-S11c-10w gives the verifier one private scratch authority. It creates /tmp/rustdesk-verify.XXXXXXXXXX with checked mktemp -d under umask 077, marks the resulting path variable readonly, verifies current-UID ownership and mode 0700, and redirects every generated checker output beneath that directory. A single EXIT handler preserves the verifier result, treats cleanup failure as failure, and works with nonzero HUP/INT/TERM exits; the old fixed/PID-suffixed names and scattered per-file cleanup branches are absent. A separate structural verifier proves the exact full-line create, cleanup, signal, and metadata blocks without placing its assertion literals in the input being checked; its mutation-negative self-test removes every required real line in memory and requires rejection. It also rejects fixed-string self-inspection, old scratch prefixes, and unquoted, single-quoted, or double-quoted direct public-/tmp redirections. The Apple checker is tracked separately.
116Apple checker private host scratch authority — the supported host-side scripts/apple-conform-check.sh, also invoked by scripts/verify-release.sh, redirected seven fixed/PID-suffixed diagnostics and its per-target cross-check log through public /tmp. An elevated invocation on a shared host could follow attacker-created symlinks during shell redirection. No tracked caller elevates either checker, so this was not promoted to a default build-host or shipped-runtime local privilege escalation. Separate fixed /tmp/apple-vcpkg and SDK-root /tmp values execute only inside fresh trusted-image docker run --rm containers and are not host filesystem authority.FIX R-S11c-10x gives the Apple checker one host-private workspace. It creates /tmp/rustdesk-apple-check.XXXXXXXXXX with checked mktemp -d under umask 077, marks the path variable readonly, verifies by lstat that it is a current-UID mode-0700 directory, and redirects every host diagnostic and target log beneath it. A single EXIT handler preserves prior status, treats cleanup failure as failure, and runs after nonzero HUP/INT/TERM exits. The old public host paths and scattered per-file cleanup branches are absent, the unnecessary container-local rustfmt diagnostic file is replaced by in-memory stderr capture, and required container-private fixture paths remain in their container namespace. Anchored source-shape assertions prevent the gate from satisfying itself and reject old host prefixes or any direct public-temp redirection.
117Windows obsolete updater temporary-file authority — normal Windows GUI startup still called try_remove_temp_update_files() after the supported self-updater had been excised. The function enumerated the process temporary directory and deleted every file older than one hour whose name matched rustdesk-*.msi or rustdesk-*.exe, without proving a producer, owner, installation transaction, or file identity. A privileged-capable process therefore retained ambient filesystem deletion authority over installer-shaped names it did not own. No supported updater producer or consumer remained, and remove_file did not establish a default symlink-to-target arbitrary deletion path, so this was not promoted to a confirmed default local-to-SYSTEM exploit.FIX R-S11d-39 deletes the obsolete authority rather than inventing ownership for a removed feature. Normal startup no longer enumerates or removes temporary installer-shaped files. The uncalled cross-platform download-file-<version> FFI asset-name query, its updater-only Windows MSI registry probe, the dead Flutter updateUrl state, and the four exclusively owned translation keys are deleted with the sweep. Live peer-compatibility wording such as Download new version remains because it is not updater authority. verify.sh gates absence of the sweep, filename query, MSI probe, Dart state, and dead language entries plus this requirements/ledger disposition; dart-verify.sh independently gates the Flutter state and strings.

Honest exceptions are explicit, not accidental. The at-rest PRS is accepted, not fixed, by the §2 threat model (an authenticated peer holds the password and is trusted with the host; endpoint compromise is out of scope): it is a memory-hard salted hash (Argon2id), connect-equivalent but not the plaintext password (#14), so the residual is the connect-equivalent cold-read on the endpoint (§2), hardened and disclosed, not eliminated. (#10's symlink TOCTOU is fixed; its full-filesystem reach is intended single-mode capability, not a defect, and deliberately carries no confinement toggle per R-S12. #99 likewise accepts Linux root/headless FileTransfer as the authenticated owner's R-S8 file authority, while gating that it remains file-only, CM-token-bound, and not a generic IPC or cross-capability grant.) The dependency-advisory class is now gated by R-R3/R-A7 rather than left as a hand-audit note: fixed lockfile bumps, reasoned accepts, pinned Rust/Dart advisory snapshots, and a separate vcpkg native-codec watch. The local IPC service-boundary class is structurally gated: the generic config/proxy IPC write shapes are absent, service-owned process-control actions are receiver-authorized, main_channel_admits_state_mutation is an exhaustive policy table with no wildcard fallback, and R-A6 pins that no Config::set* is reachable from IPC handle() except through named receiver-authorized operations. Everything is excised (compiled out, CI-grepped absent), logic-fixed/asserted/tested, explicitly accepted, or covered by a pinned advisory gate above.

#Finding / exposureDisposition in this fork
118Windows terminal service principal authority — an authenticated remote Terminal login selected its shell principal from uninstall-registry-derived is_installed() state. The fixed-root service-owned --server --service-owned-server child can be positively running as LocalSystem while a stale legacy uninstall key makes that compatibility query false. That selected SelfUser/None; terminal_service then used token presence as its only role discriminator and opened the PTY directly in the current LocalSystem process. Persistent services recorded only is_specified_user: bool, accepted simultaneous same-class attachments, and reacquired entries by bare service ID, so another Windows session/logon principal could pass the boolean check and stale cleanup could remove a replacement entry. Service creation errors were ignored, and the attempted token repair used DuplicateToken, which creates an impersonation token rather than the primary token required by CreateProcessAsUser. The network trigger required the permanent password and an authenticated Terminal session, but this still violated the declared Windows serving-user contract by turning remote Terminal into a LocalSystem shell under supported compatibility metadata state. The same audit found lifecycle faults around that authority: synchronous PTY/helper construction blocked the Tokio connection task; revocation did not cancel an in-flight open; a new session became output-visible before its TerminalOpened response; delayed close responses used terminal-ID absence and could pass after a second open/close generation; output polling drained a concurrently refillable channel without a work or memory ceiling while holding the session mutex; revocation and connection destruction created teardown threads on demand and could lose ownership or block when spawning failed; detached dead helpers remained reconnectable; helper pipe rights used generic write and therefore included pipe-instance authority; one-shot CancelSynchronousIo could miss a subsequent synchronous read and block join; and helper/shell failures were converted into process exit code zero.FIX R-S11c-25 derives terminal authority from the positively proved process role, never installation metadata. A service-owned LocalSystem server derives the served session from its own process token, then must obtain that session user's primary token with WTSQueryUserToken or reject Terminal before authorization succeeds. A direct non-LocalSystem server uses only its process owner; every LocalSystem/service-owned no-token direct PTY is rejected again at the service sink, and the terminal helper refuses LocalSystem execution. The reconnect principal is token session ID + user SID + TOKEN_STATISTICS.AuthenticationId. The persistent entry separately owns the original primary token through a non-Copy RAII handle, never replaces it on reconnect, and revalidates TokenId/ModifiedId before launch. Canonical ts_<UUID> service IDs are bound atomically to an exclusive opaque lease carrying exact entry identity, attachment generation, and authority epoch; every action, output read, persistence mutation, and cleanup uses that lease, simultaneous attachment is rejected, and exact Arc identity prevents registry, session, output, and close-response ABA. Preparation and a current-authority checkpoint precede the checked login-success write; activation immediately revalidates under the service lock before committing persistence and the exact subscriber. A bounded ordered worker performs terminal construction outside the Tokio task and service lock. Every opening owns a shared cancellation capability checked across helper-pipe waits and before exact generation/epoch commit. A committed session remains output-invisible until its exact TerminalOpened response is enqueued under the service lock; natural close response and exact-session removal share that same lock, so the ID cannot be reused between them. Each service and each output poll have explicit session, chunk, and byte ceilings; sorted IDs, a rotating start cursor, and per-session quanta prevent cross-session starvation, while split replay/output tails remain owned for later polls. Orderly termination becomes pending state and emits TerminalClosed only after replay, deferred tails, and the disconnected reader channel are exhausted. Direct PTY launch drops the parent's slave descriptor immediately after spawn and retains only the master, allowing child exit to end the blocking reader and complete that barrier. For an attached session's close response, reader-only completion starts a bounded direct-child status grace and re-queries the retained child on each poll; a real exit status wins, grace expiry is reported as -1, and writer failure remains immediate. Transport-worker completion is nevertheless immediate for reconnect admission and detached cleanup, so a dead persistent session is never republished during that grace. Revocation advances the epoch, marks workers fatal, cancels openings, takes the token, drains exact session owners, removes only the exact registry entry, and admits teardown to a bounded queue served by fixed workers created before terminal admission. Every lease and opening reserves a teardown permit before resource creation and retains it through rollback or complete joins, so admission and pending cleanup cannot grow without bound. Queue insertion shares no lock with cleanup work; session shutdown is signaled outside the session mutex, and Windows holds a shared TerminateJobObject capability that stops the helper tree immediately before deferred cleanup; unexpected termination failure aborts the service so process teardown closes the sole service-owned job handle and activates kill-on-close rather than continuing with a revoked shell. Detached monitoring removes and completes teardown of reader-dead, writer-dead, child-dead, and helper-dead sessions. The helper boundary is restricted to SYSTEM and the exact enabled logon SID: the service holds a duplex server handle needed for SetNamedPipeHandleState, while the user side receives only directional FILE_READ_DATA or FILE_WRITE_DATA plus SYNCHRONIZE, never generic-write/append/create-instance rights. Pipe connect is timeout- and authority-cancellable, switches from PIPE_NOWAIT to synchronous PIPE_WAIT, and shutdown repeatedly applies CancelSynchronousIo to retained worker thread handles until each is observably finished; every framed read checks the shutdown latch before issuing another operation, and unexpected cancellation failure terminates the dedicated helper while both worker handles remain owned. The helper is launched suspended into a kill-on-close job with an isolated token environment and validated profile directory. Its retained process handle supplies the actual Windows exit status; helper output EOF waits through a bounded grace longer than the I/O cancellation deadline for that status, the dedicated helper entrypoint exits with the shell status or a defined nonzero infrastructure-failure status, and query/transport failures are never reported as clean zero. Periodic served-session validation revokes the token and surviving shells on logoff or principal replacement. The invalid DuplicateToken conversion and fallback are deleted because WTSQueryUserToken already returns a primary token; Microsoft separately states that DuplicateToken creates an impersonation token unusable by CreateProcessAsUser, and documents AuthenticationId as the represented logon-session identifier while TokenId/ModifiedId identify the token object and its modification state. Linux's intentional authenticated-owner root terminal (R-X8/R-F1) is unchanged.
119Linux Debian shipped ELF runtime-library provenance — after the runner was bound to $ORIGIN/lib/librustdesk.so and Debian payload ownership was normalized, the shipped Rust core still carried Cargo's inherited release RPATH output: DT_RUNPATH [$ORIGIN/../../../../tmp/tc/rustinstall/lib/rustlib/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/lib]. From the installed location /usr/share/rustdesk/lib/librustdesk.so, that resolves under public /tmp/tc. A normal user can create that tree, and glibc uses a shared object's DT_RUNPATH for its direct DT_NEEDED dependencies before default system directories; local runtime proof showed the root-capable core searching the /tmp/tc path for libpulse-simple.so.0. The same package sweep found copied Flutter plugin shared libraries with absolute /src/flutter/linux/flutter/ephemeral build RUNPATHs. The plugin paths were not promoted to the same default local-user-to-root primitive because /src is not normally user-writable, but shipped root-capable ELFs must not contain build-directory runtime search paths at all.FIX R-S11c-10y makes Debian shipped ELF search paths an artifact invariant. Cargo release rpath is pinned off in Cargo.toml, build.py, and scripts/build-debian.sh, so the Rust core no longer receives a Rust sysroot RUNPATH from the container's /tmp toolchain. Flutter plugin targets are built with install RUNPATH and $ORIGIN, so their copied artifacts search only their installed bundle directory for same-directory dependencies. scripts/verify-debian-package-authority.py now inspects every ELF under /usr/share/rustdesk in each emitted .deb: legacy RPATH is rejected, the runner must have exactly $ORIGIN/lib, libflutter_linux_gtk.so and plugin libraries may have exactly $ORIGIN, and every other bundled ELF, including librustdesk.so, must have no runtime library search path. The verifier carries synthetic good and bad ELF package tests, including a public-/tmp RUNPATH negative case, and scripts/build-debian.sh runs this artifact gate before hashing packages.
120Protected service IPC resource boundary — the service-owned _service channel had the right typed authority shape but still used the generic uncapped IPC frame codec and, on Linux/macOS, a persistent spawned read loop. BytesCodec::new() defaults to usize::MAX, so an admitted local service peer could declare and deliver a very large frame before UTF-8/JSON deserialization and before service_channel_admits_message rejected it. A peer that sent no complete frame could hold a Unix service task indefinitely; repeated admitted messages on one persistent Unix connection could rerun expensive receiver checks such as polkit or macOS service proofs. Windows read only one request with a timeout, but the LocalSystem service loop performed that read inline and still used the uncapped codec. This was not promoted to a credential-write bypass or local-to-root code execution path because per-action receiver authorization remained intact; it was a protected-service availability and resource-exhaustion flaw across Linux, macOS, and Windows.FIX R-S11c-26 makes protected service IPC a bounded one-request RPC surface. Service clients and service receivers use ConnectionTmpl::new_protected_service, which sets BytesCodec::max_packet_length to 32 KiB before any service frame can be read. Linux/macOS _service accepts an already authorized connection only under a four-slot service-transaction semaphore, reads exactly one frame with SERVICE_IPC_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS, dispatches only service_channel_admits_message traffic, sends that operation's response, and closes; malformed, timed-out, unauthorized, or oversized frames fail closed. The macOS password setter no longer depends on a persistent connection: readiness is a separate no-secret RPC, Authorization Services runs locally, and the password-bearing request opens a fresh bounded service connection. Windows _service now uses the same capped constructor and a four-slot one-shot worker; LocalSystem close sets a service-owned stop flag after the existing pipe-client token proof, while password and RDP requests retain their elevated-client and service-owned commit gates. Linux, macOS, and Windows share a 4096-byte service-owned password value cap checked before polkit, Authorization Services verification, named-pipe elevation checks, hashing, or service-to-main password forwarding. verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh gate the capped constructor, service connect/accept wiring, one-shot timeout shape, transaction budgets, common password cap, codec over-cap rejection test, service-frame envelope tests, and this disposition.
121Permanent-password provisioning through visible process arguments — the sole CPace owner credential was accepted as rustdesk --password <value>, copied from the OS argument vector, and documented in the Debian deployment guide. On Linux, /proc/<pid>/cmdline exposes the complete live command line under the default procfs visibility policy; the invoking sudo command can retain the value while administrator authentication is pending, and the RustDesk process retained it while polkit or macOS Authorization Services ran. Shell history and same-login process inspection extend the class to every desktop platform. This is CWE-214: an ordinary local observer can learn the actual permanent password, then authenticate through CPace as the owner; on a root/headless Linux controlled side that intentionally grants the documented root-capable Remote, Terminal, and FileTransfer authority.FIX R-S11e-16 removes password-valued process arguments from provisioning. Bare rustdesk --password uses rpassword::prompt_password to read and confirm the value from the controlling terminal with echo disabled. Noninteractive headless automation uses the distinct no-value --password-stdin form, which refuses an interactive stdin, reads one bounded UTF-8 line, and shares the 4096-byte unattended-password ceiling; an explicit empty line remains the deliberate credential-removal operation. Both commands require an exact one-argument command shape. --password <value>, --password-stdin <value>, environment-variable input, and any positional compatibility fallback are rejected before the typed owner-aware password setter is called. The existing user-owned/service-owned routing, receiver authentication, OS-admin authorization, and typed commit paths are unchanged. The deployment guide uses only the hidden prompt; the smoke harness uses stdin without putting the secret in RustDesk or timeout argv; unit and source gates pin exact command parsing, confirmation, TTY refusal, bounded UTF-8 line input, positional-secret rejection, and absence of password-valued provisioning examples.

#118 lifecycle and helper closure. The lease also carries an authority epoch and stages reconnect persistence until a current-authority checkpoint and successful login-response write. With no option processing between that checked write and activation, the post-write transition validates the checkpoint under the service lock, commits staged state, installs the exact subscriber, and makes output eligible; concurrent revocation fails closed. Existing persistent state survives failed preauthorization, while new reservations roll back. A bounded ordered action worker accepts only nonblocking queue sends, so PTY/helper construction never occupies the Tokio connection task. Every epoch-bound opening owns a shared cancellation capability; revocation signals it before clearing reservations, helper connection polling observes it, and any process already created is under direct-child or kill-on-close-job ownership before later fallible work. Final commit still requires the exact generation, epoch, reservation identity, and uncancelled capability. Input and resize linearize against the exact session Arc and use nonblocking sends. A service-entry watchdog runs after Windows logoff events and periodically even while detached. Revocation advances the epoch, latches worker failure, cancels openings, takes the token, and drains exact session Arcs under the service lock, then conditionally removes only that registry Arc and enqueues tracked teardown without waiting for a session mutex or join. A new logon can therefore reuse the opaque ID without the revoked Arc affecting its replacement. Detached monitoring removes naturally exited direct/helper sessions by exact Arc identity and releases their process, pipe, and job ownership; dead sessions are not reconnectable metadata. Attached connections close on fatal-latch detection, and stale output is sent only while its exact session Arc or verified absence condition remains authoritative. Helper pipes use no overlapped handle: a timeout-and-authority-cancellable joined worker polls a local-only first-instance PIPE_NOWAIT server, verifies the retained helper process PID and token principal, then restores PIPE_WAIT for synchronous I/O. The server handle is duplex so it has the mode-change access required by SetNamedPipeHandleState; the enabled logon SID receives only FILE_WRITE_DATA|SYNCHRONIZE or FILE_READ_DATA|SYNCHRONIZE according to direction, never generic-write/append/pipe-instance authority. Helper shutdown targets both retained I/O thread handles with CancelSynchronousIo before joining them. Token and security buffers are pointer-aligned. CreateEnvironmentBlock(FALSE) and GetUserProfileDirectoryW must both succeed for the same token; no LocalSystem environment or working-directory fallback exists. The helper is created suspended, assigned to a JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE job, and resumed only after assignment, so dropping the single RAII owner terminates the helper, ConPTY shell, and descendants. The offline committed-source Windows build runs native terminal runtime tests before artifact compilation, including real blocking round trips for both pipe directions, external opening cancellation, and pending synchronous-read cancellation.

Appendix D · Dependency advisories — gated disposition ledger

The dependency-advisory posture is now implemented as a repeatable gate. The Rust side is scripts/audit.sh: a digest-pinned Rust 1.75 audit image installs the pinned cargo-audit and cargo-deny versions from scripts/pins.env, bakes the pinned RustSec ADVISORY_DB_COMMIT, parses only deny.toml [advisories].ignore objects as accepts, and fails on any unignored advisory. The Dart side is scripts/dart-audit.sh: pinned osv-scanner runs offline against flutter/pubspec.lock and the currently-empty reason-bearing accept-list. Native vcpkg C/C++ codec libraries are outside RustSec/Pub OSV and are tracked by docs/NATIVE-CODEC-WATCH.md plus scripts/native-codec-watch.sh.

AreaDispositionEnforcement
Rust lockfile fixesFixed in the committed graph where the pinned Rust 1.75 build allowed it: url 2.5.4 removes the idna 0.5.0 RUSTSEC-2024-0421 path; rand 0.8.6 removes the RUSTSEC-2026-0097 CSPRNG issue; crossbeam-channel 0.5.15 and tracing-subscriber 0.3.20 remove their reported advisories; the in-tree users dependency surface is the maintained uzers 0.12.2 package.Cargo.lock plus the Rust advisory gate. Comment-only RUSTSEC mentions in deny.toml are not ignore flags.
Rust reasoned acceptsThe remaining RustSec findings are accepted only in deny.toml, each as an { id, reason } object. The accepted set covers the Rust-1.75/MSRV-blocked OpenSSL advisories, platform-pruned or build/dev-only findings such as time and libgit2-sys, compile-time or radio-silence unmaintained crates, the load-bearing sodiumoxide crypto dependency, the retained Linux fuser CLIPRDR path with its service/mount bounds, the GTK/unic clusters, and other transitive unmaintained warnings whose reachable surface is documented in the reason.scripts/audit.sh turns only real TOML ignore entries into cargo-audit --ignore flags; cargo-deny check advisories reads the same deny.toml policy against the same pinned advisory snapshot.
Dart/PubThe resolved Flutter/Pub graph is OSV-clean against the pinned Pub snapshot. The accept-list exists but has no active entries.scripts/dart-audit.sh requires scripts/dart-audit-ignores.txt, rejects malformed accepts or accepts without reasons, and runs osv-scanner --offline --lockfile=flutter/pubspec.lock.
Native vcpkg C/C++ librariesRustSec and Pub OSV do not cover libvpx, libyuv, opus, libjpeg-turbo, oboe, or cpu-features. Open runtime-reachable decoder advisories remain part of Appendix C #2b's accepted in-process viewer residual until a future decoder sandbox or pin bump closes them. aom is retired by Appendix C #88, and AV1/libaom remains runtime-quarantined by Appendix C #87 as wire-level policy only.scripts/native-codec-watch.sh verifies the exact vcpkg package set, overlay pins, current requirements hash, retired-aom record, and native-codec watch ledger. It is a watch/sync gate, not a decoder sandbox.
Release gate shapeThe Apple source-conformance, Rust-advisory, Dart-advisory, native-codec, runtime-smoke, Flutter-analysis, and build-harness fail-loud gates are intentionally slower than the day-to-day verifier, but release verification runs them. The fast verifier pins the release-gate shape so the slow gate cannot silently lose a checker, stop using pins, drop Apple-only source conformance, or return to grep-based accepts.scripts/verify-release.sh includes verify.sh, smoke-server.sh, dart-verify.sh, native-codec-watch.sh, apple-conform-check.sh, audit.sh, dart-audit.sh, and test-build-faillo.sh; scripts/verify.sh asserts both the Apple-gate structure and the full release-gate bundle.

This ledger does not claim dependency perfection. It claims repeatability and explicit ownership: a dependency advisory is either fixed in the locked graph, accepted in a machine-readable policy with a reason, outside the shipped role, or tracked in the native-codec residual ledger that Rust/Dart package tools cannot cover.

requirements.html — normative security requirements for a hardened RustDesk fork. Base pinned at upstream 1.4.7 (0c86d46) + hbb_common@df6badc; build pinned by the committed Cargo.lock (910 crates; 38 git-sourced, all rev-locked) and the toolchain of §3.1. Every architectural and surface claim was verified by direct read of that tree. The bar is SSH. Corrections, adversarial review of the §10 handshake, and an independent audit of the chosen PAKE implementation are actively welcomed.
